# Parity ledger

Every known place where the Rust program could, or deliberately does, differ from C++ XMALab 3 (commit `2967a7e`). Nothing that changes numbers goes into the code without an entry here.

Decisions:

- **Copy**: reproduce the C++ behaviour, including when it is a bug.
- **Fix**: do the correct thing. Used only where the C++ behaviour cannot have produced valid results, or does not affect results.
- **Switch**: copy by default, with a named option for the corrected behaviour.
- **Fix, compat**: do the correct thing by default, and keep the C++ behaviour in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`xma_model::CppCompat::XMALAB3`; `--xmalab3-compat` on `xma recompute` and `xma export`). `xma parity` always runs in that mode, since the reference dumps are the C++ program's, and its report says so. The mode is not stored in project files. Used for items the author (Peter Falkingham, who wrote RustXMALab) decided to fix (14 November 2026).
- **Open**: not yet decided.

Entries marked **Copy** are of two kinds only: method choices that are not defects (which frames are filtered, how rotations are filtered, the triangulation and refinement methods themselves) and the internals of libraries XMALab uses (OpenCV, levmar, FFmpeg). Known defects are fixed (the author, 14 November 2026: "don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). The author can ask for any Copy entry to be changed, and did so for two method choices on 14 November 2026: the filter cutoff (A7) and 8-bit processing (V1). A decision quoted as the author's is final until the author changes it.

"Confirmed" means the C++ source was read and the behaviour checked by hand. "Reported" means an automated survey of the C++ source reported it and nobody has checked it by hand.

C++ paths are relative to the `src` folder of the XMALab 3 source.

Test programs, reference data and development notes named in this ledger are kept outside the public repository.

## Algorithms

| # | Item | C++ location | Evidence | Decision | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | The marker size check never rejects anything. `updateMeanSize` computes `sizeRange` as the largest distance from `mean`, but `mean` still holds the sum of sizes, not the average. `isValid` then tests against `3 * sizeRange`. | `core/Marker.cpp:1015`, `:1708-1736` | Confirmed | Fix, compat; the size test kept as it is (the author, 16 November 2026) | Fixing it would change which tracked points are accepted. Unset sizes are also included in the range loop. Decided 14 November 2026: fix. Intended: the size range is measured from the mean of the detected sizes, over set sizes only, so the 3 x range check can reject a detection. The check has never run in XMALab 3, so its effect is to be measured on real data in phase 3 (tracking). **Built 14 November 2026** (`CppCompat::size_range_from_sum`, `xma_engine::markers::update_mean_size`). Measured on the reference data: mean sizes unchanged; size range on the pig reference project 568 to 679 px in XMALab 3 against 0.76 to 3.19 px corrected. **The corrected test still cannot reject anything:** detection writes a point's size before the validity check runs, so the size being tested is already part of the range and always lies within one range of the mean, well inside 3 x range. Whether the size test should do something (for example three standard deviations, or a range computed without the size under test) is a choice of method, decided below: the size test is as ineffective as in XMALab 3, and tracking results do not depend on this row. **Corrected 15 November 2026 (review of the default mode):** the corrected range counted sizes in the open interval (1, 50) while `isValid` accepts the closed [1, 50], so a size of exactly 1 or 50 was measured against a range it was not part of and rejected where 1.0001 was accepted. Default now counts the sizes in [1, 50] for both the mean and the range (XMALab 3 mode unchanged, (1, 50) and the sum). Tests `review_a1_boundary_sizes_treated_like_their_neighbours` (`crates/xma-engine/tests/review_size_range.rs`), `a1_size_range_from_the_mean_of_counted_sizes`, `a1_size_range_after_a_run` (the run's mean and range recomputed from its sizes by each mode's rule). On the 11 plain reference runs the A1 switch changes nothing, before and after (the size test still cannot reject, see above). Decided by the author, 16 November 2026: keep the size test as it is (in both modes it rejects nothing, as in XMALab); the A1 fix of the range's arithmetic stays. |
| A2 | In rigid-body pose optimisation each virtual ("dummy") point is added (number of cameras) × (number of calibrated cameras) times, while a real marker is added once per camera that sees it. | `processing/RigidBodyPoseOptimization.cpp:165-177`, `core/RigidBody.cpp:511-538` | Confirmed | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::virtual_points_per_camera_pair`. The refinement now adds each virtual point once per calibrated camera, after that camera's markers. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: on `rv_vpchain`, A2 fixed alone (every other item as the C++) changes the poses by up to 11.5, `rb_errors` by up to 60 px and `rb_errors_filtered` by up to 778 px (independent review, 14 November 2026); with every item fixed the virtual-point datasets change as A3 describes. Test `a2_one_virtual_observation_per_calibrated_camera`. |
| A3 | A virtual point whose source transform fails, or whose frame has no coordinates, is still added to the pose optimisation, with both its world and its reference position at the origin (unless that origin projects to a depth of exactly zero in the camera, in which case the observation is skipped). | `core/RigidBody.cpp:486-538` | Confirmed | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026: do not copy. The Rust program now leaves such a point out of the refinement for that frame, as `computePose` leaves it out (`RefineOptions::virtual_points_at_origin` false); a body with one unusable virtual point refines to the same pose, bit for bit, as the same body without it (test `a3_fixed_refines_as_if_the_unusable_point_were_absent`; on the synthetic scene the C++ behaviour moves the pose by 0.21 rad and 1.4 units). The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` always uses. Measured effect of the default on the reference data (with A4 where the lens model is on): only the datasets with virtual points change (`rv_vpchain*`, `rv_loadtime_vp_lens*`, `v08_virtual_optimized_markerrefs`): refined poses by up to 26 units, mean 2D errors by up to 205 px unfiltered and 1113 px filtered (for example `rv_vpchain` body 3 frame 84: 197 px in the C++, 0.044 px fixed; `v08` body 0 frame 38: 136 px, 120 px). A virtual point that refers to another body whose transform fails still never falls back to its own per-frame coordinates. |
| A4 | Lens distortion is applied twice in pose optimisation: targets are fully undistorted (grid and lens), and the residual applies k1, k2, p1, p2, k3 again. | `processing/RigidBodyPoseOptimization.cpp:155,170`, `processing/optimizationFuncs/projFuncWithDistortionPoseSeperateDimensions.h` | Confirmed | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026: do not copy. The Rust program now refines with the plain pinhole projection: the coefficients are taken as zero (`RefineOptions::lens_distortion` false), which is exactly what the C++ computes for a camera without a lens model, so the targets (undistorted points) and the model agree. Synthetic camera with non-zero distortion and exact undistorted observations: the fixed refinement reaches the true pose within 1.6e-15 rad and 3.7e-15 units, the C++ behaviour stops 3.9e-5 rad and 4.4e-4 units from it (test `a4_refinement_with_lens_distortion_against_the_true_pose`). The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` always uses. Measured effect of the default on the reference data: no change where model distortion is off (every `pig_*` dataset); with it on, refined poses move by up to 5.0e-2 (`rv_lens_sparse`), 7.2e-3 (`rv_lens_sparse_noadv`) and 1.1e-3 (`v10_calibration_files`), mean 2D errors by up to 4.0e-2 px. |
| A5 | Cube calibration draws from the C library's `rand()` without seeding. Results differ between runs, compilers and platforms. | `processing/CubeCalibration.cpp:292,300` | Confirmed | Fix | Use a seeded generator, recorded with the result. Parity is judged on the converged calibration, not the draw sequence. **Built 15 November 2026** (package "cube"): `xma_vision::crand::CRand` is glibc's `srand`/`rand` (`TYPE_3`, the default generator), checked against glibc 2.44's `rand()` (`tests/crand.rs`: no seed, seeds 0, 1, 2, 5, 12345, 2^31 - 1, 2^31, 2^32 - 1, first ten values and the 100,001st), so the sequence is XMALab 3's on Linux on every platform. The cube functions (`xma_vision::cube`) take the generator by `&mut`; the seed, and whether a session's calibrations continue one sequence as in XMALab (seed 1 at start, never reseeded), is the engine's choice, to be recorded with the result. With the recorded seeds every reference run's search is reproduced draw for draw (`tests/cube.rs`). The calibration's dependence on the seed is small on the pig data: seeds 1 to 5 give byte-identical final calibrations (`oracle/DATASETS.md`, "Calibration runs"). **Engine, 15 November 2026** (package "engcal"): XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::calibration_rand_continues`): one generator per session (`Session::rng`, `CRand::new(1)` when a project is opened or created, continued by every cube calibration; the replay's `Seed` sets it). Default: every cube calibration starts from `CRand::new(1)`, so its result depends only on its inputs. On the reference runs both give the same calibrations (test `crates/xma-engine/tests/calibration_fixes.rs` `a5_random_generator`). |
| A6 | Tracking uses an OpenCL path when a GPU offers one, so results can depend on the graphics driver. | `processing/MarkerTracking.cpp:200` | Reported | Fix | One CPU implementation; parity is judged against the C++ CPU path. |
| A7 | The Butterworth filter is applied forwards and backwards without correcting the cutoff for the second pass. | `processing/ButterworthLowPassFilter.cpp:256,355` | Confirmed | Fix, compat (the author, 14 November 2026) | A method choice, not a defect, changed by the author's decision: "Correct it, but note clearly in the readme with emphasis, why and that results will differ from xmalab slightly if using filtered data. Once we are up and running this might need me to canvas users." As the C++ has it, the filter as applied passes half the amplitude at the entered cutoff and 1/√2 about 10% lower (27.1 Hz for 30 Hz at 250 frames per second); the usual MATLAB `butter` with `filtfilt` behaves the same. Default: the single pass is designed at `(fs/π)·atan(tan(π·fc/fs)/k)` with `k = (√2 − 1)^(1/8)`, so that the entered cutoff is the frequency at which both passes together pass 1/√2 (`xma_vision::filter::design_cutoff`, `CutoffCorrection::BothPasses`). XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::filter_cutoff_single_pass`): as the C++. For the same entered cutoff every filtered value differs from XMALab's; to reproduce XMALab's filter in the default mode, enter `(fs/π)·atan(k·tan(π·fc/fs))`. Measured with only this item changed (250 frames per second; lengths in cm, rotation vectors in radians; largest difference at any frame, RMS in brackets; test `a7_a58_measured_effect` in `xma-cli/tests/review_fixes_effect.rs`, ignored by default): `pig_clean_cut25` (25 Hz, designed at 27.692915 Hz): filtered 3D points 1.043e-2 (1.614e-3), body translations 1.809e-2 (4.322e-3), rotation vectors 9.235e-4 (2.249e-4), filtered 2D errors 0.148 px (0.023), 3D errors 3.665e-3 (7.782e-4); `pig_clean_cut10_rb40` (10 Hz designed at 11.150290 Hz; body 2 at 40 Hz designed at 43.806882 Hz): points 4.296e-2 (6.959e-3), translations 3.358e-2 (6.235e-3), rotation vectors 1.361e-3 (2.990e-4), 2D errors 0.511 px (0.073), 3D errors 1.592e-2 (2.485e-3); `pig_sparse` (25 Hz): points 1.410e-2 (1.692e-3), translations 4.342e-2 (8.280e-3), rotation vectors 1.472e-3 (3.043e-4), 2D errors 0.879 px (0.112), 3D errors 5.136e-2 (5.058e-3). The cutoffs of these test projects are arbitrary. Tests: `xma-vision/tests/filter_cutoff.rs` (the as-applied amplitude at the entered cutoff is 1/√2 from the coefficients and from a filtered sinusoid; the corrected design equals the single-pass design at the inverse cutoff to 7.4e-16 relative; extreme inputs). Independent review (14 November 2026, no defect found): the design cutoff agrees with one found by root-finding scipy's Butterworth to 1.1e-12 relative, the coefficients with `scipy.signal.butter` to 3.6e-15, and the filtered output with `scipy.signal.filtfilt`; the effect figures above were reproduced by filtering the C++ exports with scipy; `design_cutoff` gave a finite, accepted value on 2 million random inputs per order and every special value; every filtering path takes the document's mode. Just below the Nyquist frequency the design cutoff is clamped to the entered one. `Document::set_compat` does not recompute stored filtered poses (only tests call it). See also A59. The decision may be revisited once the author has asked users. README, "Filtered data differs from XMALab". |
| A8 | Runs of 12 frames or fewer are left unfiltered: their unfiltered values are copied into the filtered output, and a body's frames are marked as filtered. The filter pads each end with 12 reflected frames (3 × order 4), so it cannot work on fewer. | `core/Marker.cpp:1515`, `core/RigidBody.cpp:1615` | Confirmed | Copy (the author, 14 November 2026: "Keep silently") | No message is given, as in XMALab. A filtered export can therefore contain unfiltered values. |
| A9 | Rigid-body rotations are filtered as Rodrigues vector components by default. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:1699` | Confirmed | Copy (the author, 14 November 2026: "keep") | The alternative already exists in both programs as the setting `Filter3DPoints` (off by default): filter the marker positions and refit the body. |
| A10 | Settings that affect results (triangulation method, pose optimisation on/off, filter 3D points, blob parameters, merge thresholds, the default marker threshold and others, 40 in all) are stored per machine, not in the project. | `core/Settings.cpp` | Confirmed | Fix | Done in phase 1: the Rust writer adds one entry to the archive, `RustXMALab_settings.ini`, holding them under their C++ names; the reader uses it when present and XMALab's defaults otherwise, and command-line options override both. XMALab 3 ignores the entry on load (its dump of a project carrying it is byte-identical in every table) and drops it on save, so a project saved by XMALab 3 loses the stored settings. `RecomputeWhenSaving` is not stored. |
| A11 | `Camera::undistortPoint` applies the grid transform first and the lens model second in both directions. For distorting, the true inverse would apply the lens model first. | `core/Camera.cpp:668-680` | Confirmed | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::grid_first_when_distorting` (`ModelCompat` in `xma-vision`). Distorting now applies the lens model first and then the grid transform, the inverse of undistorting. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: `rv_lens_sparse` has two cameras with both a grid and a lens model, and there `reprojection.csv` changes by up to 2.7e-2 px, `rb_errors` by 6.8e-3, `rb_errors_filtered` by 8.4e-3, `PrecisionInfo` by 2.9e-3 and the reprojection error exports by 1.2e-2 (independent review). Synthetic camera with a grid and a lens: ideal to raw and back returns within 9.4e-7 px fixed (the accuracy of the grid's own inverse), 5.5e-2 px in the XMALab 3 order (test `a11_distorting_inverts_undistorting`). Separately, the grid of `rv_lens_sparse` camera 0 alone fails to invert near the left edge (11.7 px round trip at (0, 409)); that is the grid model's inverse (A43), not this item. |
| A12 | OpenCV builds that include Intel IPP give different results from builds without it for `getRectSubPix` at sub-pixel centres (by one grey level), `matchTemplate`, `goodFeaturesToTrack` and `cornerSubPix`. C++ tracking results therefore depend on how OpenCV was built for each platform. | Primitive oracle cases with a `.noipp` copy | Reported, with oracle evidence | Open | Find out whether each shipped XMALab build has IPP. Until then the plain (non-IPP) behaviour is the reference. |
| A13 | On a featureless patch, template matching is decided by rounding noise: all scores are near 1 and `normalize` stretches the noise to the full range. | `processing/MarkerTracking.cpp:157-270`; oracle case `tracking-step-flat_tie` | Reported, with oracle evidence | Copy | Parity cannot be expected on such patches; they are excluded from tier C statistics. |
| A14 | Detection methods 1, 3 and 6 measure distance from `searchArea + 1`, not from the patch centre. | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:316-404` | Confirmed: reproduced exactly in the XMALab 3 mode (16,448 reference rows on real frames, all six methods) | Fix, compat | Default: distances measured from the seed itself, its position in patch coordinates `center - offset`, sub-pixel fraction included, as methods 0, 2 and 5 do (`CppCompat::search_centre_plus_one`; `xma_vision::imgproc::blob::SelectionCompat::search_centre`). (Built first, 14 November 2026, from patch index `searchArea`, the seed rounded to a pixel; corrected 15 November 2026 after review, which showed two features 0.4 px either side of the seed's pixel treated alike.) Tests `review_a14_blob_selection_measured_from_the_seed`, `a14_fractional_seed_chooses_the_nearer_feature` (`crates/xma-vision/tests/detection_fixed.rs`, methods 3 and 6), `a14_a73_effect_on_reference_calls`. Measured, the switch alone against XMALab 3 on the 16,448 defined calls of the step-1 dump's `detection_methods.csv`: method 1, 2 of 2,844 calls select another blob (up to 49.9 px; same before and after the correction); method 3, 318 of 2,228 calls change and 30 select another corner (up to 15.7 px), against 252 and 25 with the patch-centre version; methods 0, 2, 5, 6 unchanged. On the 11 plain reference runs (`tracking_fix_effects.rs`): only r11 changes, 15 points differ, 9 moved by up to 13.6 px, 6 tracked in one result only, before and after. |
| A15 | The threshold offset is an unsigned 8-bit value in the threaded detection path but an `int` in the settings preview path. | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp` | Confirmed against the reference dump | Fix, compat | Negative offsets wrap in the threaded path. Verify before porting. Decided 14 November 2026: fix when detection is ported. Intended: the threshold offset is a signed integer in every path, so negative offsets do not wrap. Confirmed when detection was ported: the C++ output for the reference marker with offset -3 is reproduced only with the offset wrapped to 253. Switch: `CppCompat::threshold_offset_wraps`. |
| A16 | The mask size for the background blur is an `int`, so a fractional marker size is truncated. | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:145` | Reported | Copy | |
| A17 | `findContours` runs on the threshold image after it has been blurred, so every non-zero pixel counts as foreground. | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:220` | Reported | Copy | |
| A18 | Detection method 3 reads past the end of the corners vector (its loop runs to 50) when fewer than 50 corners are found. | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:323-340` | Confirmed: 616 of 2,844 points of the reference project have fewer than 50 corners (the dump flags them and gives no output) | Fix | Undefined behaviour cannot be copied; the loop is bounded by the number of corners found, in both modes. |
| A19 | `cv::undistortPoints` is called with its default of 5 iterations, so removing lens distortion is approximate. | `core/Camera.cpp:695` | Reported | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::five_iterations` (`ModelCompat`). Removing lens distortion now runs OpenCV's iteration until the point, distorted again, lies within 1e-10 px of the observation (its `EPS` criterion with step damping, at most 1000 iterations, `TermCriteria::CONVERGED`); where that has not converged, or has converged to a point where the model does not keep its orientation from the centre, Newton's method on the lens model (analytic Jacobian) from the last iterate, then Newton's method with continuation from the image centre, which follows the principal branch; if neither reaches 1e-9 px the point has no undistorted position (`undistort_lens_converged` gives `None`, `CameraModel::undistort_point` gives NaN, `try_undistort_point` gives `None`), and triangulation and the rigid-body computation leave that camera's view out at that frame. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: strongly distorted synthetic camera (f = 800 px on 1024 px, k1 = -0.35, k2 = 0.12, k3 = -0.02): 5 iterations leave a point up to 1.09 px from the true undistorted point, the fixed rule 1.5e-10 px (test `a19_undistortion_converges`). The independent review found that OpenCV's damped iteration alone does not converge in 1000 iterations at 8 of 1161 lattice points of a camera with a rational term and strong tangential distortion (f = 800, k1 = -0.3, k2 = 0.05, p1 = 0.01, p2 = -0.01, k4 = 0.2), returning points up to 951 px from the root without notice; the Newton fallback now reaches the principal root at all 8 (within 4.5e-13 px of an independent inverse). Points beyond the fold of the lens model have no preimage on the principal branch and now get no result (instead of a wrong point or another branch's root): 275 of 1161 lattice points on that camera, 9 on the strong-barrel camera above, 423 on f = 600, k1 = -0.45 (tests in `review_fixes_undistort.rs`). Reference data (lens model on: `rv_lens_*`, `rv_loadtime_vp_lens*`, `v10`): undistorted 2D points move by up to 9.5e-11 px, 3D points by up to 2.6e-12; no point without a result. |
| A20 | Rigid-body error standard deviations divide by `count - 1`, giving NaN when only one value contributes. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:1269,1315` | Confirmed | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::error_sd_nan`. A rigid-body error standard deviation with one contributing value is now 0, as for markers. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: Reference data: no effect (no frame with a single value). Test `a20_one_value_sd_is_zero`. Before: The NaN appears in reported errors, not in poses. Marker and marker-distance standard deviations use 0 in that case instead. |
| A21 | A marker's `error3D` is only ever zeroed: never computed, never saved in the project file, and it has no getter. | `core/Marker.h:208` | Confirmed | Copy | Held as 0. Not presented as a result. |
| A22 | `MarkerDetection::m_size` is uninitialised; with detection method 4 (no detection) an undefined value reaches `Marker::setSize`. | `processing/MarkerDetection.h:75` | Reported | Fix | Undefined behaviour cannot be copied. **Built 14 November 2026, both modes:** method 4 writes size -1 ("no size measured"), after tracking and after placing a point (`xma_engine::tracking::DetectionJob::run`). `updateMeanSize` ignores -1 as it ignores the C++ values (all below 1 in the reference runs), so the mean size is unchanged; the size range in the XMALab 3 mode (A1, measured from the sum over every entry) differs from the C++ where the C++ wrote a garbage value, and the method-4 marker's sizes and size range are left out of the parity check of run `r11_methods_t3`. The validity check skips method 4, so nothing else depends on it. |
| A23 | Several double-precision computations pass through single precision (`cv::Point3f`, `cv::Point2f`): the inputs of the rigid-body fit, the refit in `Filter3DPoints` mode, the virtual points in the refinement, `projectToImage` (and so the rigid-body 2D errors), and the EPnP inputs. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:1085-1116,1778-1796,493-508,2418-2439` | Confirmed (survey, `docs/cpp-recompute.md`) | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::single_precision`. The rigid-body fit, the Filter3DPoints refit, the virtual points of the refinement, `projectToImage` (the 2D errors) and EPnP (`solve_pnp_epnp_f64`) now stay in double. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: synthetic exact data: rigid-fit translation error 4.0e-15 fixed against 5.3e-8; mean 2D error at the true pose 5.4e-14 px against 1.4e-5 px. Reference data (every pig, `v*` and `video_*` dataset): `rb_pose_kabsch` up to 4.8e-6, refined poses up to 4.7e-8 (9.2e-8 in `pig_original`), `rb_errors` up to 2.1e-5 px, precision report up to 7.7e-9. In `pig_sparse` (and its format 2 and markerrefs dumps) the unrefined pose of body 1 at frame 69 (0-based) moves by 39.5: no marker has a 3D point there and markers 0, 1, 2 and 4 are seen only in camera 0, so both modes take the EPnP fallback from exactly four points in one camera. Neither unrefined pose is right: the 2D error over the four views is 9.61 px for the fixed pose, 22.73 px for the C++ pose and 0.55 px for the refined pose. Both are poor four-point EPnP solutions (OpenCV's own four-point EPnP is inaccurate too); the difference between the modes is double against single precision feeding an ill-conditioned minimal problem, and the refined poses agree to 1.8e-9 (independent review). |
| A24 | `RigidBody::getFramesTracked` counts frames with `poseComputed >= 0`, which is every frame. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:397-405` | Confirmed; the pig precision report shows 145 for both bodies | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::frames_tracked_all`. The precision report's frames tracked now counts the frames whose pose was computed. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: Reference data: the count changes where frames have no pose: by up to 52 (`pig_original`), 47 (`pig_sparse_noadv`), 145 (`v02`, `v07`: no poses). Test `precision_info_fixed`. Before: Shown in the precision report. |
| A25 | `Marker::getReprojectionError` averages over all cameras, including those where the marker is not set. | `core/Marker.cpp:1661-1687` | Confirmed (survey) | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::reprojection_error_all_cameras`. A marker's reprojection error in the precision report now averages over the cameras in which the marker is set. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: Reference data: no difference found apart from what A24 changes in the same file. Test `precision_info_fixed`. Before:  |
| A26 | The EPnP fallback uses the last camera in which at least 4 of the body's markers are visible, not the one with the most. Its return value is ignored, so a failure leaves a zero pose marked as computed. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:1140-1155,1170` | Confirmed (port; the 25 EPnP frames of the sparse reference match bit for bit) | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::epnp_last_camera`. The EPnP fallback now uses the camera in which most of the body's markers are visible (at least 4; the first on a tie), and a failed EPnP (OpenCV's exception or a non-finite result) gives no pose. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: Reference data: no effect measured (the `pig_sparse` EPnP frames pick the same camera). Test `a26_epnp_camera_with_most_markers` (a degenerate EPnP gives a NaN pose marked as computed in the C++ mode, none fixed). Before: An earlier version of this entry also said the pose composition was wrong. It is not: `CalibrationImage::getTransformationMatrix` returns the inverse (camera to world), so the composition is correct. EPnP from a camera whose reference image is uncalibrated inverts an uninitialised matrix in the C++; the port gives no pose there. |

| A34 | Triangulation does not check that a camera is calibrated. Methods 2 to 4 have no degeneracy check; methods 0 and 1 keep the old 3D point when the homogeneous coordinate is 0. | `core/Marker.cpp:239-616` | Confirmed (survey) | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::triangulation_unchecked`. Triangulation now uses calibrated cameras only, and refuses a result that is absent (zero homogeneous coordinate), not finite, behind any camera used (third component of P (X, 1) not positive), or from rays whose largest angle is below 1e-4 radian (`MIN_RAY_ANGLE`); the point is then undefined (3D status UNDEFINED; the stored coordinates are not written). The threshold: over the 58327 marker points seen by two or more calibrated cameras in the reference datasets the smallest largest ray angle is 1.763 radian (101 degrees, `v05_datafile_lines` marker 1 frame 10; test `a34_smallest_ray_angle_in_the_reference_data`), four orders of magnitude above it, while parallel rays give about 1e-12. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: reference data: no effect (every camera calibrated, no degenerate result). Parallel rays (two calibrated cameras differing by a translation, the same pixel): XMALab 3 gives a point about 1e18 away behind the cameras (methods 0 and 1) or an arbitrary nearby point (methods 2 to 4); fixed, undefined (tests `a34_parallel_rays_give_an_undefined_point`, `a34_uncalibrated_cameras_are_not_triangulated`). |

The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. | A36 | `Trial::recomputeAndFilterRigidBodyTransformations` and the rigid-body export recompute bodies one at a time without clearing other bodies' pose flags, unlike the full trial update. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:1584-1592`, `core/Trial.cpp:939-955` | Confirmed (survey) | Fix | Only differs from the full update when a virtual point refers to another body. Ported, but not distinguishable in any reference dump: at export time the stored state equals a fresh recomputation, so removing the step changes nothing there. It needs an edited state to test (phase 3). Decided 14 November 2026: fix in phase 3 (needs editing). Intended: recomputing bodies one at a time clears other bodies' pose flags as the full trial update does, so the result does not depend on the order. Built (default mode): every recomputation of a body after an edit clears **every** body's pose flag first and computes the listed bodies, so a virtual point driven by a body outside the list uses that body's pose computed afresh at the frame, never its stored pose, whose rotation the continuity step may have replaced (`body_ops::recompute_bodies_fully`, `Document::recompute_bodies_at_frame`; independent review, finding 1). Tests `review_edits.rs` (`review_body_recompute_uses_the_drivers_fresh_pose`, `review_correspondences_of_a_driven_body_give_the_full_recomputation`), `edit_consistency.rs` `body_and_structure_edits_with_replaced_rotations`. |
| A37 | OpenCV hands SVD, QR and LU to LAPACK above fixed sizes (`max(rows, cols) >= 25`, `rows >= 30`, `n >= 100`). The Rust code always runs OpenCV's own routines. | OpenCV `modules/core/src/hal_internal.cpp:61-64,663-680` | Confirmed against the primitive oracle | Fix | Below those sizes results are bit-identical. Above them values agree to rounding, but singular vectors can differ in sign: the DLT null vector with 13 or more points (`processing/Calibration.cpp:176`, `CubeCalibration.cpp:832`) and the quadric fit in `MarkerDetection.cpp:539`. To be handled where those are ported (phases 3 and 4). Triangulation methods 0 to 3 cross the SVD threshold with 13 or more cameras: on ordinary data they then agree to about 1e-13 relative (method 1 to about 6e-10, because its reweighting amplifies the difference), and on degenerate input (identical cameras, a point at infinity, `P(2,3)` of 0, a NaN coordinate) the two give unrelated answers. At 30 or more rows the QR solve differs on rank-deficient or all-zero input in whether it reports success. The undistortion fit crosses the threshold if `LocalUndistortionNeighbours` is set to 25 or more (default 12): coefficients then differ by up to 2.7e-9, transformed points by up to 5.7e-10 px, and a handful of lookup-table values by up to 3.8e-6. Independent reviews measured all of this against the real OpenCV. **Polynomial point refinement (ported in phase 3, `MarkerDetection.cpp:539`):** its 15-column fit goes to LAPACK in XMALab 3. On the pig frames the port and the C++ function as XMALab 3 runs it agree to under 1e-14 relative wherever the fitting window is not flat. On an exactly flat window the fit is degenerate: in exact arithmetic the function returns no result, but as built XMALab 3 returns no result in 32 of 77 reference runs and a point and radius made of rounding noise in the other 45. The port returns no result on a flat window, in both modes (`refine_point_polynomial_fit`; the test is exact equality of the window's pixels, no tolerance). A window that is flat except for one pixel is still fitted and is ill-conditioned: in 30 synthetic runs the two solvers end at the same point with radii up to 25% apart. **Camera calibration (ported in phase 4, `xma_vision::calib3d`, `cv::calibrateCamera`):** the reference hands the `J^T J` and `J^T e` products of a view with 50 or more points to BLAS, and the normal-equation solve with 25 or more free parameters (four or more views with the lens model off, three or more with the rational model) to LAPACK; the port runs OpenCV's own code. Below those sizes every output is bit-identical (all 92 recorded calls of the calibration runs, 51 probe cases). Above them, measured against the OpenCV 4.13 of the XMALab 3 AppImage (`calibratecamera-c3d-*` cases): a single cube view of 60 points (BLAS only) differs by 1.6e-10 relative in the camera matrix and 8.2e-10 px in reprojection; five views with the lens model off by up to 9.4e-12 relative (camera matrix), 7.2e-9 (rotation) and 9.7e-11 px; with the rational model the coefficients are poorly determined (k1..k3 against k4..k6) and after the 30 iterations differ by up to 5.5 in absolute value, the camera matrix by up to 5.5e-7 relative, rotations by up to 6.7e-5 relative, and the reprojections by up to 1.8e-4 px. Planar objects: `findHomography` takes an eigenvector from `cv::eigen`, which this OpenCV build computes with Eigen (not ported; the port uses OpenCV's own Jacobi method), so planar calibrations agree to rounding: up to 3.3e-12 relative and 2.6e-11 px with the lens model off, 5.2e-5 relative (camera matrix) and 3.9e-4 px with the rational model (which also crosses the LAPACK size). The bounds are asserted in `crates/xma-vision/tests/calib3d_primitives.rs`. **Cube calibration (phase 4, `CubeCalibration.cpp:832`), measured 15 November 2026** on every calibration run (`c02_cube_s1` to `_s5`, `c03` to `c07`, the full 1,000,000-iteration `c05` search included; `crates/xma-vision/tests/cube.rs`): the six-point DLTs of the search (12 x 12) are bit-identical, so every improving hypothesis is reproduced in order; the refits from all matches (13 to 36 points) go to LAPACK in XMALab 3. Their null vector came back negated in most of those refits (3 to 6 rows of `ransac_refine_rounds` per run, every `k_from_projection` row); after aligning the sign the normalised projection differs by up to 2.28e-7 (2.8e-12 relative, `c04_refs_snap` camera 0, `p_13`), the camera matrix decomposed from it by up to 1.37e-8 (3.2e-11 relative, `k_02`), and the camera matrix handed to `calibrateCamera` by up to 5.85e-9. With `calibrateCamera` answered from the recorded calls, everything after (refinement rounds, poses, matches, the result) is identical to the bit. Whether `calibrateCamera` itself turns a 5.85e-9 difference in its start into a different result is for the calib3d package to measure. |

| A52 | Cases the C++ leaves undefined, and what the engine does: a camera with no calibration image at the trial's reference index is left out of triangulation and its projection is not touched; a grid marked computed whose fit fails is not used (a warning is recorded); marker arrays shorter than the trial are skipped at the missing frames; a marker that has no `<Marker>` element gets mean size -1 and size range 1 (the C++ leaves its range uninitialised). | `core/Camera.cpp:398`, `processing/UpdateTrialFrame.cpp:51-60`, `core/Marker.cpp:45-58` | Confirmed in the source (engine port) | Fix | Undefined behaviour cannot be copied. A body whose marker list and reference points differ in length is read out of bounds by the C++; the engine uses the common prefix. A virtual point whose body index is past the last body is read out of bounds by the C++; the engine treats it as attached to a body with no pose. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:297-300,491,1081-1099,1729` | Confirmed in the source (engine port) | Fix | Undefined behaviour cannot be copied. |
| A54 | While loading, a rigid body whose `<RigidBody>` element names no reference files has its references reset, which recomputes and filters its poses at that moment, before the cameras' undistortion is fitted and before any trial update. If every marker of the body has its own reference point the body is initialised, and frames that later get no pose keep the poses computed at load, raw and filtered. | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:574,797`, `core/RigidBody.cpp:262-278`, `core/Trial.cpp:861-890` | Confirmed against a dump built for it (`pig_sparse_markerrefs`): with the port every table is identical; without it 42 values in each pose table differ by up to 17 units | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::poses_at_load`. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: Reference data: `pig_sparse_markerrefs` only: `rb_pose_optimized` up to 16.6 and `rb_pose_filtered` up to 17.4 at the frames the update leaves without a pose (which no longer keep the load-time poses). Test `a54_no_poses_at_load` (145 posed frames after loading in the C++ mode, 0 fixed). Before: The port computes with the stored 3D points and treats the unfitted grid as the identity, as the C++ effectively does at that moment. It assumes `<Marker>` elements come before `<RigidBody>` elements, as XMALab writes them. |
| A55 | With `RecomputeWhenSaving` on, a save waits for the scheduler's `running` flag to clear, but an update that starts no work leaves it set, so the C++ waits for ever when no trial is flagged or a flagged trial has no frames. The setting is off by default. | `processing/ThreadScheduler.cpp:67`, `ui/MainWindow.cpp:965-967` | Confirmed in the source (save port) | Fix | The engine does not hang. |
| A56 | The C++ has a data race between its interface thread and the trial update. Drawing the frame shown calls `transformPoint` on a body that another visible body's virtual point refers to; if that body's flag has just been cleared by the update, the interface thread computes its pose too, and the refinement reads the held pose outside its lock, so one thread can refine the pose the other has already refined. The body's errors at that frame then come out one of two ways from run to run. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:297-300,2669-2692`, `processing/UpdateTrialFrame.cpp:62-70`, `processing/RigidBodyPoseOptimization.cpp:187-203,255` | Confirmed by experiment: four dumps of the same project gave one value three times and the other once (they differ by 6e-14); refining the held pose once more reproduces the other value bit for bit | Fix | The Rust engine is deterministic and always gives the unraced value. `xma parity` replays the other orders of the race (each affected body's pose at the frame on screen refined twice, with and without the bodies that depend on it recomputed) for the two updates whose results a dump holds, accepts a differing cell only if it equals one of those orders exactly, and reports it as explained, not as identical; a cell further downstream that no order reproduces is reported as not compared, naming the raced cell. One raced dump is kept on purpose as a test (`rv_vpchain_b_raced`). Only projects with virtual points attached to another body are affected, at the frame on screen during an update. An independent review saw it in about 1 dump in 40, and found it can change a pose as well as an error (by about 1e-10 in the case seen), in any of the updates the dump runs. |
| A57 | The numeric code calls `sin`, `cos`, `acos` and `pow` from the platform's C maths library (Rodrigues conversion, the filter design, the pose refinement, rotation continuity), as the C++ does. Those functions are not specified to the last bit, so their results can differ between Linux (glibc), macOS and Windows. Everything else in the Rust code is specified exactly (no reordering, fused multiply-add only where written), but through these functions the Rust results can differ between platforms in the last bits, and the refinement can amplify that (compare F21). | `crates/xma-vision/src/camera.rs`, `filter.rs`, `pose_refine.rs`, `rigid.rs` | Not measured: every comparison so far was run on one Linux machine | Open | The bit-for-bit agreement with the C++ is established for Linux with glibc only. To be measured when the tests are run on Windows and macOS. If platform-independent results are wanted, a pure-Rust maths library could replace these calls, at the price of no longer matching glibc to the last bit. Yours to decide after the measurement. Added 14 November 2026 (editing parity): XMALab's standard deviations add `pow(x, 2)`, which the reference build does not turn into `x * x` but sends to glibc's `pow`; that differs from the correctly rounded product in the last bit for about 1 argument in 1,100, and so the rigid-body error standard deviations (`rb_error_details.csv`) and the marker-to-marker distances (`MarkerDistances_<trial>.txt`) differed from the C++ in their last bits. The Rust code now calls the platform's `pow` there too (`xma_vision::cmath::pow`: `core/RigidBody.cpp:438, 1267, 1313`, `core/Trial.cpp:1392, 1479, 2488`, `core/Marker.cpp:1683`, the plots' statistics), and every such value of the editing reference is identical. |
| A58 | The marker-to-marker standard deviation pools the squared deviations of every marker pair (each about its own pair mean) and divides by the total count minus 1, not by the sum of each pair's count minus 1; `Trial::getMarkerToMarkerSD` then pools the bodies' values weighted by `count - 1` and divides by the total count minus 1. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:407-445` (`RigidBody::getMarkerToMarkerSD`), `core/Trial.cpp:2476-2495` (`Trial::getMarkerToMarkerSD`) | Confirmed in the source | Fix, compat (the author, 14 November 2026: "Fix it") | Default: the standard pooled value, the summed squared deviations divided by the sum over pairs of (n − 1), for a body and for a trial; 0 when no pair has two distances. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::pooled_sd_total_count`): as the C++. Used in the precision report (column "marker to marker sd", `core/Trial.cpp:1500`, `Trial::savePrecisionInfo`) and Project Overview (`ui/ProjectOverview.cpp:163,193`); the C++ also writes it to the XMA Portal metadata (`ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:1284,1315`), not yet ported. XMALab's value is the smaller by the factor √((N − pairs)/(N − 1)): 0.2% for 3 pairs over 145 frames, 2.3% for 10 pairs over 20 frames. Measured (test as for A7): `pig_clean` body 1 0.012588266 → 0.012630344 (+0.33%), body 2 0.010299133 → 0.010334264 (+0.34%), trial 0.011510120 → 0.011551139 (+0.36%); the largest relative change on any reference dataset is 0.58% (`pig_original`, `v05_datafile_lines`); a body whose markers form one pair does not change. When the bodies of a trial hold one distance in total the C++ trial value is NaN (0/0), kept in the XMALab 3 mode; fixed, it is 0. The Project Overview copy of this arithmetic had summed each pair separately and differed from the C++ by up to 1.1e-15 relative; both callers now share `xma_io::export::precision::body_marker_to_marker_sd` and `trial_marker_to_marker_sd`. Independent review (14 November 2026, no defect found): the `pig_clean` values were recomputed in Python from the C++ 3D export in both definitions and agree to the last digit or two (body 1: 1,354 distances, 1,344 degrees of freedom); 20,000 random trials against a separate implementation gave no mismatch; the XMALab 3 mode precision report is byte-identical to the C++ file. |
| A59 | The filter is run in transfer-function form (one numerator and one denominator polynomial of order 4), which loses accuracy when the cutoff is a very small or very large fraction of the recording speed. Measured as the amplitude passed at the entered cutoff, which should be 0.7071 in the default mode: 0.7071 from 1 Hz to 124 Hz at 250 frames per second, but 0.620 at 0.01 Hz and 0.855 at 124.99 Hz; scipy's `filtfilt` and this program then differ by up to 0.58. The same loss is in the C++ and in the XMALab 3 mode. | `processing/ButterworthLowPassFilter.cpp` (`filter`, `filtfilt`); `crates/xma-vision/src/filter.rs` | Measured by the independent review of A7 (14 November 2026) | Open | Copied for now. It matters only for cutoffs below roughly 1/250 of the recording speed or within a fraction of a hertz of half of it, far outside normal use. Running the filter as second-order sections would remove it and change filtered values in the last digits everywhere else. For the author to decide; no hurry. (16 November 2026) The zero-phase filter and the spline are now this project's own code, written from `docs/filter-spline-spec.md` instead of ported (A208); results are unchanged, bit for bit. |
| A60 | After an edit (a point set, moved or deleted, a tracking step, an interpolation), rotations are not made continuous again and nothing is refiltered: filtered poses, filtered errors and what is drawn from them stay as they were until Plot > Update, a Trial-dialog change or a full update. Several rigid-body changes likewise recompute poses without filtering, or recompute nothing (new correspondences, the cutoff override, the `OptimizeRigidBody`, `DisableRBComputeAdvanced` and `Filter3DPoints` settings). | `core/Marker.cpp:236`; `ui/RigidBodyDialog.cpp:236-259`, `:404-410`, `:497`, `:508`, `:522`, `:527`; `ui/SettingsDialog.cpp:363-376` (`docs/cpp-editing.md` D10) | Read in the source (editing survey, 14 November 2026) | Fix, compat | Default: when an edit's step is committed, derived data is what a full recomputation would give (`Document::settle`, `CppCompat::edits_not_refiltered`). To build in phase 3 with editing. **Built (phase 3):** the rule holds value for value, including chains of virtual points, bodies driven by later bodies and bodies whose rotations the continuity step replaces: a body recomputed in full gets the continuity step at once (so a later point edit of the same step carries the step on from its frame, `Document::continue_rotations`), the driving bodies are always computed afresh (A36), and the finalize order is fixed (A91). A project opened with a cycle of virtual points (A92) keeps the rule for raw poses and the pose filter: a body on the cycle is recomputed with the bodies that follow it, so the cycle is entered where the update enters it; with `Filter3DPoints` the filter computes a driving body without a pose on demand from the stored poses, and on a cycle that can find a pose the update did not, which an edit does not reproduce (not reached by any test; cycles cannot be made by an edit). Tests `edit_consistency.rs` (`body_and_structure_edits_with_replaced_rotations`, `a_project_opened_with_a_cycle_stays_consistent`), `review_random.rs` (randomised sequences, also `REVIEW_STEPS=300`). |

No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour. Measured, the switch alone, on the 11 plain reference runs (`crates/xma-engine/tests/tracking_fix_effects.rs`; points differing / moved / largest move / tracked in one result only), centred at index 30 before, on the prediction after: r01 15/15/0.71/0 before, 30/30/0.71/0 after; r02 6/6/0.50 to 14/14/0.71; r04 5/5/0.71 to 6/6/0.71; r05 4/4/0.52 to 6/6/0.52; r09 3/3/0.50 to 4/4/0.50; r10 3/3/0.50 to 4/4/0.50; r12 36/36/0.71 to 38/38/0.71; r11 (penalties 90 and 10, several methods) 199/183/9.8/16 to 319/187/9.8/132 (the fix changes which frame markers are lost at); r03, r13, r14 none. |

Tracker (`MarkerTracking.cpp:176-177`): built 14 November 2026 (`xma_vision::tracking::TrackCompat::window_round_toward_zero`). On the 11 plain reference runs no window reaches past the left or top edge: no point changes. |
| A66 | The polynomial point refinement computes the fitted ellipse's rotation with a mis-bracketed expression; the refined radius is affected, and through it the next cycle's window and weights, so the refined centre changes as well. A NaN radius is not caught until after the loop, and the next cycle converts NaN to an integer (undefined behaviour). | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:597` (`docs/cpp-tracking.md` T15, T16) | Read in the source | Fix, compat | Default: `0.5 * atan2(2b, c - a)`, the principal axes of the quadratic part, defined everywhere (`CppCompat::polyfit_rotation_bracket`, `xma_vision::detection::polyfit_rotation`); a NaN radius ends the refinement without a result in both modes. (Built 14 November 2026 as the C++'s intended `0.5 * (pi/2 - atan((c - a) / 2 / b)) + [a < c] * pi/2`, which is 0/0 for an isotropic fit, a = c and b = 0, so a valid round peak gave no result; corrected 15 November 2026 after review. The two differ by a multiple of pi/2, which leaves the radius unchanged in exact arithmetic.) In the XMALab 3 mode the port equals the C++ function bit for bit on 244 runs on pig-frame patches when both use OpenCV's own QR solve. Against the probe's implementation of the intended expression (`out_qrimpl_fixedrot`, 367 runs): same outcome and cycle count everywhere, 121 bit for bit, the pig-frame runs within 2.3e-14 relative; on the synthetic windows flat but for one pixel (quartic terms that are rounding noise, the windows on which the solvers of A37 part by 25%) the radius parts by up to 4.9% in 16 runs. Tests `fixed_rotation_matches_probe`, `fixed_rotation_is_the_principal_axes` (eigenvectors of the form, isotropic and b = 0 included), `elliptical_peak_of_known_axes_radius_does_not_depend_on_orientation` (an exact quartic peak at 12 orientations: corrected radius within 3.2e-5 of the known 4.0825, XMALab 3 off by up to 41%), `gaussian_spot_radius_spread_with_orientation` (a Gaussian spot: 2.9% spread corrected against 8.9% in XMALab 3; the remaining spread comes from the square window that truncates the weights, a property of the method), `review_a66_rotation_defined_for_isotropic_quadric`. The rotation alone changes 249 of the 367 reference results, before and after. The whole default mode (with A65, A90) against XMALab 3 on the 367 runs: before 118 same, 229 changed, 10 refined only in XMALab 3, 10 only in the default; after 128 same, 212 changed, 27 refined only in XMALab 3 (A90), none only in the default; since the minimum rule of A90 (15 November 2026), 128 same, 157 changed, 82 refined only in XMALab 3, none only in the default. |

XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::clear_point_idx_keeps_original`). Tests `e70_copy_from_trial`, `apply_default_to_trials_copies_markers_and_bodies` (both sides). |
| A80 | `RigidBody::copyData` ("Apply to trials", "Copy From trial"): the virtual-point loop tests `this->dummyRBIndex[i]` of a list it has just cleared (an out-of-range read), so virtual points are copied unpredictably or not at all; and the cutoff is set to the source's override **flag** (0 or 1). | `core/RigidBody.cpp:113-135` (`docs/cpp-editing.md` D4) | Read in the source; the editing reference keeps out of copying bodies with virtual points (`oracle/cpp/DEVIATIONS.md` item 50) and shows no difference with or without the flag behaviour | Fix | In both modes: the virtual points driven by a body are copied (the loop's evident intent; points animated by a file hold the source trial's frames and are not copied) and the cutoff is copied. The out-of-range read is undefined behaviour; the cutoff flag behaviour is defined but no reference reaches it, so the XMALab 3 mode does not reproduce it (no switch). `xma_engine::edit::body_ops::copy_data`, the only port of `copyData` (the edit-trials package's second port was removed). |
| A81 | `RigidBody::copyData` ends with `initialised = rb->initialised`: a copied body whose own markers give it references (`referencesSet` 1) stays uninitialised when the source was, and gets no pose until the project is reloaded. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:136` | Read in the source; not reached by the editing reference | Fix | In both modes: initialised when its references are set. No switch (no reference shows the C++ behaviour). |
