No switch. |
| A83 | `RigidBody::addDummyPoint` reads an optional fourth value on the reference file's second line (the driving body's index, as `saveDummy` writes it; -1 for a point animated by coordinates), which replaces what the dialog chose. When it contradicts the dialog the C++ makes a point with no data (-1 for a point chosen to follow a body: no coordinate file was asked for), follows another body, or crashes (a body index for a point chosen to be animated by coordinates: `coords_list.at(1)` of the second file, which was never asked for; an index that is no body of the trial). | `core/RigidBody.cpp:1966-1975`, `:2012-2021`; `ui/RigidBodyDialog.cpp:412-500` | Read in the source; the editing reference (`e03_bodies`) writes no fourth value | Fix, compat | Default: the input given to the edit (`VirtualPointInput`) decides; a fourth value that disagrees with it is ignored, with a Console line saying so. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::virtual_point_file_chooses_body`): the fourth value decides where the C++ is defined (another body of the trial; -1 giving a point with no coordinates); where the C++ crashes the edit is refused (`EditError::Rejected`). `xma parity e03_bodies` is unchanged. Tests `review_edits.rs` `review_a83_a_fourth_value_in_the_file_does_not_change_the_input`, `edit_bodies.rs` `e42_virtual_points` (both sides). |
| A84 | Merge-aware tracking: when a provisional split is confirmed with the identities swapped, a provisional frame where any member's point is no longer `TRACKED` or `PREDICTED` (reset, or a user's point) gets no swap at all: every editable member keeps its own point, which the decision has just said is the other marker's, and it is confirmed as `TRACKED`. | `processing/MergeMonitor.cpp:733-743` (`MergeMonitor::commitPending`; `docs/cpp-tracking.md` T13) | Read in the source; not reached by the reference runs (r06 confirms without a swap) | Fix, compat (the users'-point part decided by the author, 16 November 2026) | Default: on such a frame a member whose partner's point is editable gets the partner's point and size; a member whose partner's point is gone is reset there (its own point is the partner's blob, and where its own blob was is no longer known). XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::merge_commit_swap_needs_all`). No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. Test `a84_swap_with_a_missing_partner` (`tracking/merge/groups.rs`, both sides). Changes nothing on the reference runs. When the confirming frame itself holds a user's point (`SET`) for one member, the swap is decided with that point as a candidate and applied to the provisional frames, so that member's provisional points become its partner's and the user's point says the opposite; at the confirming frame the other member keeps its own point in XMALab 3 and is reset in the default mode (test `a84_swap_against_a_users_point_at_the_confirming_frame`, both modes). Decided by the author, 16 November 2026: a point set by hand fixes that marker's identity; a swap that contradicts it is not confirmed. **Built 16 November 2026** (default mode; XMALab 3 mode `CppCompat::merge_swap_against_users_points`, a separate switch from `merge_commit_swap_needs_all`: that one says how a confirmation writes frames where a member is not editable, this one whether a swap is confirmed at all, and points below `SET` that are not editable, such as `INTERPOLATED`, still reach the first). First step (its confirming-frame part superseded by the second step below): when a pending group's assignment at the confirming frame would confirm a swap that moves a member (`choice[i] != i`) whose point at any frame the confirmation writes (the pending frames and the confirming frame) has status `SET` or above, the group is **stopped** (`stopGroup`, Console line "... stopped in this camera because confirming the split would swap marker M away from its point set by hand at frame F; N frame(s) cleared"). Stopped rather than kept pending: pending is the monitor's state for evidence below the margin, while here the evidence is decisive (margin above the threshold) and contradicts the user's point; that contradiction cannot resolve later, because the user's point stays in the window and every later swap would be refused too, so pending would only lead to the pending limit's stop with more provisional points to clear; stopping is what the monitor does for its other unresolved splits ("overlapped again", "marker lost"). Consequences: every unconfirmed point of the members in the group's window (held and pending frames, the confirming frame; with two visible cameras also their unconfirmed points in the other camera) is cleared, as in every stop; points set by hand are never moved or overwritten (only `TRACKED` and `PREDICTED` points are cleared); no provisional point is confirmed under either marker; a member whose point at the confirming frame was cleared is not tracked on from there in that camera, and a member with its own point set by hand there is tracked on from it. A swap that moves no member with a hand-set point (for example among three members) is confirmed as before. Tests (`crates/xma-engine/src/tracking/merge/groups.rs`, `users_point_tests`, on run r05's result with the trackers' blobs swapped): `a84_swap_confirmed_without_users_points` (both modes confirm), `a84_users_point_at_the_confirming_frame_fixes_the_assignment` (renamed in the second step; XMALab 3 confirms the swap, default now confirms without one) and `a84_no_swap_against_a_users_point_at_a_provisional_frame` (XMALab 3 confirms; default stops, keeps the user's point, clears the rest); `a84_swap_against_a_users_point_at_the_confirming_frame` kept for what `commit_pending` does when called with such an order (now reached only in XMALab 3 mode). Reference runs: `xma parity` passes on r05 to r08 unchanged (XMALab 3 mode); in the default mode the switch changes nothing on r05 to r08 (`tracking_fix_effects.rs`, 0 points differ): no run confirms a swap. **Second step, 16 November 2026** (review 9, finding 4, `crates/xma-engine/tests/review9_merge_users.rs`: a separating group placed marker 4 `TRACKED` on marker 8's hand-set point, as XMALab's `placeMember` does): the author's rule is built for every assignment the monitor makes, in the default mode, under the same switch `CppCompat::merge_swap_against_users_points` (one decision: a point set by hand fixes its marker's identity; XMALab 3 mode unchanged). Hand-set means `SET`, `SET_AND_OPTIMIZED`, `MANUAL`, `MANUAL_AND_OPTIMIZED`. Places: `update_group` for a merged group (separation, provisional split, partial placement), for a pending group (each provisional frame, the confirmation) and `commit_pending` (`docs/cpp-tracking.md` 5.6). Rule: a member with a hand-set point at the frame is fixed on it: `candidates` adds its point first, so any other point or detector blob closer than the merge distance (`2 max r + 1`, the monitor's own test of two points being one blob) is that blob and is dropped; `assign` gives the fixed member only its own candidate (cost 0, no alternative, infinite margin) and makes that candidate unavailable to every other member (the fourth step corrects how a member whose tracker sits on it, and the other members' margins, are treated); the other members are assigned among the remaining blobs with the costs, margins and thresholds unchanged, and a fixed member counts as clean. A member left without a blob, or a split the evidence cannot resolve, gets what the existing design gives the same case without hand-set points: held (merged group), kept pending, or dropped when its only blob is beyond 3 T (pending group, "it no longer matches its evidence", after which a survivor is confirmed with its own points); in a group of two a hand-set point therefore resolves the group at its frame. Distance rule: no marker is placed on a blob within the merge distance of another marker's hand-set point at that frame (candidates); a blob within the merge distance of the hand-set point of another marker is not available (the fourth step extends this from the run's markers to every marker of the trial, and replaces the pending group's stop by a stop of the member concerned). The first step's refused swap is now needed only for hand-set points at earlier provisional frames (a group of three or more, where the frame with the hand-set point left the other members ambiguous): at the confirming frame the assignment already keeps the fixed member in place, so `users_point_against` checks the pending frames only. Part of the rule: a stop's clearing with two visible cameras keeps the marker's point in the other camera at frames where its point was set by hand, so it is tracked on from the user's point (XMALab 3 clears it, and the marker is then stopped as tracked in one camera only). Tests: `crates/xma-engine/tests/merge_users_points.rs` (`a84_hand_set_points_fix_identity_in_every_assignment`: 120 combinations of groups of two and three, forward and backward, a clear or provisional first split, the hand-set point on each member at the separation, a provisional and the confirming frame, each hand-set status, checking that no hand-set point changes, no two members share a position and no marker is placed or held within the merge distance of another's hand-set point, and that every path above is reached; `a84_xmalab3_places_members_on_hand_set_points`; `a84_undo_and_redo_of_a_step_the_rule_acted_in`; full `Tracker` runs from r06's start project with marker 8 set by hand at frame 55, `a84_full_run_hand_set_point_on_its_own_blob` and `a84_full_run_hand_set_point_on_the_other_markers_blob`: the marker set by hand is tracked on from its point, the other is cleared and not tracked on). Review 9's finding test passes unchanged; four other review-9 tests assert the first step's refusal for groups of two and were changed with dated comments (`review9_hand_set_points_are_never_changed_and_block_the_swap`, `review9_undo_restores_the_window_after_a_refused_swap`, `review9_hand_set_points_in_the_other_camera`, `review9_other_stops_leave_hand_set_points`). Reference runs after the second step: `xma parity` passes on r05 to r08 unchanged; in the default mode the switch changes 0 points on r05 to r08 (none has a hand-set point in a merge). No C++ reference for the default behaviour. **Third step, 16 November 2026** (the coordinator, correcting the second step's brief): holds are allowed near a hand-set point. In an overlap two markers are at one place in the image; a hold is `PREDICTED` from the epipolar line with size -1, claims no blob and is never confirmed from one, and the author's rule is about identity, not about markers overlapping. Removed: the second step's stop of a marker whose hold (`hold_member`, also at group formation, or `hold_single`) would land within the merge distance of another marker's hand-set point; such a marker is held as in XMALab. Kept: the assignment rule above (candidates, `assign`, the confirmation, `users_point_against` on the provisional frames); the pending group's stop when a member's provisional point is within the merge distance of the hand-set point of a marker of the run outside the group (a provisional point is a blob placement that the confirmation writes `TRACKED`, so this is the assignment rule, not a hold); the two-camera clearing above, part of the rule. Paths from `PREDICTED` to `TRACKED` for a held point: `commit_pending` writes only pending frames, which hold blob placements, never held frames; in a group, the next frame's tracker point is a candidate only if it is not a blob a hand-set point fixes, otherwise the member is held again; outside a group, `check_markers` releases a held marker only when its new point is not within the merge distance of any measured marker of the run ("it came back on marker N's blob", unchanged from XMALab), so a hand-set point keeps it held. Tests: `a84_hand_set_points_fix_identity_in_every_assignment` now checks that no marker is on a blob (`TRACKED`, or `PREDICTED` with a blob's size) within the merge distance of another's hand-set point and allows holds; new `a84_tight_overlap_with_a_hand_set_point_holds_the_other_member` (process-frame, both directions: the other member is held through the overlap and separated `TRACKED` after it, nothing stopped or cleared) and `a84_full_run_hand_set_point_inside_the_overlap` (r06 with marker 8 set by hand at frame 45: marker 4 has data at every frame from 34 to 60 and is `TRACKED` from 58, no stop); review 9's `review9_hand_set_points_are_never_changed_and_block_the_swap` allows a hold next to the hand-set point (its other second-step changes still hold: the assignment rule is unchanged). `a84_full_run_hand_set_point_on_the_other_markers_blob` is unchanged: the pending group stops ("overlapped again", the members' provisional points closer than the merge distance), marker 8 is tracked on from its point, marker 4 is cleared. Reference runs: `xma parity` passes on r05 to r08 unchanged (XMALab 3 mode); in the default mode the switch changes 0 points on r05 to r08. **Fourth step, 16 November 2026** (review 10, `crates/xma-engine/tests/review10_merge_cases.rs` and `review10_merge_r06.rs`). (1) Regression corrected: a member whose own tracker point was within the merge distance of another member's hand-set point was given an infinite own cost, which opened the detector's blobs to it; its only candidate then had an infinite ("clear") margin, and on r06 with marker 4 set by hand at 52 at its own position marker 8 was written `TRACKED` on a blob about 10 px away and the run lost both markers from 54. Now such a member still overlaps the marker set by hand: it can take no candidate, the assignment fails and the members are held (merged group) or the split stays provisional (pending), as XMALab holds overlapping markers. Margins: a free member's costs are computed as XMALab computes them (own cost, detector gate, reach), and its margin is its cost at the best other candidate, taken candidates included, minus its assigned cost; a margin is never clear only because alternatives were taken, and a single remaining candidate is accepted only with the margin, cost (3 T) and blob size XMALab would require. Taken candidates (a fixed member's point, or a blob within the merge distance of another marker's hand-set point) are kept in the list for the margins and only made unavailable, rather than removed. A consequence: a hand-set point at a provisional frame no longer resolves a group of two by itself (the other member's margin is the evidence's own); the split stays provisional, and a swap decided later against the hand-set point is refused as in the first step. (2) A member with only a rigid-body prediction (no clean camera) cannot be held on an epipolar line; XMALab leaves its tracker's point as it is, `TRACKED`, also on another marker's hand-set point (kept in XMALab 3 mode). Default: where that point is within the merge distance of another marker's hand-set point it is held at its rigid-body prediction (`PREDICTED`, size -1): it keeps a position, and claims no blob; clearing it would lose the frame, and the prediction is the evidence the monitor already holds such a member by. (3) Hand-set points of markers the run does not track: `candidates` and the pending check now consider every marker of the trial, by one pass over the trial's markers collecting their hand-set points in that camera at that frame. (4) Left as it is by the author's decision of 21 November 2026 ("Leave it": two markers can coincide in one camera's image, and outside a group nothing signals a conflict): a marker outside any group whose tracker lands on another marker's hand-set point stays `TRACKED` there, as in plain tracking, in XMALab and with either value of the switch; and a hand-set point at the frame where an overlap would start keeps the group from forming (`detect_new_groups` takes editable markers only). Measured by review 10 on r06: with a point set on the other marker's position at frames 26 to 34 or 58 to 60 the other marker is tracked onto it; set on its own position at frame 34, the run stops with about 22 frames lost in both modes. A remedy would mean letting a hand-set marker take part in group formation as a fixed member, and having the audit hold (`hold_single`) a marker outside a group whose fresh point is within the merge distance of another marker's hand-set point, i.e. changing when groups form and what the plain tracker's points are accepted as. Tests `review10_finding_outside_group_tracker_on_hand_set_point` and `review10_finding_outside_group_tracked_on_hand_set_point_r06` are ignored with that reason. (6) Checked: two members set by hand on one blob: the second's own point is the first's candidate, its cost row is infinite, the assignment fails and nothing is written (holds skip hand-set points), as XMALab holds them; no loss found (`review10_both_members_hand_set_on_one_blob`), not changed. A pending member on another marker's hand-set point outside the group stopped the whole group; it now stops only that member ("it is on marker N's point set by hand", its unconfirmed points cleared) and a lone survivor keeps its provisional points as tracked, as when a member no longer matches its evidence (test `a84_pending_member_on_a_hand_set_point_outside_the_run`). (5) Yardstick: 140 full runs of r06 (frames 26 to 60, each marker, set on its own and on the other's position), the default mode against the default mode with only this switch set as XMALab: 128 identical trials, 4 different with equal measures, 8 better (frames 54 to 57 with a point set on the other marker's position: the default follows the correction and keeps 3 to 6 more frames for the marker set by hand, fewer stops and the identity the user set at 58 to 60, where the switch stops both markers), 0 worse (worse: fewer frames with data for either marker, fewer of frames 58 to 60 with the right identity, or more stops). Tests `a84_r06_default_never_worse_than_xmalab_every_frame` (ignored, several minutes) and `a84_r06_default_never_worse_than_xmalab_subset` (frames 30, 34, 45, 52, 53, 55, 57, 59, in the normal check). Review tests changed: `review10_undo_redo_of_single_steps_where_the_rule_acts` asserted the "clear px margin" of the corrected fault (now asserts the later confirmation); `review9_hand_set_points_are_never_changed_and_block_the_swap` and `review9_other_stops_leave_hand_set_points` have their original default-mode assertions back for hand-set points at provisional frames; unit test `a84_users_point_at_the_confirming_frame_fixes_the_assignment` now expects the split to stay provisional. Reference runs: `xma parity` passes on r05 to r08 unchanged (XMALab 3 mode); in the default mode the switch changes 0 points on r05 to r08 (no hand-set point in a merge). **The code this row describes was removed with merge-aware tracking on 26 November 2026 (A226).** |
| A85 | Merge-aware tracking: the messages of a group stopped in a camera ("... stopped in this camera because ...") and of an overlap not resolved in time ("... their overlap was not resolved within N frames ...") print the size of a one-element vector, so always "1 frame(s) cleared". | `processing/MergeMonitor.cpp:766-768`, `:834-836` (`docs/cpp-tracking.md` T14) | Read in the source; not reached by the reference runs | Fix, compat | Default: the number of points cleared (as the single-member message does). XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::merge_cleared_count_one`). Messages only; no numbers change. Test `a85_group_stop_messages_count_the_points_cleared` (`tracking_merge.rs`: run r06 with `MergeMaxPendingFrames` 2 and run r07 with `MergeMaxOverlapFrames` 5 reach both messages; XMALab 3 prints 1 where 84 points were cleared). **The code this row describes was removed with merge-aware tracking on 26 November 2026 (A226).** |
| A86 | A stop (Escape, Stop, a second press of the running button) that arrives while a step's tracker or detection jobs run sets the direction and kind to 0 before the step's check: types 1-3 then skip the validity check of that step, and types 5-6 run `MergeMonitor::processFrame` with `forward = false`, which counts the frame as a run change (groups and holds forgotten) and uses `frame + 1` as the previous frame for motion, holds, recovery and clearing. | `ui/WizardDigitizationFrame.cpp:464`, `:859-864`, `:722-743` (`docs/cpp-tracking.md` T11, section 7) | Read in the source; depends on when the stop arrives, so no reference run reaches it (their driver stops after the step's check, `oracle/DATASETS.md`) | Fix | In both modes a stop takes effect between steps (`Tracker::stop`): every step is checked, and every frame is processed with the run's direction. Not reproducible as a switch (timing-dependent). |
| A90 | The polynomial point refinement returns a refined point and radius from a last cycle whose fit is not a peak: the loop stops at the first cycle with `J = ac - b^2 <= 0`, but that cycle's clamped correction has already been applied and its radius computed, and both are returned (the radius is stored as the marker size). A last-cycle radius of `+inf` (a quartic term of zero) is returned too, and one of 0 would be. It also returns a last fit with `J > 0` whose quadratic part is a minimum of the fitted quantity (`a + c > 0`): the fitted quantity is `255 - v` for a dark marker and `v` for a bright one (`:513-520`), so a marker is a maximum of it in both polarities (`J > 0`, `a + c < 0`, with `a = p3`, `c = p5`), and a spot of the other polarity (a light spot refined as a dark marker, a dark one as bright) is a minimum, to whose centre the C++ moves the point. Finally, a fit can diverge: on an image that is exactly quadratic around the point the quartic terms are rounding noise, the radius comes out near 1e8, and the next cycle's window of `(2w + 1)^2` rows of 15 doubles cannot be allocated: the C++ ends with OpenCV's out-of-memory exception, the port (before this fix) aborted the process, in both modes. | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:471-494`, `:583-606`, `:682-691` | Confirmed with the probe (`oracle/py/refine_probe`, columns `peak`, `maximum`, `wmax`, `big`; cases `refinenonpeak-*`, `refinedip-*`, `refinebound-*`): on `refinenonpeak-*` (70 runs) the C++ returns 59 results from a last fit that is not a peak, one with radius `+inf` (`refinenonpeak-line` run 5); on `refinedip-*` (40 runs: a round and an elliptical spot, dark and light, each refined in both polarities) it returns all 20 runs of the wrong polarity, at the spot's centre with a finite radius; on `refinebound-quadratic` its windows grow to half-widths of 2e7 to 2.4e8 (the probe stops past 1024). The port reproduces every run of these cases bit for bit in the XMALab 3 mode, except where the C++ has no defined result or the window is flat (A37; below). On the pig frames XMALab 3 (LAPACK solve) refines 198 stored runs, 10 of them from a non-peak fit and 40 from a minimum (every run that refines one of the dark markers as a bright one). A radius of exactly 0 was not reached. | Fix, compat | Default: no result when the last cycle's `J` is not positive, its `a + c` is not negative, or its radius is not finite and positive (`CppCompat::refine_accepts_non_peak`, `DetectCompat::refine_accepts_non_peak`, `xma_vision::detection::refine_point_polynomial_fit`; the minimum rule added 15 November 2026 under the same switch). Window bound (15 November 2026; the same switch, which means "the C++ outcome for a refinement that has left the marker", sets it; no switch of its own): the refinement ends with no result as soon as a cycle's window half-width exceeds, by default, the larger of the image's width and height `N` (a window that large holds nothing but the image and its replicated border, and means the fit has diverged; each cycle allocates at most `(2N + 1)^2` rows of 15 doubles, about 0.5 GB for a 1024 x 1024 frame at the bound, which no real refinement approaches), and in the XMALab 3 mode the larger of `N` and 1024 (`REFINE_MAX_HALF_WIDTH_CPP`, the half-width up to which the probe runs the C++: the C++ returns a defined result for a window larger than the image whenever it can allocate it, and beyond 1024 that depends on the machine's memory; a cycle can then allocate up to `(2 x 1024 + 1)^2` rows of 15 doubles, about 0.5 GB, whatever the image size). The C++'s crash for a window it cannot allocate is not reproduced in either mode. Both modes: no result when the point is not finite or its window origin is not an `int`, and when the window size is negative. Measured on the stored cases (C++ window rounding, against the probe), the default mode gives no result for 80 runs the C++ refines from a non-peak fit (53 synthetic non-peak, 18 on windows flat but for one pixel, 9 on the pig crops, 5 of them the points with no marker 24 px off a marker) and 75 from a minimum (20 `refinedip-*`, 15 flat but for one pixel, 40 on the pig crops: all 40 runs that refine a dark pig marker as a bright one, and no other); the 177 others are kept to rounding. The window bound changes no stored run of `refinepolyfit-*`, `refinenonpeak-*`, `refinedip-*` (no window there passes it). On `refinebound-*` (134 runs) the XMALab 3 mode is the C++ (OpenCV's own QR) bit for bit on 121 runs; the other 13 are the 7 whose half-width passes 1024 (all on the exactly quadratic image, where the C++ would fail to allocate; no result) and 6 whose window is flat in some cycle (all on the 1 x 1 image; no result in both modes, A37, where the C++ returns rounding noise in 4). Of the 57 runs whose window grows past the image's larger side (images of 1 x 1 to 64 x 64 with a first window wider than the image, and the quadratic 41 x 41 images) the C++ returns a result in 46, and the XMALab 3 mode reproduces 42 of them bit for bit (the other 4 are those flat windows); the default mode returns none of the 57. On the real pig frames (all 2844 stored 2D points, 145 frames, both cameras, each marker's mean size and polarity, frames decoded from the AVIs and equal to the reference dumps on the 30 dumped frames): neither rule removes a single refinement in the default mode, and the XMALab 3 mode is unchanged on all 2844; refined in the wrong polarity, the default mode now gives no result on all 2844 (before: 2828 results, each at a dip). The same holds for the 40 points the `e10_place_refine` run refines. On the synthetic spots of `fulldepth_measured.rs` (`refinement_deep_and_reduced`, 600 trials a cell) the minimum rule adds misses only in noisy cells (contrast 1 with noise, up to 17; contrast 4, noise 2, slope: 2), and the window bound none. Tests `review_refinement_rejects_non_peak_fit`, `non_peak_cases_as_the_cpp`, `non_peak_fits_give_no_result_when_fixed`, `a_dip_is_not_a_marker` (a round spot in each polarity: refined in both modes; inverted, no result fixed, the dip's centre in XMALab 3; 8- and 16-bit), `bound_cases_as_the_cpp`, `exactly_quadratic_peak_gives_no_result`, `xmalab3_bound_at_1024` (a first window of half-width 1024 is fitted in the XMALab 3 mode, one of 1025 is not, neither in the default mode), `no_argument_allocates_without_bound_or_panics` (24330 calls in 2.4 s in a debug build, XMALab 3 calls whose first window is wider than the image limited to the first point and one polarity: images 1 x 1 to 64 x 64 and an exactly quadratic one, radii from `-f64::MAX` to `f64::MAX`, NaN and infinities, points far outside the image and not finite, both modes, both entry points: no panic, every result finite, cycles at most `3.2 radius + 3`). |
| A91 | The scheduler's finalize (`finalize_updateTrialData`) makes one body's rotations continuous and filters it before going on to the next body. With `Filter3DPoints` a body is filtered from its virtual points, whose world positions use the driving bodies' stored rotations: an earlier driving body's after the continuity step, a later one's before it (the same rotation, a different vector in the last bits where the step replaced it), so the filtered pose depends on the order of the bodies. | `processing/ThreadScheduler.cpp:101-106`, `core/RigidBody.cpp:1701-1904` | Found by the independent review of the editing code (15 November 2026; `review_random.rs`, `rv_vpchain` seed 11) | Fix, compat | Default: every body's rotations are made continuous first, then every body is filtered (the trial update, `Plot > Update` in the default mode, a settings change and `Document::settle`), so the result does not depend on the order and an edit can reproduce it. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::finalize_body_by_body`): body by body. Differences are in the last bits of filtered poses of bodies driven by later bodies, with `Filter3DPoints` only. Test `edit_consistency.rs` `a91_finalize_order_both_sides`. |
| A92 | A cycle of virtual points (bodies animating each other round to the first, or a body driven by itself) makes `RigidBody::transformPoint` and `computePose` call each other without end (a stack overflow). The dialog, the trial import, "Copy From trial" and "Apply to trials" can all create one. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:297-300`, `:1094-1118` | Found by the independent review of the editing code (15 November 2026) | Fix | In both modes no edit can create a cycle: an edit whose result has one in a trial that had none is `EditError::Rejected` with a sentence naming the bodies ("Virtual points cannot form a cycle: rigid body 1 follows rigid body 3, ..."), and changes nothing (`edit::ops::refuse_new_cycles`, checked after every edit; "Add virtual point" checks before computing). A project opened with a cycle keeps it: the computation cuts it where it meets it (the inner reference has no pose) with a warning, and edits of that trial are accepted; see A60 for what holds then. `xma_engine::edit::ops::virtual_point_cycle`. Tests `review_edits.rs` `review_a_cycle_of_virtual_points_is_refused_or_consistent`, `edit_bodies.rs` `e42_virtual_points`, `edit_trials.rs` `apply_default_to_trials_copies_markers_and_bodies`, `edit_consistency.rs` `a_project_opened_with_a_cycle_stays_consistent`. |
| A93 | "Set Body from File" on a body whose current reference points are all at one place (references never set and no frame with every marker in 3D, so `computeCoordinateSystemAverage` leaves the mean shape at the origin; a body made before tracking) fits all n! orders of the file's points, which fit equally to rounding, and keeps the one rounding favours. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:2512-2539` | Found by the independent review of the editing code (15 November 2026) | Fix, compat | Default: the file's order (the first point for the body's first marker, and so on), without a search, with a Console line saying why. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::exhaustive_degenerate_reference_search`): the exhaustive search and its tie rule while it stays within the search budget (up to 9 markers: 9! = 362,880 fits, the C++ result), and beyond it (10 markers and more, refused before) the file's order with the same line. The refusal ("could not be found in reasonable time") remains only for references that are not degenerate and whose search exceeds the budget, which needs 10 markers or more and references that neither match the file closely nor differ from it enough to prune (nearly symmetric or partly coincident shapes). Tests `review_edits.rs` `review_references_from_file_for_a_large_body_before_tracking`, `edit_bodies.rs` `a93_references_from_a_file_before_tracking` (both sides). The default-mode rule applies to references that are exactly equal; references that differ by 1e-6 or 1e-12 run the search, whose result then depends on those differences (measured by the third review: 3 markers gave order [2,1,0] for one pattern and [0,1,2] for another; 10 markers were refused); such references are reachable only from a stored project. |
| A96 | Merge-aware tracking: every frame of a run updates every overlap group the merge monitor holds, also groups none of whose members the run tracks (left by an earlier run whose monitor the window carries on, `MergeMonitor` being a singleton). Those members were not tracked to the frame, so they read as lost: a provisional (pending) group is stopped and its unconfirmed window cleared, a merged one releases them. A run of two other markers that continues a run of markers 4 and 8 on r06 resets 84 of their points. | `processing/MergeMonitor.cpp:303-310` (`MergeMonitor::processFrame` iterates `m_groups`) | Found by the second independent review of merge-aware tracking (15 November 2026); not reached by the reference runs, each of which starts from a new monitor | Fix, compat | Default: a run updates only the groups all of whose members it tracks. A group none of whose members it tracks is left as it is (state and points) and is taken up again by a later run that tracks all its members only when the document has not changed since (A97) and the group was followed at the frame before. A group only some of whose members the run tracks, or one not followed at the previous frame (a run of other markers came between), is forgotten without touching any point, with a Console line ("overlap group of markers ... left because ...; their points are kept"); the run's members of it are then tracked, audited and grouped as any other marker. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::merge_updates_every_group`). Order in a step: in the C++ the trackers of a step read `templateFrame` (`ui/WizardDigitizationFrame.cpp:392`, `trackMerge`) before `processFrame` runs (`:464`, `checkIfValidMerge`, after the detections), so what `processFrame` forgets at a step (here: the groups this selection drops) has already given that step's template frames. Default: the selection is applied when the step begins, before any template frame is read (`MergeMonitor::begin_step`, called by `Tracker::do_step`), so a member of a forgotten group is tracked from the start frame like any other marker; and a group not followed at the monitor's last processed frame gives no template frame (`MergeMonitor::template_frame`). XMALab 3 mode: the C++ order (`CppCompat::merge_template_before_reset`, shared with A97). Messages and points change only when a run continues a monitor holding groups of markers outside it, which no reference run does. Tests `review3_merge.rs` `review3_a96_runs_never_touch_markers_outside_them` (default), `merge_monitor_state.rs` `a96_a97_forgetting_precedes_the_first_template_frames` (default: a run of markers 4 and 0 continuing the monitor gives what a run from a new monitor gives, 2 points differed before; XMALab 3 mode measured), `a96_group_not_followed_gives_no_template_frame` (default), `review2_merge_stale.rs` `review2_merge_run_of_other_markers_leaves_a_pending_group_alone` (default), `merge_monitor_state.rs` `a96_xmalab3_run_of_other_markers_stops_the_group` (XMALab 3: the C++ behaviour, 84 points reset) and `a96_partial_group_is_forgotten_with_its_points` (default). No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. **The code this row describes was removed with merge-aware tracking on 26 November 2026 (A226).** |
| A97 | Merge-aware tracking: the merge monitor is a singleton reset only on a trial change, so the next merge-aware step continues the previous one's groups, holds and template frames whenever its frame follows the last one processed, whatever happened to the points in between (a hand correction between two single steps keeps the group and its pre-merge template frames). The C++ has no undo of a run; here, after an undone run, the next run would continue groups that describe points that no longer exist. | `processing/MergeMonitor.cpp:261-275` (`processFrame`), `ui/WizardDigitizationFrame.cpp:222` (the only `reset()`) | Found by the second independent review of merge-aware tracking (15 November 2026) | Fix (both modes; decided 15 November 2026) | The monitor remembers `Document::changes()` as its last processed frame left the document (carried across the commit of that run's own undo step, `MergeMonitor::follow_commit`); a run started from a monitor whose count differs (an undo, a redo, an edit or placed point, a settings change, a save, the writes of any other run) starts with an empty monitor (`MergeMonitor::for_document`), in both modes. `Document::changes()` moves exactly when the content changes: every write of a tracking run moves it (`Document::marker_mut`, `write_results`); an edit that fails (rejected, invalid) or stores what is there, and the commit of a step that changed nothing, leave it as it was (`Document::transact`, `commit_step`; trial updates and recomputations move it only when they changed a stored value); a copy saved by the autosave does not touch the document. Only a merge-aware run carries a monitor on: any other kind of run ignores the monitor it is given and hands on an empty one (`Tracker::with_monitor`, `into_monitor`), so the window's next merge-aware run after a plain run starts empty, in both modes. A merge-aware run that does not continue the monitor's last processed frame (another trial, a frame not next to it, the other direction) forgets its groups and holds; in the C++ this happens in `processFrame` (`processing/MergeMonitor.cpp:269-277`), after the first step's trackers have read their template frames from them (`ui/WizardDigitizationFrame.cpp:392` before `:464`). Default: forgotten when the step begins, before any template frame is read (`MergeMonitor::begin_step`); XMALab 3 mode: the C++ order (`CppCompat::merge_template_before_reset`; on r06, a kept monitor and a run backward from frame 55 differ in 5 points from a run from a new monitor in that mode, 0 in the default). Single merge-aware steps with nothing in between continue one overlap as in the C++. What a user can notice: correcting a point by hand (or changing a setting, or saving) between two merge-aware single steps ends the overlap groups and holds in progress; the next step tracks from the corrected frame's own template and finds any overlap afresh, where XMALab keeps holding the group with its pre-merge templates. The reference runs each start from a new monitor in one run, so they are unaffected. Test `review2_merge_stale.rs` `review2_undone_merge_run_does_not_steer_the_next_run` (both modes), `merge_monitor_state.rs` `a97_monitor_kept_only_while_the_document_is_unchanged` (both modes: a carried monitor, also across the commit, gives what one run gives; after a change the run equals one from a new monitor), `a97_plain_run_hands_on_an_empty_monitor` (both modes), `a97_change_count_moves_only_with_content`, `a96_a97_forgetting_precedes_the_first_template_frames` (both modes), `review3_merge.rs` `review3_a97_what_empties_the_monitor`, `review3_a97_plain_run_keeps_the_monitor`, `review3_a97_non_continuing_run_with_kept_monitor_measured`. **The code this row describes was removed with merge-aware tracking on 26 November 2026 (A226).** |
| A104 | Grid matching, orientation and spacing of the hexagonal grid: they come from the six beads nearest the centre bead, each of the six target directions (90, 30, -30, -90, -150, 150 degrees) independently taking the one whose angle is closest. This is right only when the six are the centre's six lattice neighbours, each near its own target. When a neighbour is missing, a second-ring bead (at about 1.73 spacings) is among the six; one bead can serve two targets (its raw angle, up to 150 degrees, then enters the mean and one distance is counted twice); and on a grid turned by about 30 degrees, where every neighbour lies midway between two targets, detection noise assigns neighbours to targets 60 degrees apart even when the six picks are distinct. The orientation is then off by multiples of 5 to 10 degrees and the spacing by up to 12 %; the lattice grows from them and stops early, pairs wrong beads, or matches every bead with references that are not a similarity of the beads (a distortion that is not there). | `processing/LocalUndistortion.cpp:604-628`, `:658-680` (`docs/cpp-calibration.md` CD1) | Read in the source; not reached by the calibration runs (every grid matching of the 12 runs with grid hooks passes the consistency test below). Review 4 (synthetic): with one neighbour of the centre missing (726 cases, every 0.5 degree from 0 to 60) XMALab 3 is off by up to 30 degrees and leaves 111 matchings incomplete; with 0.1 px of detection noise at 28 to 32 degrees XMALab 3 is wrong in 277 of 1,203 cases | Fix, compat | Default: XMALab's estimate is kept, bit for bit, wherever it is consistent with a hexagonal lattice: the six picks are six different beads, the six residuals `angle + target` lie within 20 degrees of each other (max - min), and the largest of the six distances is at most 1.25 times the smallest. Otherwise the corrected estimate: among the 12 beads nearest the centre, each distance up to 1.2 times the nearest is tried as the spacing; the beads within 20 % of it are the first ring; the orientation is their mean modulo 60 degrees (the circular mean of `6 angle - 180`, divided by 6) and the spacing their mean distance; the estimate is checked against the lattice it implies (a bead supports it when it lies within 0.2 spacings of one of the six first-ring sites, each site once), recomputed from its supporting beads, and accepted with at least 4 of them (the smallest spacing, then the most support, then the smallest summed distance to the sites). When no estimate passes: XMALab's when its picks are distinct, else each of the six beads takes its own closest target and the spacing is the mean of the six distances. Measured (package fixcal1, 15 November 2026): the corrected estimate is taken in all 726 missing-neighbour cases (worst orientation error 9e-14 degrees, spacing 3e-14, references within 5e-13 px of a similarity of the beads, every bead matched) and in the 279 noisy cases near 30 degrees where XMALab's is inconsistent (afterwards none is off by more than 2 degrees and every matching is complete and consistent); it is not taken on any grid of the 12 calibration runs, nor on the reviewer's full grids (241 angles, 200 noisy grids away from 30 degrees, 100 barrel-distorted grids), whose orientation, spacing, grid points and references are bit for bit those before the change. XMALab 3 mode (`GridMatchCompat::six_neighbours_any_target`): the C++ estimate always. Tests `grid_match.rs` `a104_consistent_estimate_kept_corrected_otherwise`, `a104_duplicate_target_pick`, `grid_hooks_identical_default`, `grid_hooks_identical_xmalab3`, `c01_dumps_identical_default`; `review4_grid.rs` `missing_centre_neighbour_orientation_and_references`, `noisy_beads_near_30_degrees`, `rotation_sweep_full_grid`. No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: needs the independent review. Engine switch: `CppCompat::grid_neighbour_for_two_targets`. |
| A105 | Grid matching: a lattice site counts as already matched when its accumulated `double` coordinates equal those of a matched site (`contains`, exact `==`), but the same site reached along two paths of the breadth-first growth can differ in the last bits, and is then matched again to a second bead when one lies within the threshold of the expected position: two control points with the same reference position. | `processing/LocalUndistortion.cpp:690-698`, `:700-711` | Read in the source; not reached by the calibration runs (no site matched twice in any `grid_matches.csv`) | Fix, compat | Default: sites are compared by their integer lattice coordinates (column, row); when no site is matched twice (every reference grid) the result is the C++'s bit for bit. XMALab 3 mode (`GridMatchCompat::lattice_site_by_coordinates`). Tests `a105_no_site_matched_twice_in_default_mode`, `grid_hooks_identical_default`, `grid_hooks_identical_xmalab3`. No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: needs the independent review. Engine switch (package "engcal"): `CppCompat::grid_sites_compared_by_coordinates`. |
| A106 | Grid matching: the references are rescaled onto the matched beads' bounding box by `(max - min)` of the beads over `(max - min)` of the lattice sites, which divides by zero when only one column or row (or only the centre bead) was matched: references NaN or infinite, and every fit, map and undistorted point after them. | `processing/LocalUndistortion.cpp:234-241` (`docs/cpp-calibration.md` CD3) | Read in the source; not reached by the calibration runs | Fix, compat | Default: `match_grid` returns `GridMatchError::DegenerateLattice` (the engine reports it and keeps the grid uncomputed). XMALab 3 mode (`GridMatchCompat::unchecked_reference_scale`): the C++ division, NaN or infinite references. Test `a106_single_column_lattice`. Engine switch (package "engcal"): `CppCompat::grid_reference_scale_unchecked`. |
| A107 | "Remove outlier at border" **sets** every grid point's inlier flag from the circle test before the border test, so points the user toggled out by hand inside the circle become inliers again (the user's edits are lost). | `core/UndistortionObject.cpp:183-188` (`docs/cpp-calibration.md` CD4) | Seen in the calibration run `c01_undistortion`: the two points of camera 0 toggled out at lines 6 and 7 are inliers again in `u5_outliers_removed` (3001 inliers; 2999 with the fix) | Fix, compat (confirmed by the author, 16 November 2026) | Default: the circle test only clears flags. XMALab 3 mode (`OutlierCompat::circle_sets_inliers`). Changes the reference run from the removal on: `u5_outliers_removed` differs in those two flags, `u6_recomputed` is fitted without the two points (maps up to 0.40 px, transformed samples up to 0.31 px, undistorted grid points up to 0.075 px in camera 0). Tests `a107_manual_outliers_survive_removal` (`grid_match.rs`), `c01_from_images_default_differs_only_by_a107` and `c01_from_images_identical_xmalab3` (`crates/xma-engine/tests/undistortion_compute.rs`). Engine switch (package "engcal"): `CppCompat::outlier_circle_sets_inliers`. Engine test `calibration_undistortion.rs` `a107_remove_outliers_keeps_manual_outliers`; the run c01 replays identically in the XMALab 3 mode. Decided by the author, 16 November 2026: confirmed; points toggled out by hand stay out in the default mode. |
| A108 | "Remove outlier at border": the circle test is `d^2 <= (r - 1.5 t)^2` with `r` the bright region's enclosing radius and `t` the circle threshold; when `1.5 t > r` the radius is negative and its square grows again with `t`, so a larger threshold keeps more points. | `core/UndistortionObject.cpp:183-188` (`docs/cpp-calibration.md` CD4) | Read in the source; not reached by the calibration runs (radius above 1.5 x 25) | Fix, compat | Default: a radius `r - 1.5 t` of 0 or less keeps no point. XMALab 3 mode (`OutlierCompat::circle_radius_signed`). Test `a108_negative_circle_radius`. The empty case is defined: with no pixel above 100, OpenCV's `minEnclosingCircle` returns centre (0, 0) and radius 0 (it initialises both; `docs/cpp-calibration.md` section 13's open question), reproduced in both modes. Engine switch (package "engcal"): `CppCompat::outlier_circle_radius_signed`. |
| A109 | Grid matching with fewer than seven detected beads: with none, `center[0]` is read from an empty vector; with one to six, `findNClosestPoint(6, ..., skipfirst)` reads the sorted index array past its end. Test `too_few_beads_is_refused_in_both_modes`. |
| A110 | Cube calibration counts a reference as clicked when its **undistorted** position has `x > 0 && y > 0`, and undistorts the unset references (-1, -1) too. A click whose undistorted position is at or left of / above 0 is dropped (three clicks become two: no search, the frame fails); with a lens model an unset reference can undistort to a positive position and be used as a click of its point. The button counts raw clicks with `x >= 0 && y >= 0`. (Survey `docs/cpp-calibration.md` CD33.) | `processing/CubeCalibration.cpp:64-67,152-158,206-212,273-276`; `ui/WizardCalibrationCubeFrame.cpp:607-610` | Confirmed (source); no reference run reaches it (every recorded click is set and positive) | Fix, compat | Default: a reference is used exactly when it was set (`CubeReference::set`, the button's raw test), whatever its undistorted position. XMALab 3 mode (`CubeCompat::reference_from_undistorted_position`): the undistorted test, the unset references' undistorted positions included. `xma_vision::cube::references_used`. Tests `cube_fixes.rs` `a110_references_used_fixed_and_xmalab3`, `a110_unset_reference_with_positive_position` (c05 camera 0 with the unset reference moved to a positive position: XMALab 3 draws four clicks in every hypothesis, the default three); XMALab 3 unchanged: `cube.rs` (every run). No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. Engine switch (package "engcal"): `CppCompat::cube_reference_from_undistorted_position`. |
| A111 | `refineResults` marks the camera calibrated (`calibrated`) and the pose computed (`poseComputed`) after its loop whatever its final refit achieved. The final `calibrateFromInliers` can be refused by `calibrateOpenCV` (8 or fewer inliers) or rejected by OpenCV, leaving as camera matrix the decomposition of the last DLT (clamped, skew 0; with fewer than 6 inliers the DLT of an earlier round), with the pose and projections of the previous refit, written back as a calibrated camera. In the pose modes a final EPnP not run (fewer than 5 inliers) keeps the previous pose, written back as computed. The inlier count can also have dropped to anything, 0 included. (Survey CD15.) | `processing/CubeCalibration.cpp:527-571`, `:339`, `:405-409`, `:655`, `:683` | Confirmed: `OutlierThresholdForCalibration` 0 on the c02 and c06 inputs gives 0 inliers and a result written back as calibrated (camera) or computed (pose); a rejected final `calibrateCamera` gives the DLT decomposition as camera matrix | Fix, compat | Default: the result fails (the frame is marked -1, nothing written back; projected and detected lists cleared as in the C++'s failure branch) when the final refit does not run or is rejected: `calibrateOpenCV` returning false, or EPnP not run or refused. XMALab 3 mode (`CubeCompat::success_after_failed_refit`). On every recorded calibration the final refit succeeds and both modes agree (`cube_fixes.rs` `a111_unchanged_when_the_refit_succeeds`, and the pose case in `a111_final_refit_without_inliers_pose`). Review 4 (synthetic cube, not a defect): a six-point search shortened to 1,000 iterations can report success with a wrong camera, while 5,000 iterations and XMALab's 100,000 never did. Engine switch: `CppCompat::cube_success_after_failed_refit`. |

| A123 | Multi-camera optimisation, the generated Jacobians. (a) Method 1 (`jacFuncNoDistortionSeperateDimensions.h`) never assigns six entries of `A0[2][16]` (x by ty, fy, cy; y by tx, fx, cx), which `projErrorJac` then copies into the Jacobian uninitialised. (b) Methods 0 and 2 multiply by a coordinate difference's sign written `d / fabs(d)` (`t132`, `t144`; `t173`, `t214`): when that difference is exactly 0 it is 0/0 = NaN and the whole row is NaN, although the derivative of the distance exists there; levmar then accepts no step (stop 5) and the C++ writes the unchanged parameters back. | `processing/optimizationFuncs/jacFuncNoDistortionSeperateDimensions.h:282-307`, `jacFuncNoDistortion.h:131`, `:146`, `jacFuncWithDistortion.h:172`, `:215`; `processing/MultiCameraCalibration.cpp:164-189` | Confirmed (source); (a) the reference run of method 1 is reproduced bit for bit with zeros there; (b) not reached by the references, reached by synthetic data | Fix (a, both modes); Fix, compat (b) | (a) Undefined behaviour is not reproduced: the entries are 0, their true value, in both modes. (b) Default: the sign of an exact 0 is 0, which gives the derivative (the sign only ever multiplies `fabs(d)`); elsewhere the same division. XMALab 3 mode (`MulticamCompat::jacobian_nan_at_zero_difference`): NaN. A residual that is exactly 0 in both coordinates still has no derivative (the distance residual, a method choice). Tests `multicam_jacobian.rs` `a123_unassigned_separate_entries_are_zero`, `a123_zero_coordinate_difference` (four exact-zero points: NaN in XMALab 3, central differences to 5e-7 fixed), `multicam_synthetic.rs` `a123_run_with_a_zero_difference`. Review 4 (method choice, not a defect): the distance-residual methods (0 and 2) converge slowly: on exact synthetic data, after 300 iterations the error is under 1 % of the start but the intrinsics are still up to 21.5 px off, where method 1 (x and y residuals) recovers them to 1e-11. Engine switch: `CppCompat::multicam_jacobian_nan_at_zero_difference`. |
| A124 | Multi-camera optimisation, method 2: the new coefficients are applied (`setDistortionCoefficiants`, which builds the lens maps and undistorts every calibration image's points) before `setCameraMatrix` sets the new camera matrix, which rebuilds nothing; the maps and undistorted points use the old camera matrix until the next lens change. | `processing/MultiCameraCalibration.cpp:732`, `:739`; `core/Camera.cpp:350-367` (CD20) | Confirmed (source) | Fix, compat | Default: the new camera matrix. XMALab 3 mode (`MulticamCompat::lens_maps_with_previous_camera_matrix`): the old one. The result says which (`CameraUpdate::lens_maps_camera_matrix`); the engine builds the maps and undistorts with it. On `c08_optimize_m2` fx changes by 2.5e-3 px and cx by 6e-4 px. Tests `multicam_synthetic.rs` `a124_a125_method2_write_back`, `multicam_reference.rs` `c08_fixed_mode_same_numbers`. Engine switch (package engopt): `CppCompat::multicam_lens_maps_with_previous_camera_matrix`. The engine (package engopt) keeps the old matrix in `DerivedCamera::lens_points_camera_matrix` for the calibration images' undistorted detected points and the lens maps, until the camera changes again; `c08_optimize_m2` is identical with it, test `optimize.rs` `a124_a125_method2` (both modes). |

Engine switch (package engopt): `CppCompat::multicam_keeps_rational_coefficients`. |

| A127 | Multi-camera optimisation: the parameters are written back whatever levmar did. When it refuses (fewer residuals than parameters: few inliers) the initial values are written, the lens model of methods 0 and 1 is switched off and the cameras marked optimised although nothing was optimised; non-finite parameters are written as they are. | `processing/MultiCameraCalibration.cpp:801`, `:706-789` | Confirmed (source); not reached by the references | Fix, compat | Default: `optimize` returns `MulticamError::SolverRefused` or `NotFinite` and nothing is written. A run that stops for a singular matrix or invalid values (stops 4, 7) after accepting a step keeps its last accepted, finite parameters and is written in both modes; one that accepted no step has only the start values, and the default returns `MulticamError::NothingOptimised` (review 4 measured this case: a NaN detection among the inliers made levmar stop at its first evaluation, stop 7, and the default wrote the start values back as optimised). A non-finite input the residuals read (an inlier detection, a calibration-object point, a starting parameter) is refused before the solver runs (`MulticamError::NonFiniteInput`, naming it). A run that accepts no step and stops on a small gradient, a small error or no further reduction (stops 1, 6, 5) is written: the start is then the minimum levmar found (XMALab 3's NaN Jacobian of A123 alone gives stop 5 without a step and is written, as the switch's own test expects). XMALab 3 mode (`MulticamCompat::failure_written_back`): written back (`MulticamResult::info` is `None` after a refusal). Tests `a127_refusal_writes_nothing_when_fixed`, `a127_non_finite_detection` (`multicam_synthetic.rs`), `review4_multicam.rs` `nan_detection`, `too_few_observations`. Engine switch: `CppCompat::multicam_failure_written_back`. |
