| A157 | `cv::calibrateCamera` with a NaN or infinite object point, image point, camera-matrix entry or starting coefficient: OpenCV's checks (`fx <= 0`, the principal point outside the image) are comparisons that NaN passes, so it runs on and returns NaN values without throwing; XMALab would store them as the camera. The cube calibration can pass a NaN camera matrix (the decomposition of a degenerate DLT, whose `C(2,2)` can be 0). Review 4: `Ok` with NaN values in 20 of 22 such calls. | OpenCV 4.13 `modules/calib3d/src/calibration.cpp`; `processing/CubeCalibration.cpp:363-421` | Review 4 (synthetic); package fixcal1 | Fix (both modes) | `xma_vision::calib3d::calibrate_camera` returns `CalibrateError::Rejected` with a sentence naming the value ("object point 5 of view 0 is not finite (NaN or infinite)") for a non-finite input, checked before OpenCV's computation, and ("the result is not finite: ...") for a non-finite RMS, camera-matrix entry, coefficient or pose. The cube calibration treats it as OpenCV's exception (the frame fails). The 92 recorded calls and the 84 probe cases have finite inputs and are unchanged (`calib3d_runs.rs`, `calib3d_primitives.rs`). Tests `review4_calib3d.rs` `nan_and_infinite_inputs`, `calib3d_primitives.rs` `a157_non_finite_inputs_rejected`. |
| A158 | Multi-camera optimisation: levmar refuses only when the problem as a whole has fewer residuals than parameters (A127), so a camera, or a listed frame's object pose, with fewer residuals than the parameters it adds is optimised and written back although its data do not determine it. Review 4: a camera with 4 inlier points in one frame (method 1: 8 residuals for its 10 parameters) was written back as optimised, 6.1 px off in its intrinsics. | `processing/MultiCameraCalibration.cpp:264-624`, `:801` | Review 4 (synthetic); package fixcal1 | Fix, compat | Default: `optimize` refuses, before the solver runs and when the problem as a whole has at least as many residuals as parameters (otherwise it is levmar's refusal, A127), with `MulticamError::CameraUnderdetermined` ("camera 2 has too few inlier points for the optimisation: 8 residuals for its 10 parameters") or `FrameUnderdetermined`. The counting rule (`multicam::check_determined`): a camera's residuals are its observations (the inlier points of its calibrated images in the listed frames) times the residuals per observation (2 for method 1, else 1), and must be at least its parameters (10, or 15 for method 2); a listed frame's residuals are the observations of every camera in that frame times the same step, and must be at least 6. Equal counts are accepted. The reference runs (two cameras, one frame) pass. XMALab 3 mode (`MulticamCompat::underdetermined_written_back`). Tests `review4_multicam.rs` `camera_without_enough_observations`, `multicam_synthetic.rs` `a158_underdetermined_camera_and_frame`, `chain_through_a_reference_camera_same_in_both_modes` (method 2 there is refused by default: camera 3 has 12 residuals for 15 parameters). |
| A159 | Undo and redo of the calibration actions (XMALab 3 has no undo for them, U52): an undo step held the project, the cameras' derived state and three session flags, and was recorded only when the project changed. Review 5: after Delete Frame and Undo the frames were back but the session's calibration-frame count stayed lower, so a second Delete Frame was refused and the optimisation read the wrong count; a grid refit after a toggled outlier, "use camera distortion model" switched on when it was on, and a removal of outliers that cleared no flag recorded no step, so undo then also undid the edit before. | No C++ counterpart | Review 5 (`review5_undo.rs`); package fixcal2 (15 November 2026) | Fix (both modes; not a number of XMALab) | An undo step holds the project and what `Computed` holds besides it: the derived cameras (model, lens-points matrix, lookup table, held point lists) and the session values an edit writes and a later edit reads: `undistorted`, `camera_recalibration`, `grid_recalibration`, `n_calibration_images`, `active_camera`, `active_frame_calibration`, and the XMALab 3 mode's generator `rng` (`Session` lists them). Not held: the wizard's input, which the window sets between edits and every `setDialog` clears or reloads: the reference clicks and `next_reference` ("compute calibration" reads and clears them; its undo leaves them cleared, as the window's test `calibration_ui.rs` `undo_and_redo_of_a_calibration` requires), `manual_point`, manual mode; and the workspace and the wizard settings. A step is recorded when the project (`same_project`) or that state (`Computed::same_as`: derived cameras compared by value with shared parts skipped, NaN equal to NaN) differs from the step's start; undo gives the state just before the edit, redo the state just after. A recorded step moves `Document::changes()` and gives a new revision, so a step that changes only derived or session state marks the document modified (its revision is not the saved one; undo back to the saved revision clears it): the saved file would be the same, but the session differs from the reopened file (pending flags, a refitted grid) and a later save from the Undistortion or Calibration workspace asks the pending-changes question first. An edit that fails or changes neither records nothing and leaves `changes()` and the revision as they were (A97: `edit_history.rs`, `merge_monitor_state.rs`, `review3_merge.rs` unchanged). Tests `review5_undo.rs` (all), `fixcal2.rs` `a159_derived_only_step_marks_modified`, `a159_no_effect_edit_records_nothing`, `a159_undo_restores_active_image_not_clicks`, `a159_undo_restores_the_generator_xmalab3`. No C++ reference: independent review pending. |
| A160 | A click or prediction whose window the C++ computes in `int` with overflow: Alt + click snapping (`DetectionMethodForCalibration`), detection at a stored point, the tracker step and the polynomial refinement take `(int)(centre - half + 0.5)`, add the window size, and `getRectSubPix` takes `cvFloor` of the `float` centre. For a NaN or infinite centre, or one beyond about +-2^31, that is undefined behaviour. Review 5: the port panicked ("attempt to add with overflow" in `get_sub_image_at`) for an infinite click and for a finite one of 2^31 or more. | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:111-112`, `:474-475`; `processing/MarkerTracking.cpp` (`trackMarker_thread`); `core/Image.cpp` (`getSubImage`); OpenCV `imgproc/src/samplers.cpp` | Review 5 (`review5_fixes.rs` `a130_non_finite_click`); package fixcal2 | Fix (both modes) | Where the window's `int` arithmetic is defined nothing changes, bit for bit: the range test (`subpix::window_in_int_range`, `detection::window_fits`; the origin, the far edge `off + 2 size + 1` and `cvFloor` of the `f32` origin within `int`, that origin above `INT_MIN`, A169) is true for every window whose `f32` origin lies above -2^31 and whose far edge `origin + 2 size + 1` is at most 2^31 - 1 (review 6: the earlier statement "within about 2^31 - 2 size - 128 of 0" was not the condition coded; sizes are also bounded by A167 and A168), and the computations on those centres are the ones before (the sum `off + size` in `i64` equals the `int` sum; `getRectSubPix`'s origin is only held to +-2^40 beyond that range). Elsewhere "nothing found": `detect_point` and its variants return the input centre with size -1, `track_step*` `None` (the point stays at the prediction), the refinement `None` (as before, A66); `get_rect_sub_pix` and `get_sub_image*` no longer panic for any `f64` or `f32` centre (the window is the replicated edge), and `cut_template*` for any centre and any marker size (review 6 found that an infinite or huge marker size still panicked or asked for terabytes; corrected by A167). Snapping (`Document::snap_calibration_click_in`): such a click comes back as given in both modes (no rounding, no round trip through the distorted image); in the default mode a click the detection leaves where it was (nothing found) also comes back as given, where before the undistorted view returned it moved into the distorted image and back (a difference of rounding). Checked with NaN, +-infinity, +-2^31, +-1e300, `f64::MAX`/`MIN` on: snapping (both views, both modes, methods 1 to 6 through `detect_point`), `detect_point`, `detect_point_u16`, `detect_point_preview` (all six methods, both modes), `track_step_with`, `track_step_u16_with`, `cut_template`, `cut_template_u16`, `refine_point_polynomial_fit(_u16)` (centre and radius), `get_sub_image_at(_32f)` at `i32::MIN` and `i32::MAX`. In-range results unchanged: every detection, tracking, refinement and calibration reference test passes unchanged (`detection_point.rs`, `detection_refine*.rs`, `tracking_parity.rs`, `tracking_merge.rs`, `calibration_parity*.rs` with run c04's snapped clicks). Tests `a160_out_of_range.rs` (xma-vision), `review5_fixes.rs` `a130_non_finite_click`, `fixcal2.rs` `a160_huge_click_comes_back_as_given`, `a160_nothing_found_comes_back_as_given`. No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. |
| A161 | Undistortion: a matching or refit whose inliers cannot give a grid model. With fewer inliers than `LocalUndistortionNeighbours` (default 12), or 0 neighbours, the C++ fit reads past its arrays (A44); with no inlier at all it is defined (empty matrices: every point maps to itself, the grid marked computed). The port treated both as "no model" and accepted them. Review 5: a grid image of 7 beads in one line was accepted, 4 beads matched, the grid marked computed and the session undistorted, but no model fitted, so the camera had no grid correction (8 such beads are refused by A106). | `processing/LocalUndistortion.cpp:113-122`, `:247-252`, `:301-305` | Review 5 (`review5_grid.rs` `seven_collinear_beads_refused_or_fitted`); package fixcal2 | Fix, compat | Default: "compute undistortion", "recompute undistortion" and the pending-changes "Yes" are refused (`EditError::Rejected`) when a grid's matching or refit gives no model, and nothing changes: "<camera>: the undistortion grid has <n> inlier points, fewer than the <k> neighbours (LocalUndistortionNeighbours) each point of the fit needs, so no undistortion can be fitted." or "<camera>: the undistortion grid has no inlier point, so no undistortion can be fitted." XMALab 3 mode: the same refusal where the C++ is undefined (1 to k - 1 inliers, or 0 neighbours); a refit with no inlier is applied as XMALab does (no model, grid computed; `CppCompat::grid_fit_without_inliers_accepted`). A grid that has a model is unchanged (every undistortion reference run passes). The load refit (C12) of a stored grid is not an edit and is not refused. Tests `review5_grid.rs` `seven_collinear_beads_refused_or_fitted`, `fixcal2.rs` `a161_seven_collinear_beads_refused_in_both_modes`, `a161_refit_without_enough_inliers` (both modes). No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. |
| A162 | External calibration, "APPLY CHANGES": any rotation matrix is accepted and `cv::Rodrigues` stores a rotation made from it (the nearest rotation for a matrix with positive determinant; for a reflection, a singular or a zero matrix one up to 1.5 per entry away from it, review 5), as are a width or height of 0 and a camera matrix with a focal length of 0 or less (a zero camera matrix). | `ui/ExternalCalibrationFrame.cpp:115-182`; OpenCV `calib3d/src/calibration.cpp` (`cvRodrigues2`) | Review 5 (`review5_external.rs` `apply_rotation_that_is_not_a_rotation`, `apply_degenerate_sizes_and_matrix`); package fixcal2 | Fix, compat | Default: refused with a sentence and nothing changes: a width or height of 0 ("The image size of the external calibration must be at least 1 x 1 pixels ..."), `fx` or `fy` of 0 or less ("The focal lengths of the camera matrix must be positive ..."), a rotation matrix whose determinant is not positive ("... is not a rotation: its determinant is not positive.") or that differs from the nearest rotation (`U Vt` of its SVD, `xma_vision::linalg`) by more than 1e-4 in an entry (`external::ROTATION_TOLERANCE`, `distance_from_rotation`; a matrix written with 4 or more decimals is within it). Checked after the non-finite test (A150) and before the spin-box rounding (A148). XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::external_calibration_accepts_degenerate`): applied as now. The reviewer's tests `apply_rotation_that_is_not_a_rotation` and `apply_degenerate_sizes_and_matrix` expect the default to accept these values and fail with this rule (left unchanged for a later decision). Tests `fixcal2.rs` `a162_external_values_checked` (both modes). No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. |
| A163 | Cube calibration, the session and the reopened project: after a calibration the session holds the cube's undistorted detected beads (from which the distorted ones stored in the project were computed through the grid's inverse), while a reopened project undistorts the stored distorted beads again: a round trip through the grid. Review 5 on the pig data: the undistorted detected points differ by up to 9.9e-7 px, their errors by up to 1.07e-6 px. XMALab 3 has the same property (the session keeps `detectedPointsUndistorted`, the load recomputes them). | `processing/CubeCalibration.cpp` (`computePoseAndCam_threadFinished`), `core/CalibrationImage.cpp` (`undistortPoints`) | Review 5 (`review5_save.rs` `save_and_reopen_every_stage`, bound `HELD_ROUND_TRIP` 1.1e-6); package fixcal2 | Accept | Nothing that is stored differs; the camera, the poses and the inliers are the ones the calibration computed from the session's points. They could be made equal without changing what is stored, by replacing the held undistorted points and errors with those recomputed from the stored distorted points after each calibration (as `held::undistort_points` does after loading); that would make the session's views differ from XMALab's session by the same 1e-6 px, and is not done. |
| A164 | Manual mode (C25): `reloadManualPoints` gives the active calibration image `n` unset points (-1, -1) and `n` inlier flags 0 (`CalibrationImage::init`) when it has fewer than the calibration object's `n`, from the "manual mode" check box, a click on another image or camera in manual mode, and every `setDialog`. The port wrote those lists into the project there, outside any undo step: the project changed while `Document::changes()`, the revision and the modified flag stayed put (review 6, `review6_history.rs` `review6_manual_mode_changes_the_project_without_moving_changes`). XMALab 3 has no undo (U52); the lists reach the file only once the image is calibrated. | `ui/WizardCalibrationCubeFrame.cpp` (`on_checkBoxManual_clicked`, `reloadManualPoints`, `setDialog`), `core/CalibrationImage.cpp` (`init`) | Review 6; package fixcal3 (15 November 2026) | Fix (both modes; not a number of XMALab) | Rule: no function changes the project outside the history. Outside a step, `reloadManualPoints` records the image in `Session::manual_lists_pending` (wizard input, not undone) and leaves the project as it is; `Document::calibration_image_lists` gives the lists as XMALab holds them, for the views (the window's manual rows still read the project, so they show an empty position where XMALab shows "-1 / -1" until the first manual edit; left to the window). Every calibration edit first creates the pending lists inside its own step (`calibration::apply`), so it reads what XMALab's reads; if the edit fails, or succeeds without changing anything else, they are not created after all and stay pending, so such an edit still records nothing. Inside a step (a calibration edit calling `setDialog`) they are created at once, as before. `changes()` and the modified flag move only with the step that creates them. Checked: switching manual mode on, clicking through images of both cameras and switching it off leaves the project, `changes()`, the revision and the undo stack as they were, and the project saved afterwards equals the one saved before byte for byte (both modes); a manual point creates the lists in its own undo step; the XMALab 3 replay c07 (manual mode) is unchanged (`calibration_parity_edits.rs`). Tests `fixcal3.rs` `a164_manual_mode_changes_nothing_and_saves_the_same_bytes`, `a164_manual_point_creates_the_lists_in_its_step`, `a164_edit_without_effect_leaves_the_lists_pending`. Review 7 (package fix5, 15 November 2026): an undo or redo that changed the active image (the undo of the manual point that created its lists, for one) left it with no lists and not pending, so the window read 0 points where XMALab holds `n`. Rule now: whenever manual mode is on, the active calibration image has its lists in the project or is pending after every edit, undo, redo and change of active camera or image; after each committed or taken-back step, undo and redo, images that no longer exist or no longer need their lists leave the pending set and, in manual mode, the active image is given its lists again (pending) when it has fewer (`calibration::all_frames::sync_manual_lists`, called from `history.rs` `restore` and `commit_step`; session only, both modes). Tests `review7_manual.rs` (random scripts of manual mode, image changes, every calibration edit, undo, redo, save and reopen), `fix5_manual.rs` `a164_undo_and_redo_keep_the_active_image_lists_in_both_modes`. No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. |
| A165 | External calibration, "APPLY CHANGES": `cv::Rodrigues` turns the rotation matrix into the stored rotation vector; its branches for `sin(theta) < 1e-5` store a rotation within about 1e-5 rad of 0 as none and one within about 1e-5 rad of 180 degrees with an axis from the diagonal, so the stored rotation differs from the matrix given by up to 2.1e-5 in an entry (review 6: a rotation by 1e-5 rad is stored as the identity). | `ui/ExternalCalibrationFrame.cpp:115-182`; OpenCV `calib3d/src/calibration_base.cpp` (`cv::Rodrigues`) | Review 6 (`review6_external.rs` `review6_a162_every_axis_and_angle`); package fixcal3 | Fix, compat | Default: the rotation vector of the nearest rotation (`U Vt` of the SVD, `xma_vision::linalg`), through its unit quaternion (Shepperd's method) and `theta = 2 atan2(|v|, w)`: `xma_vision::camera::nearest_rotation_to_rodrigues`, beside the port `matrix_to_rodrigues`, which is unchanged. For every axis and angle the stored rotation gives back the matrix to 7.8e-16 (reviewer's test, 245 rotations; bound asserted 1e-9). XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::external_rotation_cv_rodrigues`): `cv::Rodrigues` as before (bit for bit, `fixcal3.rs`). Left as XMALab has them, with the ported routine: reading a project's stored rotation matrices (`xma-io` `project_read`, F21), the cube calibration's pose (`xma_vision::cube`), `solvePnP`'s and EPnP's initial and final poses (`calib3d::extrinsic`, `epnp`), the multi-camera optimisation (`multicam`), the rigid-body poses (`rigid`, `pose_refine`). The effect there is the same, and only for rotations within about 1e-5 rad of 0 or 180 degrees: at most 1.0e-5 per matrix entry near 0 and 2.1e-5 near 180 degrees (`fixcal3_bounds.rs` `a165_nearest_rotation_to_rodrigues` measures 1.0e-5 over its angles), nothing elsewhere. Tests `fixcal3.rs` `a165_external_rotation_near_0_and_180_degrees` (both modes), `fixcal3_bounds.rs` `a165_nearest_rotation_to_rodrigues`. No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. |
| A166 | Undo of the calibration actions (A159): an undo step held the cameras' derived state as it was, including the grid lookup table (`undistortionMapX/Y`, 8 MiB per camera on the pig data) and the grid model (0.8 MiB, with its cell index), so every refit kept the previous ones. Review 6: 923.5 MiB of history after 200 calibration edits on the pig data (100 outlier toggles and 100 refits), about 4.6 MiB per step. | No C++ counterpart (XMALab 3 has no undo, U52) | Review 6 (`review6_undo_memory.rs`); package fixcal3 | Fix (both modes; not a number of XMALab) | A step on the undo or redo stack keeps no lookup table and no grid model that the document does not hold now (`Computed::drop_lookup_tables`, run after every commit, undo and redo; the steps that share a camera share one copy without them). The table is a function of the model and the image size; the model is a function of the stored grid points it was fitted from and the number of neighbours, which the camera's derived state now records (`DerivedCamera::grid_source`: the project's camera as the fit left it, shared with the project, and `LocalUndistortionNeighbours`). Restoring a step fits the model again (about 110 ms per camera) and makes the table again (about 180 ms), unless the document's camera has the same source or model, whose model and table are then taken: bit for bit what the step held (`review6_history.rs`, `review5_undo.rs` and `fixcal3.rs` `a166_undo_of_a_refit_gives_back_model_and_table` compare the models and the tables' bits). What a user sees: an undo or redo of a grid refit takes about 0.3 s longer per camera; the views show the restored table at once. A refit of a computed grid no longer copies the project's camera (it writes nothing there). The cell index of a grid transform now prints as `LwmIndex(..)` (`Debug`), as it compares (`==`): a cache, not part of the value. Measured with a counting allocator (`fixcal3_undo_memory.rs`, asserted below 50 MiB, 50 MiB and 5 MiB): 200 calibration edits 923.5 MiB before, 31.2 MiB now; 200 point edits on the pig trial 15.1 MiB (unchanged: about 77 KiB per step, the edited marker and the recomputed trial data); a 145-frame tracking run of all markers from `first_only.xma` 0.28 MiB (one step). The held point lists are shared per image (`Arc`) and copied only where an edit writes them. |
| A167 | Windows and templates of any size: `getSubImage` and `getRectSubPix` allocate a `(2 size + 1)^2` window for any `int` size, whatever the image (a detection with a search area of 4096 on a 1 x 1 image asks for 67 MB, one of 2^20 for 4 TB; the template of a marker size of 10^6 for terabytes; a size of `INT_MAX` overflows `2 size + 1`). Review 6: "capacity overflow" panics in `get_sub_image*` for a size of `i32::MAX`, an allocation above 256 MiB for a search area of 4096 or more, an overflow in `cut_template` for a marker size of infinity, 2^31 or `f64::MAX` (`size + 3`). | `core/Image.cpp` (`getSubImage`); OpenCV `imgproc/src/samplers.cpp`; `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:111-116`; `processing/MarkerTracking.cpp:124-161` | Review 6 (`review6_a160.rs` `review6_a160_cut_template_marker_size`, `review6_a160_sizes_and_parameters`); package fixcal3 | Fix (both modes) | A window's half-width is at most `max(L, 1024)`, `L` the image's larger side (`subpix::max_window_half`, `WINDOW_HALF_FLOOR`); beyond it nothing is cut: `get_rect_sub_pix(_32f)` (either side above `2 max(L, 1024) + 1`) and `get_sub_image*` return an empty (0 x 0) image, `cut_template(_u16)` an empty template (`size + 3` saturating), `track_step*` `None` for an empty template or search region, and `detect_point` and its variants "nothing found" for a search area beyond it. The largest window is then `(2 max(L, 1024) + 1)^2` values (16.8 MB as `f32` for an image up to 1024 pixels). In-range inputs give what they gave, bit for bit: 42,600 random cases with search areas up to 120, marker sizes up to 25, sub-images up to 200 and offsets up to +-2e9 compared with the code before the change (`detect_point(_u16)`, `detect_point_preview(_u16)`, `refine_point_polynomial_fit`, `cut_template(_u16)`, `track_step_surfaces(_u16)_with`, `get_sub_image(_at)`, `get_rect_sub_pix`, `select_contour`, `window_in_int_range`, `window_fits`: 0 differ), and every detection and tracking reference test passes unchanged. What the engine passes is far inside: search areas of 30, 40, twice the mean marker size (at most 100: only sizes from 1 to 50 are counted, A1) or `max(12, 6 r + 6)`; marker sizes at most 50; so no project file or setting reached these. Tests `review6_a160.rs` (all), `fixcal3_bounds.rs` `a167_window_bound`. No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. |
| A168 | Other `int` parameters of detection and tracking: the background blur's radius `(int)(1.5 masksize + 0.5)` overflows for a mask size beyond about +-1.43e9 and its kernel `2 radius + 1` beyond about 7.2e8, and a kernel of `2 radius + 1` taps is allocated and convolved for any radius (a mask size of 10^8: 2.4 GB and hours); `100 - maxPenalty` overflows for a penalty of `INT_MIN + 100` or less; `findContours` adds the window offset (and -1 for its border) to every point. Review 6: overflow panics for a mask size of `i32::MAX` (`detect_point`, `detection_image_stages`), a penalty of `i32::MIN` (`track_step*`), a contour offset of `i32::MAX` (`select_contour`). | `processing/MarkerDetection.cpp:144-150`, `:217-230`; `processing/MarkerTracking.cpp:171-215`; OpenCV `imgproc/src/contours_new.cpp` | Review 6 (`review6_a160.rs` `review6_a160_sizes_and_parameters`); package fixcal3 | Fix (both modes) | The blur is computed only when the radius is an `int` and at most `max(2 searchArea + 1, 4096)` (`detection::blur_radius_within_bound`, `BLUR_RADIUS_FLOOR`; the kernel is computed in `i64`, the same value for every radius within it, `max(1)` for a negative one as before); otherwise `detection_image_stages(_f32)` return `None` (they now return `Option`) and the detection finds nothing. `track_step*` give `None` when `100 - max_penalty` is not an `int` (the point stays at the prediction). `select_contour` gives no candidate when a point of the image plus the offset (less 1) is not an `int` (offset `INT_MIN`, or beyond `INT_MAX - width`). In-range inputs unchanged (A167's comparison). What a project file can carry: `TrackingPenalty` any `int` up to 100 (`setMaxPenalty` clamps only above), so a penalty of `INT_MIN + 100` or less panicked in a debug build and wrapped in a release build; `SizeOverride` any `int`, the detection's mask size, so an override of 715,827,882 or more overflowed the kernel size (debug panic) and one of about 10^8 asked for gigabytes; contour offsets of `INT_MIN` were reached by a stored point or click whose window origin is `INT_MIN` (A169). Tests `review6_a160.rs` `review6_a160_sizes_and_parameters`, `fixcal3_bounds.rs` `a168_parameter_edges`. No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. |
| A169 | The `int` range test of A160 (`subpix::window_in_int_range`) took `i64` origins but computed `off + 2 size + 1` itself in `i64`, which overflows for an origin near `i64::MAX`; and it accepted a `getRectSubPix` origin of `INT_MIN`, which OpenCV's `adjustRect` negates (`rect.x = -ip.x`, undefined). | OpenCV `imgproc/src/samplers.cpp` (`adjustRect`) | Review 6 (`review6_a160.rs` `review6_a160_window_in_int_range_i64`, and the edge named without a test); package fixcal3 | Fix (both modes) | The test computes with checked arithmetic (false for any origin or size it cannot add) and requires the `cvFloor` origin to be above `INT_MIN` (`centre_in_int_range` too). An origin that is now out of range finds nothing (A160). Near `-2^31` the `f32` origin is a multiple of 128, so the windows given up are those whose `f32` centre rounds to `-2^31 + size`: an origin of `-2^31` with size 40 is out, `-2^31 + 128` is in. In-range tests unchanged (A167's comparison: 3,000 random origins within +-2.147e9). Tests `review6_a160.rs` `review6_a160_window_in_int_range_i64`, `a160_out_of_range.rs` `int_range_bounds` (changed: the origin `INT_MIN` is now out), `fixcal3_bounds.rs` `a169_int_range_edges`. |
| A170 | `cv::findChessboardCorners` where OpenCV 4.13 throws: a pattern side of 2 or less (`StsOutOfRange`), an image whose smaller side is below 15 pixels once the adaptive-threshold loop is reached (its second block size, `cvRound(0.1 min(w, h)) \| 1`, is 1 and `adaptiveThreshold` asserts `blockSize > 1`; reference cases `chessboard-tiny-4x4`, `chessboard-tiny-12x9`), an empty image. XMALab runs the call in a `QtConcurrent` thread; the exception never reaches `detectCorner_threadFinished`, whose point list is empty, so nothing is stored. | OpenCV `calib3d/src/calibinit.cpp:665-666`, `imgproc/src/thresh.cpp` (`adaptiveThreshold`); `processing/CheckerboardDetection.cpp:91-141` | Probe of the 4.13 libraries (`oracle/py/chess_probe/`); package p6chess | Same effect (both modes) | `xma_vision::chessboard::find_chessboard_corners` returns `found == false` and no corners there (also where `cols * rows` does not fit OpenCV's `int`), which is what XMALab ends with. Reference case `chessboard-11x8-border8` (found false, 88 corners). | `processing/CheckerboardDetection.cpp:99-108`, `:133-134` | Probe of the 4.13 libraries; package p6chess | Fix proposed (default: store only when found; XMALab 3 mode as the C++); to be built where the detection is stored (engine) | The vision functions return both: `ChessboardCorners { found, corners }` from `find_chessboard_corners` / `detect_corners_xmalab` (which refines only when found, as the C++). | A172 | Planar (checkerboard) calibration, `Calibration::process` with `m_planar`: `cv::calibrateCamera` without `CALIB_USE_INTRINSIC_GUESS` reads neither the camera matrix nor the starting coefficients, but `calibrate_camera` refused a non-finite camera-matrix entry or coefficient before running (A157), and the planar call passes whatever `setInitialByReferences` (a DLT on a flat board) left in the matrix. Also: the planar calibrations of detected corners agree with OpenCV to rounding, not to the bit. | `processing/Calibration.cpp:120-125`, `:238-273`; OpenCV `calib3d/src/calibration.cpp:290-331` | Package p6chess (`chesscalib-*`, 7 cases, made by the 4.13 libraries on the corners they detected) | Fix (both modes) | The A157 check of the camera matrix and the coefficients now applies only with an intrinsic guess; without one they are ignored, as OpenCV ignores them (test `chessboard_calib.rs` `a172_planar_call_ignores_matrix_and_coefficients`; the A157 tests, all with a guess, unchanged). Measured on `chesscalib-*` (`chessboard_calib.rs`): lens model off, camera matrices to a relative 1.9e-11 and reprojections to 5.3e-10 px; rational model, reprojections to 6.6e-6 px with three views and to 0.011 px with five (RMS 0.0325021 against OpenCV's 0.0324670), the coefficients of the flat valley differing by up to 26. Causes: the homographies' eigenvectors (Eigen in the reference build) and, from 50 points per view or 25 free parameters, BLAS and LAPACK in the reference (A37); the rational model's eight coefficients are poorly determined by a flat board, so the 30 iterations end at different places. |

| A189 | Checkerboard, the board's numbering per camera: XMALab numbers the corners from the corner clicked (`sortGridByReference` keeps or reverses OpenCV's order, comparing the click with the first and last corners only), the page asks for "the interior upper left corner" in each camera, and OpenCV keeps its numbering's handedness in the image. Two cameras whose upper-left corners are different physical corners, a board turned about 90 degrees in one view, or a camera that sees the board from behind (an X-ray camera on the far side) number the same board differently, even frame by frame within one camera; their poses of the board then lie in different world frames (the board's frame turned about its centre). Each camera's own calibration is unaffected; everything that joins the cameras is wrong: "optimize camera setup", the 3D points and rigid-body poses of every trial (the cameras' poses at the trial's reference calibration frame) and the MayaCams export. | `core/CalibrationImage.cpp:784-830`; `ui/WizardCalibrationCubeFrame.cpp:539-542`; `processing/MultiCameraCalibration.cpp:264-624`; OpenCV `calib3d/src/calibinit.cpp` (`checkQuadGroup`, the row order by the cross product) | Reference runs k01, k07, k10 (`DATASETS.md`, "Board labelling"); rendered two-camera rigs (`checkerboard_numbering.rs`); synthetic rigs of two to four cameras (`review9_common`: `numbering_sweep.rs`, `numbering_sets.rs`, `review9_numbering_*.rs`; since the matching was removed the warning on three and four cameras, and on two groups with no common frame, is asserted by `review15b_numbering_multicam_warn.rs`) | Warn, compat. The author chose "fix it automatically in the default mode" (16 November 2026); **since 17 November 2026 the default mode only checks and warns**, because the automatic fix failed three independent checks (end of this row). **The author, 17 November 2026: "Keep the warning"**; the warning is the decision, not reopened without the author | **Reach, measured.** k01 and k07 (camera 2 clicked at its own upper-left corner, the board's opposite corner): the camera-2-from-camera-1 transform of the frame poses is 179.999 and 179.924 degrees from the truth after the calibrations; "optimize camera setup" then gives mean errors of 47.2 / 49.8 px (k01, method 0) and 39.0 / 39.7 px (k07, method 0; methods 1 and 2 leave 19.7 / 20.7 px with camera 1's focal length at 201 against 3207); k10 (the same physical corner clicked in both) 0.096 / 0.085 px. Rendered rig (`reach_of_the_fault_on_a_trial_point`, camera 2 rolled upside down): a trial's points triangulated with the calibrations land 11 to 605 mm from their true positions, reprojection errors 0.58 to 3308 px; after optimizing 351 mm, 281 px. With the numbering matched: 0.95 to 1.12 mm, at most 0.037 px; after optimizing 1.20 mm, 0.0065 px. **Fix (default mode)** (`xma_engine::calibration::numbering`, switch `checkerboard_numbering_per_camera`), run after every calibration of a board camera over its frames and again before "optimize camera setup". *Measure*: for a frame two cameras see, each candidate renumbering (the board's symmetries: kept, reversed, each row reversed, the rows reversed; for a square board also the quarter turns and the diagonal turns) of the second camera gives a camera-to-camera transform; the right one's is the physical transform and every other differs from it by a rotation of 90 or 180 degrees, however the board lies, so transforms are compared by the angle between their rotations (degrees). The translations are not used: a single camera's planar pose determines them worst (review 9: on X-ray-like rigs the correct candidate's transform scattered up to 0.105 of the board's diagonal against 0.40 for the wrong ones, and correct projects were refused under the former absolute threshold of 0.05). *Pairs*: two board cameras that see at least two frames; every frame and candidate seeds a consensus (each frame's nearest candidate, their mean rotation, three times); the consensus is the seed with the smallest median residual; a rival is a seed whose consensus is at least 20 degrees away, with its largest residual. Decided when the best rival's residual is at least 3 times the consensus's (RATIO) and at least 3 degrees (FLOOR_RIVAL); otherwise undecided. *Sets of cameras*: decided pairs join the board cameras into groups; a spanning tree from each group's lowest-index camera (each camera reached from the lowest-index camera already in the tree) gives every camera's rotation from the group's first. Each frame's numbering is that of the lowest-index camera of the group that sees it; every other camera of the group that sees it takes the candidate nearest the tree's transform when the second nearest is at least 20 degrees away (FLOOR) and 3 times as far (RATIO), otherwise the frame is undecided and left. Pairs outside the tree are thereby checked through it. The result depends only on the clicks: tests over all 6 orders of a three-camera chain and all 24 orders of a four-camera rig (frames numbered differently within cameras, the first and last camera sharing no frame), and four shuffled interleavings of single frames across cameras, give identical corners (`numbering_sets.rs`). *A frame one camera sees alone* is not renumbered: the board moves freely between frames, so nothing in a camera's own frames tells which physical corner each frame numbers first; it does not matter: its board pose is free in the optimisation and the symmetry gives every numbering the same residuals (test: kept or reversed, the optimised cameras agree to 0.05 % and the errors to 0.005 %, the rest being OpenCV's planar initialisation and the solvers' stopping, which are not symmetric under a renumbering). *Measured* (`numbering_sweep.rs`, 1200 cases: a light rig and X-ray-like rigs of focal length 3200 and 4800 px at 1200 mm; noise 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 1 px; 2, 3, 4, 6, 8 frames; boards 9 x 6, 10 x 8 of 5 and 10 mm, 7 x 7, 7 x 6; with and without unmodelled distortion; camera 2 kept, or reversed / a quarter turn with a minority of frames different): no frame renumbered to a wrong candidate; decided frames' nearest candidate at most 13.6 degrees (X-ray 3200, 1 px; 7.8 light, 1 px; 2.7 X-ray 4800, 0.5 px), second nearest at least 89.1 degrees (ratio at least 12.3 against 3); consensus residual at most 6.7 degrees, best rival at least 3.06 degrees and 3.06 times the residual (the pair rule is the binding one). Undecided, and refused at optimisation: 18 cases, all at 1 px of noise (light rig with 2 or 3 frames whose boards turn little; X-ray 3200 with the 10 x 8 board of 5 mm, 2 to 4 frames, consensus residual up to 13.4 degrees). Every case at 0.1 to 0.5 px is decided and optimises; review 9's X-ray cases (`correct_long_focal_project_is_refused`) are decided and optimise without a message. *Messages* (one per cause): a Console line for every renumbering ("Camera 2: the checkerboard's corner numbering in frames 1 and 2 was changed to agree with Camera 1.", not shown as a dialog); after a calibration, a message for cameras that cannot be related or frames not decided only once the pair is complete (every frame in which one of the two cameras has corners has them in the other: nothing left to compute could decide it), saying that the camera's calibration cannot be trusted together with the other's until it is resolved; no message in any of the 1200 cases while frames were still being computed. *Refused* ("optimize camera setup", default mode, after the matching): two board cameras that see frames in common but cannot be related (fewer than two frames, a board that turns too little, no agreement), an undecided frame, or cameras in groups that see no frame in common (XMALab's optimisation cannot place them either, A120: `CameraNotPlaced`), naming the groups. *Not found by the numbering*: images mirrored without the camera set flipped: the frames' rotations agree (the numbering is matched by rotation), their translations do not, and the optimisation's errors show it (test). A flipped camera (files mirrored, mirrored back on load) is consistent; a camera behind the board needs the turned-over candidates (tests). Renumbering moves the frame's detected points, inlier flags and held lists (undistorted points, projections, errors); `detectedPoints_ALL` (the detection's order, shown without numbers) is left; the camera is calibrated again in the same undo step. *Results, default mode*: k01 camera 2 from camera 1 within 0.147 degrees of the truth after the calibrations (fx 3207.23 / 3050.59, truth 3207.44 / 3042.64), after method 0 0.0858 / 0.0677 px (k10: 0.0961 / 0.0848); k07 within 0.254 degrees, after method 0 0.726 / 0.141 px (camera 1's strong lens distortion is taken off by method 0), after methods 1 and 2 0.216 / 0.042 px. Tests: `checkerboard_numbering.rs`, `numbering_sets.rs`, `numbering_sweep.rs`, `review9_numbering_*.rs`, `xma-cli` `checker_numbering.rs`. XMALab 3 mode: as XMALab; the ten checkerboard parity runs unchanged (A201's tolerance for the optimisation of these cameras applies to that mode). **Third version of the automatic matching, 16 and 17 November 2026** (review 10: the second version decided wrongly on degenerate board motions on mirror-symmetric rigs, 26 of 3,120 focused cases; kept a renumbering after its pair became undecided; cost quadratic in the frames). Built: a pair is decided only when every rival symmetry of the board is excluded by a frame at least 10 degrees (SPREAD_FLOOR) and 3 times that frame's noise (SPREAD_FACTOR, the noise from each camera's calibration covariance) away from the degenerate set, the smaller spread is at least 3 times the consensus's largest residual, and the camera-to-camera translations agree (TRANSLATION_FACTOR 3, floor 2 degrees); every matching starts from the frames as clicked (what was applied is kept with the derived cameras); seeds from the first, middle and last shared frames (linear cost). Measured in release builds, two cameras: review 10's degenerate families (4,224 cases) 0 wrongly decided; its boundary sweep (2,887 cases, 608 near the boundary) 0 wrongly decided, none decided near the boundary; few frames and many seeds (6,240 cases) 0 wrong at the end or at any step; 1,000 realistic X-ray projects: 962 decided and optimised, **38 correct projects refused** ("do not determine": boards of 10 x 8 at 5 mm and 9 x 6 at 10 mm with 4 to 8 frames, mostly at 0.5 px of noise). On the main tree, 17 November 2026, five tests failed: `mixed_numberings_subset` (review 10, three and four cameras with mixed numberings: **2 of 48 rigs had frames renumbered to a wrong candidate**, both X-ray rigs with a square board; the optimisation was refused in both, 3 more rigs refused, largest mean error of the 45 optimised 6.5 px); `realistic_xray_projects_subset` (2 of 24 correct projects refused); `numbering_rule_on_rigs` (two-frame cases at 0.3 px no longer decided); `board_that_does_not_move_is_left_with_a_message` (message text); `four_cameras_in_every_order` once (a rendered image missing from its temporary folder; passed when run again, cause not found). The remaining sweeps (multi-camera, the full rule sweep, the optimisation errors in both modes) were stopped unfinished and this version had no independent review. **Decision taken by the coordinator on 17 November 2026, announced to the author beforehand as the fallback ("if the next version doesn't hold up, the fallback is ... check ..., never renumber"): the programs do not use the automatic matching.** *What the programs do* (`crates/xma-engine/src/calibration/numbering.rs`, `warn_only`): the numberings are compared by the third version's analysis, read only; nothing is renumbered and nothing is refused, so every number is the default mode's without A189 (test: the cameras after the calibrations and the optimisation equal those with `checkerboard_numbering_per_camera` set, for cameras numbered alike and differently). A warning, shown as a dialog in the window and in the operation's messages in `xma calibrate`, names the camera, the camera it is compared with and the frames whose clear candidate is not the present numbering: after a calibration once every frame of the pair is computed, and at "optimize camera setup" ("Warning: Camera 2 numbers the checkerboard's corners differently from Camera 1 in frames 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6 (another corner was clicked there, or the camera sees the board from its other side). While they differ, "optimize camera setup" and the 3D points of trials are wrong. Detect the board again in those frames and click the same physical corner of the board in both cameras; if the camera setup was just optimized, undo it first."). Pairs or frames that cannot be decided give one Console line at "optimize camera setup" only ("The frames that Camera 2 and Camera 1 both see do not show whether the two cameras number the checkerboard alike. ..."). A wrong verdict can therefore only produce a wrong or missing warning, never a changed number or a refused step. Tests `crates/xma-engine/tests/numbering_warn.rs` (4). **The automatic matching was removed on 21 November 2026** (the author's decision 17: "remove it and its tests, after a commit"; it remains in the history at commit c7d10f0): the policy switch (`NumberingPolicy` and `Session::checkerboard_numbering`), the renumbering, the record of what it applied to each frame (kept with the derived cameras), its Console lines and its refusals of "optimize camera setup". The analysis that the warning reads is unchanged, and so is every message and every number in both modes. Its tests were removed or rewritten to test the warning: the rendered rigs of `checkerboard_numbering.rs` and the synthetic rigs of `review9_numbering_rigs.rs` now assert the warning (which frames it names, none when the cameras agree); `review10_common` and `numbering_sweep.rs` count wrong verdicts (a decided frame named when the cameras number it alike, or passed when they do not) instead of wrong renumberings. Everything above this paragraph about renumbering, refusals and "Results, default mode" describes the removed matching, not the programs. XMALab 3 mode: unchanged. Second round of review 11 (20 November 2026): an edit that recalibrates several cameras gives each warning once, checked after all of them (before, once per camera, on a half-updated rig; no calibration number differs, compared to the bit by an independent reviewer). A frame emptied by "Reset Frame", or given manual mode's fresh lists, no longer counts as having corners when a pair of cameras is tested for being complete, so the warning is not held back by such a frame (in all 34 checkerboard projects of the reference data a frame has a full list of corners exactly when it has detections). The two message texts lost runs of about twenty spaces left in mid-sentence by a formatting slip. |

| A191 | "Denoise Images" copies frame `f` when `f < w` or `f > N - w` (`w = (temporal - 1) / 2`) and denoises the others, so frame `N - w` is denoised with a temporal window reaching frame `N`, past the end. OpenCV refuses that with a `cv::Exception` ("imgToDenoiseIndex and temporalWindowSize should be chosen corresponding srcImgs size!") that nothing catches in the job: the camera's frames `N - w` to `N` are not written (CD29). With fewer than `2w + 1` frames every frame is copied. | `processing/DenoiseTrial.cpp:69-77`; OpenCV 4.13.0 `modules/photo/src/denoising.cpp` (`fastNlMeansDenoisingMultiCheckPreconditions`) | Read in the source; OpenCV's refusal reproduced through the XMALab 3 AppImage's library (`oracle/data/primitives/denoise`, case `refused_window_past_end`) | Fix, compat | `CppCompat::denoise_window_past_end`. Default: the last `w` frames are copied as the first `w` are (`f < w` or `f + w >= N`), so every frame is written. XMALab 3 mode: the camera's job stops at frame `N - w` with OpenCV's message (reported), the files of frames 1 to `N - w` written, and with "relink" the trial is relinked to what the folders hold, `N - w` frames per camera (the trial loses its last `w` frames), as in XMALab: Qt's `QtConcurrent::run` catches the exception (`QUnhandledException`) and finishes the job, and the last job's `signal_finished` relinks (`xma_engine::denoise::relink_due`; independent review, package "images"). Tests `a191_short_trials`, `xmalab3_mode_reproduces_the_cpp`, `xma-cli/tests/images.rs` `denoise_command`. Reference runs (`oracle/denoise_runs.json`, `xma parity`): XMALab writes frames 1 to 10, 11 and 9 of 12 (d01 and d02, d03, d04: temporal 5, 3, 7) and relinks to them; the XMALab 3 mode writes and relinks the same; the default mode writes all 12 (test `xma-cli/tests/images_parity.rs`). |

A user who expects the button to recalibrate gets no feedback; possible changes: recalibrate every camera regardless, or a message that nothing is pending. |
| A201 | The checkerboard reference runs k01 to k10 compared within named tolerances (`xma_cli::parity::checker_parity`), each the largest difference measured, rounded up: planar calibration without a lens model (A172: homography eigenvectors by Eigen, BLAS and LAPACK from 50 points per view in XMALab's OpenCV) relative 4e-6 (measured 3.73e-6, an RMS of 0.00137 px replayed from k04's hook table; 2.87e-6 in the dumps and saved projects); with the rational lens model relative 6e-2 or absolute 3e-2 (measured 5.0e-2 relative, k4 of a two-view rational calibration replayed from k07's hook table; in the dumps 3.07e-3 relative, 2.18e-2 absolute, k3 = -7.10); after the optimisation of cameras that number the board alike (k10) relative 5e-4 or absolute 5e-3 (measured 4.4e-4, 4.2e-3 px); after the optimisation of A189's cameras (k01, k07; mean error above 1 px; only the XMALab 3 mode numbers them so, which `xma parity` uses) relative 0.12 or absolute 0.4 (measured 0.117, a rotation vector component; 0.372 px); the manual-corner homography relative 8e-14 (measured 7.6e-14). levmar runs its whole iteration count from starting values that differ by rounding and the problem has no gauge constraint, so the optimised numbers drift apart. Two cells handled by rules: a reset camera's matrix (k08 `e7_camera_reset`: XMALab's `release` then `create` gives the freed memory back, so its dump shows the old values; A136, not compared) and coefficients 9 to 14 of a rational calibration (OpenCV returns 14, always 0; this program holds 8, compared with 0). | `xma_cli::parity::checker_parity` | Reference runs k01 to k10 | Accept (rounding with a known cause) | Every other table and hook value is identical: the detections (`checker_find*`), refinements (`checker_subpix`), predictions (`checker_predicted`) and sortings (`checker_sort`) to the bit. Reports `docs/parity/phase6/checker/`. Tests `calibration_parity_checker.rs`; and `xma-vision` `calib3d_runs.rs` `all_frames_calls`, which replays the 68 calibrations over all frames of the checkerboard runs (none bit-identical; the cube runs' 26 stay bit-identical) and asserts per quantity the bound measured there: without a lens model camera matrix 3.3e-7, RMS 3.7e-6, rotation 3.4e-7, translation 1.0e-6 relative; with the rational model camera matrix 9.3e-7, RMS 9.6e-8, rotation 5.2e-7, translation 2.7e-6 relative, coefficients 1.44 absolute. |
| A202 | Checkerboard, manual corners: any four set clicks are accepted, also when they span no quadrilateral (three coincident, collinear, a crossed order) or lie off the image: `cv::findHomography` (method 0) returns a matrix whenever the clicks spread in x and y, its predicted "corners" are stored (and, refined or not, calibrated as a success). Review 8 measured, default mode, three coincident clicks and a fourth: the camera "calibrated" with fx 3.0 against a true 820, no message. | `processing/CheckerboardDetection.cpp:110-131`; OpenCV `fundam.cpp:138-165`, `:364-420` | Review 8 (`review8_checker_edge.rs` `a184_degenerate_four_clicks_are_not_accepted`); source | Fix, compat | Default: the four clicks (the board's corners (0, 0), (H-1, 0), (H-1, V-1), (0, V-1) in that order) must be finite and span a convex quadrilateral with every turn in the same direction (either winding: a mirrored image reverses it), a non-zero area, and each side at least 2 px per square it spans (2 (H-1) px for the first and third, 2 (V-1) for the others): inner corners less than 2 px apart cannot be placed or refined (`cornerSubPix` works in an 11 x 11 window). Otherwise "The four corners do not form the board: click the four interior corners of the checkerboard in order around the board, starting with the upper left corner" (`EditError::Rejected`, project unchanged). The predicted corners must be finite and inside the image (-0.5 to size - 0.5); otherwise the detection fails ("Could not detect chessboard", A203: nothing stored). A board with one row or column of corners has no four corners: the detection fails. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::checkerboard_degenerate_clicks_accepted`): as XMALab (the homography made and its corners stored). Tests `checkerboard.rs` `a202_degenerate_four_clicks` (both modes), `review8_checker_edge.rs` `a184_degenerate_four_clicks_are_not_accepted`, `a184_click_shapes`, `small_boards_real_detector_and_manual`. |

Test `review8_checker_undo.rs` `failed_redetection_does_not_silently_reuse_old_corners` (both modes). Not reached by the reference runs k01 to k10 (no frame is detected twice). |
| A204 | Checkerboard: `runCalibrationCameraAllFrames` and `runCalibration` calibrate a board camera only once it has two frames with corners (A182), but the recalibration of the pending-changes question (`checkForPendingChanges`, C39) has no such test and calibrates a camera from a single frame, which without an intrinsic guess a single view of a plane does not determine. | `ui/WizardCalibrationCubeFrame.cpp:239-279` | Review 8 (`review8_checker_edge.rs` `a182_camera_with_one_frame`); source | Fix, compat | Default: every path that calibrates a board camera over its frames (`calibration_job`) needs two frames with corners; otherwise the camera is not calibrated, stays pending (as A132) and the edit reports "<camera> could not be calibrated over all frames: a checkerboard camera needs at least two frames with detected corners".
