| A208 | The zero-phase filter (`filtfilt`) and the cubic spline (`cubic.h`: `tridiag`, `getYD`, `ispline`) came to XMALab from sources whose terms are not clear (`NOTICE.md`). Since 16 November 2026 this program's versions are written from a mathematical specification, `docs/filter-spline-spec.md`, not ported; they give XMALab's numbers bit for bit, including these oddities, which are copied: (1) with exactly three knots the spline's system is singular; it fails (no result) or, when rounding leaves the second pivot non-zero, returns meaningless values (A46); (2) an evaluation point exactly on the last knot gives no result when it would open a new interval, and the last knot's value when that interval is already current; (3) only the first and the last evaluation points are range-checked: a later point below the first knot is extrapolated on the current interval, a later point above the last knot gives no result, non-increasing points reuse the current interval; (4) a first evaluation point so large that subtracting 1 leaves it unchanged (some values between 2^53 and 2^54, every value from 2^54, and infinity) is evaluated as +0, and a NaN first point gives NaN and no interval is ever opened; (5) repeated knots divide by zero and give NaN, not "no result"; (6) a denominator whose first coefficient is not 1 is normalised in the passes but not in the initial state; (7) a singular initial-state matrix (Appendix B's absolute pivot threshold of 100 x 2^-52) gives a zero initial state, with no error. | `processing/ButterworthLowPassFilter.cpp` (`filtfilt`, `filter`); `processing/cubic.h` | `crates/xma-vision/tests/filter_spec_vectors.rs` (the 37 vectors of the specification's section 6, identical); `tests/filter.rs` fixtures and dumps unchanged | Copy (the code's origin changes, not its results) | Reachable from callers: (1) to (5) no: the only caller of `cubic_spline`, gap interpolation in `crates/xma-engine/src/edit/ops.rs`, needs at least 4 knots (distinct frame numbers, increasing, exact in f64) and evaluates at gap frames strictly between the first and last knot, in increasing order. (6) no: `filter_run` and `filter_runs` are called with designed filters (`ButterworthLowPass::zero_phase`, order 4), whose first denominator coefficient is exactly 1; only a filter built from the public fields reaches it. (7) yes: a designed order-4 filter has a pivot below the threshold for very small cutoffs relative to the recording speed, for example 0.01 Hz at 250 frames per second (in both cutoff modes) or 0.001 Hz at 100; the passes then start from a zero state, as in XMALab 3 (see A59 for the accuracy at such cutoffs). |
| A209 | Calls that panicked, hung or allocated without bound in the ported filter and spline (none reachable by XMALab's callers, which use order 4): an order of 0 or from 30 up in `ButterworthLowPass::new`, `zero_phase` and `design_cutoff` (order 0 asserted; from 30 the `i32` binomial numerator overflows, as XMALab's `int` does; orders of 2^31 and above failed conversions; a large order took time quadratic in it and memory linear in it, 16 GiB at 2^30); a filter given m + 1 coefficients through the public fields built an m x m matrix and its inverse (8 MB at m = 1000); a filter with no order (fewer than two coefficients, through the public fields); `filter_run` with a filter of order above 4 and a run of 13 to 3 x order samples; `filter_runs` with `values` and `out` of different lengths or a run outside them; `cubic_spline` with `x` and `y` of different lengths; `ButterworthLowPass::order` with an empty denominator. | `crates/xma-vision/src/filter.rs` (the C++ throws or reads out of bounds, A46) | `crates/xma-vision/tests/filter_spec_vectors.rs` (`no_order_gives_no_result`, `high_order_short_run_is_copied`, `filter_runs_skips_runs_outside_the_slices`, `spline_with_mismatched_lengths_gives_no_result`) | Fix | Since 16 November 2026: a filter with no order gives no result (`filter`, `filter_unfused_zi`: `None`; `initial_conditions_for`: empty) and `filter_run` returns the values unchanged; `filter_run` returns a run the filter refuses unchanged, as it does runs of 12 or fewer; `filter_runs` skips a run that is reversed or not inside both slices (those frames of `out` keep their values) and filters the others; `cubic_spline` returns `None` for mismatched lengths; `order` returns 0 for an empty denominator. Order bound (16 November 2026, second step): `MAX_FILTER_ORDER` = 29, the largest order whose integer numerator XMALab computes exactly (the intermediate `(n - i + 1) * C(n, i - 1) = i * C(n, i)` is at most 14 x C(29, 14) = 1 085 822 640 at order 29 and reaches 15 x C(30, 15) = 2 326 762 800 > 2^31 - 1 at order 30, in the port's `i32` and XMALab's 32-bit `int` alike). Outside 1 to 29 there is no filter: `new` and `zero_phase` return the filter of order 0 (`d = [1]`, `c = [1]`, `scale = 1`; `order()` 0), which filters nothing; `design_cutoff` returns the entered cutoff unchanged; a filter whose order (the longer of `d` and `c`, less one) is above 29, also through the public fields, is treated like one with no order (`filter`, `filter_unfused_zi`: `None`; `initial_conditions_for`: empty; `filter_run` and `filter_runs`: values unchanged). No signature changed. With the bound, the steady-state matrix is at most 29 x 29 and every other step of the module is linear in its arguments. Review 9's `review9_finding_steady_state_allocates_quadratically` was changed to expect that refusal for m = 1000 (dated comment in the test). Tests `filter_spec_vectors.rs` `order_bound`, `review9_filter.rs`, `review9_filter_alloc.rs`. Arguments XMALab's callers produce give the same numbers as before. |
| A210 | `findFrom2Dwith3D` builds the whole list of combinations of ray candidates before it scores any: its length is the product of the candidate counts, up to 4 per ray (two per known 3D point), so 4^n entries for n rays. A body with two triangulated markers and ten markers seen by one camera makes about a million entries for one frame; at fourteen rays memory runs out. | `processing/RigidBodyPoseFrom2D.cpp:87-106` | Read in the source; review 11 (20 November 2026) | Fix (memory, both modes); the number of combinations is capped, compat (the author, 21 November 2026: "Cap at 1 million") | The combinations are scored one at a time in the list's order (`xma_vision::rigid::for_each_combination`), including the order after a ray with no candidate (A27), so every result is the same to the bit in both modes: checked against a copy of the old list builder for every pattern of up to five rays with 0 to 4 candidates, and old function against new on 22 scenes in both modes (`crates/xma-vision/tests/review11_rigid_combinations.rs`); an independent review found no difference. The time is still the product of the counts: ten rays of four candidates take about 1 s for one frame. The cap (the author, 21 November 2026; built the same day). Default mode: above 1,000,000 combinations (`xma_vision::rigid::MAX_RAY_COMBINATIONS`; exactly 1,000,000 is allowed) the frame gets no correspondence from markers seen by one camera only, so its pose comes from EPnP when one camera sees four or more of the body's markers, and otherwise there is none. That is reached with one triangulated marker from 20 single-camera rays of two candidates each (2^20), and with two triangulated markers from 10 rays of four candidates each (4^10 = 1,048,576); more rays are needed where some have fewer candidates. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::pose_from_2d_uncapped`): no cap, every combination scored, results as before to the bit. A Console line names the body, the trial and the frames (1-based, runs compressed), one line per body: "Rigid body 1 (Femur), trial T: frames 2-3 have more than 1,000,000 combinations of positions for the markers seen by one camera only; those markers were not used for the pose there." The lines stay pending in the document (`Document::take_capped_lines`); an edit's outcome never carries them. The window writes them to the Console once per interface frame, and at the end of a tracking run, a refinement or a long edit, not during it; `xma` prints them as `warning: <line>` on standard error (never on standard output) after opening, after each operation of `edit` and `calibrate`, after its work, and also when its work fails. A line identical to the last one written for the same trial and body (by index) is not written again until an undo, a redo, or the project is shown again; in `xma`, within one command. Tests: `xma-vision/tests/review14_rigid_cap.rs`, `review14b_rigid_cap_mixed.rs`; `xma-engine/tests/review14_pose_cap.rs`, `review14b_pose_cap_session.rs`, `review14c_capped_items.rs`; `xma-app/tests/review14_cap_console.rs`, `review14b_cap_console_routes.rs`, `review14c_cap_console_gui.rs`; `xma-cli/tests/review14_cap_console.rs`, `review14b_cap_console_routes.rs`, `review14c_cap_console_cli.rs`. Independent reviews: the cap correct; the route of the lines corrected once after review (first build: through the edits' messages, which sent them to standard output in `xma edit`). Not verified by running: the line at the end of a tracking run (no project with images reaches the cap). |
| A211 | An uncalibrated camera whose first used calibration frame has no inlier point. XMALab's `setInitialByReferences` then runs `cv::SVD::solveZ` on a 0 x 12 matrix, which in OpenCV 4.13 writes through a null pointer (read in OpenCV's source by the implementer and by an independent reviewer; the path of an OpenCV built with a LAPACK back end was not followed). XMALab 3 reaches it on a checkerboard after "Reset Camera" when frame 1 is not computed again before the others (the reset frame counts as used, A188); either mode reaches it with a project whose inlier flags do not match its detected points. The port panicked in `linalg::solve_z`. | `processing/Calibration.cpp:114`, `:136` | Review 11; `crates/xma-engine/tests/review11_checker_reset.rs` (panicked before) | Fix (both modes) | The calibration over all frames is refused before a camera matrix, a lens model or a pose is written (`calibrate_camera_all_frames`): silently, with the pending flag cleared, in the XMALab 3 mode (as A132), and with a message in the default mode. As before a refusal of this kind (A132, A188), frames that are not used have already been marked not calibrated, as the C++ constructor marks them. In the default mode "Reset Camera" followed by frames 2 and 3 calibrates as before. No number of a valid project changes. |
| A212 | Size arguments in `xma-vision` that come from a project (the rule of A160). The cube calibration's limits for the principal point, `width - 1`, `height - 1`, `width + 1` and `height + 1`, overflowed `int` for a stored size at the ends of its range, as XMALab's own `int` arithmetic does. The undistortion lookup table multiplied width by height without a check and allocated the result. | `processing/CubeCalibration.cpp` (the principal-point limits); `core/UndistortionObject.cpp` (the lookup table) | Review 11, second round (20 November 2026); `crates/xma-vision/tests/review12_sizes.rs` (panicked before) | Fix (both modes) | The limits are computed in `f64`, which is exact for every `int`, so every size that did not overflow gives the same result to the bit. A lookup table of more than 2^28 pixels (16384 x 16384: two maps of 1 GiB), or whose pixel count overflows, comes back empty (0 x 0), as A167 does for other sizes that cannot be allocated (`xma_vision::undistortion::MAX_LOOKUP_PIXELS`, `uncovered_table`). Every caller takes the table's own size (checked by an independent reviewer): such a camera's images are refused or its exported table is empty, and nothing panics. |
| A213 | `corner_min_eigen_val`, `corner_harris` and `good_features_to_track` (ports of OpenCV's): a Sobel aperture of 33 or more overflowed a shift before the refusal "aperture not ported" was reached. Only tests call them with an aperture other than 3. | OpenCV `modules/imgproc/src/corner.cpp` | Review 11, second round (20 November 2026); `crates/xma-vision/tests/review12_corners.rs` | Fix | The documented refusal (an aperture other than 1 or 3 is not ported) is made first. Apertures 1 and 3 are unchanged. |
| A214 | A trial is finalized once for every time it was queued (N10 in `docs/cpp-recompute.md`). Every update that starts no job (a switch to the Marker tracking workspace, selecting a trial that needs no recomputation) leaves one more entry, and the next update that runs repeats the continuity step and the filter on every body that many times. The repeats give the same result, except in one case shown by a test: a stored raw rotation longer than 2π, which the continuity step replaces again on every pass (its replacement `-r/|r|*(2π-|r|)` keeps the direction there), so the result depends on how many idle updates came before. A second case is suspected from the code and was not reproduced by the implementer or by the independent reviewer: with "filter 3D points", a virtual point driven by another body stores that body's pose after its continuity step. | `processing/ThreadScheduler.cpp:67-73`, `:100-109`; `core/RigidBody.cpp:1594` (`makeRotationsContinous`) | Review 11, third round (21 November 2026): the unit test `review13_finalize_repeat_is_noop_on_pig` in `crates/xma-engine/src/document.rs`; `crates/xma-vision/tests/review13b_continuity_repeat.rs`; `crates/xma-engine/tests/review13b_finalize_repeats.rs` | No number changes in either mode. Default mode: a repeat is skipped where it provably changes nothing. The two cases above are kept as XMALab 3 has them (the author, 21 November 2026, asked whether to finalize once always in the default mode: "If the project can't write a rotation greater than 360 we need not worry about this"; kept as it is. Checked on that answer: a project file stores no rigid-body pose, a computed rotation is at most π, the continuity step rewrites it to at most 2π, and a repeat on its own output changes nothing. Not proven: that the pose refinement can never move a rotation vector past 2π) | `Document::finalize_repeat_is_noop`: no raw rotation longer than 2π − 1e-4 or not finite, and no body-driven virtual point while "filter 3D points" is on. It is used in the exact default mode only; the XMALab 3 mode and every mixed mode run every repeat. The queue itself is unchanged in both modes (`xma parity` compares it with the C++ dumps). Force Update after 200 idle updates on the pig trial with a 25 Hz cutoff took 2.3 s and takes 0.08 s. |
| A215 | Merge-aware tracking: when a member of a pending group of three or more is dropped at a frame and two or more stay, `updateGroup` returns early. The frame is not added to the provisional window, the survivors' points there stay TRACKED instead of PREDICTED, the frame does not count towards `MergeMaxPendingFrames`, and a later confirmation "with identities swapped" does not swap it: at that frame each survivor keeps the other's blob, shown as tracked. | `processing/MergeMonitor.cpp:920-937` (the early return), `:952-966` (the tail it skips) | Read in the source; review 11 (20 November 2026). No reference run reaches it (r06 to r08 run in every mode; XMALab's merge logs of r01 to r14 show no group of three) | Fix, compat (the author, 21 November 2026: "Fix it") | Default: the frame stays in the window like every other frame the group waits through (`MergeMonitor::keep_waiting`, the old tail unchanged), for a member dropped for its cost and for one dropped because it sits on another marker's hand-set point (that drop exists in the default mode only, A84): the survivors are PREDICTED there, the frame is in the pending frames and counts towards the longest wait (the limit is reached one frame earlier), and it is confirmed or swapped with the rest. No confirmation is tried at the drop frame itself. One consequence: a survivor's hand-set point at the drop frame now stops a later swap against it, as at any other waiting frame. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::merge_drop_leaves_frame_out`): as XMALab 3. Results differ only in a run in which a pending group of three or more loses a member while two or more stay. Tests: `tracking/merge/groups.rs` `drop_tests::a215_*` (four; both drop reasons, both modes, the longest wait), `xma-engine/tests/review14b_merge_drop.rs` (a group of three formed by the monitor itself, forward and backward; the reference runs unchanged by the switch). Independent review: correct. Not tested: three survivors (a group of four losing one). **The code this row describes was removed with merge-aware tracking on 26 November 2026 (A226).** |
| A216 | Import 2D Points and the point edits over many cells (Reset above error, Delete Data and Set Untrackable over frames, Exchange Tracked Data, Interpolate) do their follow-up work after every single point: the marker's triangulation (the import triangulates every frame again at the end), the dependent rigid bodies with the rotation-continuity step, and the mean size. A file of 50 markers, 5000 frames and 3 cameras makes 750,000 reconstructions whose results are overwritten. | `core/Marker.cpp`, `Marker::setPoint`, `Marker::resetPoint`, and their callers in the import and the edits (`docs/cpp-editing.md`) | Read in the source; review 11 (20 November 2026) | Fix, compat (the author, 21 November 2026: "Once at the end") | Default: the points are written first. A written cell is triangulated as it is written in the point edits (Interpolate in 3D reads points it has just written) and once at the end in the import; the mean size once per marker, and each dependent body once per touched frame, in frame order with the continuity step, at the end (`edit/ops.rs`, `Deferred`): the order of a tracking run's writes. XMALab 3 mode, and whenever A31 (first body only) is on: point by point (`CppCompat::point_edits_follow_up_per_point`). The results are identical except: (1) Import, at a frame whose final triangulation over all cameras is refused (A34) after an intermediate one with fewer cameras succeeded (needs three or more cameras): point by point the marker keeps the intermediate 3D point, projections and errors under an undefined status; now a new marker's unset values. (2) An edit that resets every counted size of a marker: point by point the mean size is the last remaining size; now the mean from before the edit. (3) The pose stored at a (body, frame) where the body ends without a pose: point by point that of the last intermediate success; now the one from before the edit. It is never shown, saved, filtered or exported (every reader checked by the independent reviewer). Each edit is still one undo step. Measured (release build on a loaded machine; pig_clean, 145 frames, 2 cameras): Import 2D Points of 200 markers 0.4 to 0.7 s against 1.1 to 4.9 s; Reset above error on a body 0.004 s against 0.2 to 0.9 s. Tests: `xma-engine/tests/review14_batched_edits.rs`, `review14b_batched_orders.rs` (both orders compared unmasked on eight more datasets), `perf14_edits.rs` (ignored timing); `xma-cli` `edit_parity` (XMALab 3 mode unchanged). Independent review: correct. |
| A217 | The blob detector's threshold loop, `for (t = minThreshold; t < maxThreshold; t += thresholdStep)`, has no bound. Its three settings come unchecked from the settings file stored in a project: a tiny step (1e-30), an infinite or huge maximum, or a small step (0.001) hangs marker detection (methods 1 and 6) and grid and bead detection, for ever or for hours. On 8-bit images a threshold of 2^31 or more overflows `cvFloor` and thresholds the image all white, so a whole patch can be reported as a blob. | OpenCV `features2d/src/blobdetector.cpp`, `SimpleBlobDetectorImpl::detect`; settings `BlobDetectorThresholdStep`, `BlobDetectorMinThreshold`, `BlobDetectorMaxThreshold` | Read in the source; review 11 (20 November 2026) | Fix, both modes (the author, 21 November 2026: "Bound it") | Thresholds of 256 or more are not run (`THRESHOLD_CAP`): they find nothing (8-bit: the binary image is empty from `floor(t)` = 255 up to 2^31; deeper images on the 8-bit scale are at most 255.996). Settings that need more than 100,000 thresholds below min(maximum, 256) (`MAX_THRESHOLDS`, counted by the loop's own additions), a step that does not move the first threshold, and threshold settings that are not finite are refused (`BlobParamError::TooManyThresholds`, `ThresholdsNotFinite`). Marker detection then finds nothing (A72); "compute undistortion" and cube calibration are refused with a sentence naming the settings, for example "bead detection: the blob detector's threshold settings (minimum, maximum, step: BlobDetectorMinThreshold, BlobDetectorMaxThreshold, BlobDetectorThresholdStep) would need more than 100,000 thresholds; use a larger step or a narrower range" (every `BlobParamError` has such a sentence now; before, its internal name was shown). Differs from XMALab 3 only for: settings that hang or need more than 100,000 thresholds; settings that are not finite, including the few that used to end (an infinite or NaN step ran one pass, a NaN bound none); and, on 8-bit images, a maximum above 2^31, whose all-white passes are no longer run. Everything else is identical to the bit (1200 random cases against a copy of the old loop, and the reference data). The Settings dialog does not show these three settings. No compatibility switch: XMALab 3's behaviour here is a hang. Tests: `xma-vision/tests/review14_blob_bounds.rs`, `review14b_blob_cap_inputs.rs`, `review14_blob_messages.rs`; `xma-engine/tests/review14_bead_refusal.rs`. Independent review: correct. |
| A218 | Tracking follows each marker in each camera on its own (a template match round a 2D prediction, then a detection), and checks the cameras against each other only afterwards, by the reprojection error (at most `MaximumReprojectionError`, 5 px). That check cannot see a point that slides along the other camera's line of sight, so a camera can drift onto another dot and stay valid; a marker that fails is reset in every camera and not tracked again in that run. On the Rat example, tracking all markers forward from one frame gives a fifth of the points the author finished by hand, and at the frame a marker was lost its last accepted point was already 6 to 67 px off in one camera for 10 of 19 markers. | `ui/WizardDigitizationFrame.cpp` (`trackAll`, `checkIfValid`), `processing/MarkerTracking.cpp`, `core/Marker.cpp` (`isValid`); `docs/cpp-tracking.md` sections 1 and 3 | Measured against the author's hand-finished Rat points (`oracle/py/rat/diagnose_drops.py`, `heldout.py`) and the pig's filled project; no reference run (PLAN.md section 13) | Addition (the author, 21 November 2026: "let's improve tracking, either standard, merge aware, or a new one"); the existing kinds are unchanged in both modes | A new method beside the existing kinds, default mode only, refused with a sentence in the XMALab 3 mode: `xma track --kind multi` and `multi-selected` (`xma_engine::tracking::multi::MultiTracker`, pure functions in `xma_vision::joint_tracking`, every setting a documented field of `MultiTrackParams`). Per step, for all markers together: a 3D constant-velocity prediction projected into every camera; several candidates per camera (local maxima of a zero-mean normalised correlation, each refined by the marker's own detection, kept only with a marker-like contrast); one candidate per camera chosen jointly (reprojection error at most 1.5 px, raised per marker to 2.5 times its recent median up to 3 px; inside a gate of 5 px round the prediction); no dot given to two markers unless they truly overlap (a contested dot goes to the marker closer to it among those whose choice passes every test; a tie goes to neither); a marker followed by position for 3 frames after it was last seen and after that found again only when its distances to at least two markers already accepted at the frame fit (distances that have stayed constant, learned from the frames the run has). What a run may read (decided after the independent review, 21 November 2026): only its own record of trusted observations, that is, points at the start frame and behind it as they were when the run started, points the run wrote, and points the existing kinds may not overwrite (set by hand, and the others of `docs/cpp-tracking.md` 3.4) at any frame; at and behind the start every point with data is read whatever its status (a run starts from whatever points the user gives it, predicted and interpolated included), and ahead of the start a predicted or interpolated point is never read (it is overwritten, or reset for a marker the run has had, like any cell the run may write; an interpolated point is left as it is when `TrackInterpolatedPoints` is off); only in the cameras it tracks (shown when the run starts, calibrated, with a pose; a hidden camera is neither written nor read; the set is fixed for the run); a run that would track fewer than two cameras is refused with a sentence ("Multi-camera tracking needs at least two calibrated cameras that are shown; 1 of this project's 2 is shown. ..."; found by review 15d: hiding one camera of two had given plain 2D following with no cross-check; the author, 22 November 2026, to the recommendation to refuse such a run: "Fix the three"); its 3D positions come from triangulating those observations, never from the stored 3D points. What a run does to an existing point it does not confirm: a marker is the run's once the run has held a trusted observation of it, and for such a marker an overwritable cell at the new frame that gets no point from the run is reset to undefined, so the frames a run covered hold its points, the protected points and gaps, never an older run's point that looks tracked; a marker the run never had is neither searched for nor touched (as the existing kinds leave markers they do not track). Running over the result of an earlier "all points" run gives the result of running on the clean project, cell for cell. Two builds read what they should not: the first read the hand-finished 3D points left in the start projects (87.5 % scored for 57.8 %), and the second read whatever lay at the frames ahead (another marker's points ahead turned 10,895 good cells into 251); the guard tests give a run garbage in every such place and require the same result. Work is bounded whatever the settings say (templates of at most 129 px, 2e8 multiply-adds per correlation surface, 65,536 combinations per marker, frame counts clamped to the trial). Measured from clean start projects on twelve Rat cases (three used for tuning or checked by the builder, nine not): coverage of the author's points 50 to 83 % against 16 to 59 % for "all points" and merge-aware; 96.5 to 98.0 % of its points within 1 px of the author's against 81 to 97 %; 4 to 73 points more than 5 px off against 29 to 484. Pig, from one frame: 99.0 and 99.4 % coverage, none more than 5 px off (the existing kinds: 94.0 to 100 %, 0 to 32 off), and 17 and 40 points where the filled project has none (the existing kinds: 17 to 56). About 6 ms a step for 19 markers and two cameras, twice the existing kinds. Results are identical on repeated runs and nearly the same on the JPEG files as on lossless frames. Known limits: about a third of the Rat points are still not found from one frame (markers lost and not found again: finding again succeeds rarely); two markers that merge in one camera and separate can still be swapped; only two-camera data was measured. In the window since 21 November 2026: "Enable multi-camera tracking" on the Marker tracking Toolbox page shows the groups "multi-camera selected" and "multi-camera all", run with the default settings (row U167). Tests `xma-vision` `joint_tracking` unit tests, `xma-engine/tests/multi_tracking.rs` (floors on the Rat and the pig; the result is identical with and without stale 3D points in the start project), `xma-cli/tests/track_multi.rs`, `xma-engine/tests/track_run.rs`, `xma-app/tests/tracking_ui.rs` (`multi_camera_tracking_from_the_toolbox`), the reviews' `xma-engine/tests/review15b_multi_doc.rs` and `review15d_multi_rules.rs` (the hidden camera on three cameras, the pig's or the Rat's second camera duplicated), `xma-app/tests/review15d_multi_window.rs`, and the check of the corrections of 22 November 2026, `xma-engine/tests/review15e_multi_fixes.rs` and `xma-app/tests/review15e_multi_window.rs` (every list of shown cameras refused or accepted alike by the window and the engine; the "left empty" number equal to a recount of the result; a camera hidden during a run). **Extended on 22 November 2026** (a marker that moves fast at its first step, a marker with one rigid partner, the prediction after one camera's gap, a partner counted twice): row A219, which also gives the numbers as they are now. |
| A219 | Multi-camera tracking (A218) as first built, measured on a third example (the guineafowl: two markers on one bone, two cameras, steps of 20 to 33 px a frame when the foot comes in and leaves; `oracle/DATASETS.md`): it found 39.6 % of the author's points (none wrong), where merge-aware tracking found nearly all in most runs with 303 points more than 5 px off. Four causes: with one earlier point no velocity is known and the marker was looked for within 10 px, so a marker that had moved 19 to 30 px could not be started; a lost marker was found again only when it kept its distance to two markers already found, so a marker with one rigid partner never was; after one camera's gap the 3D track had no velocity, the prediction stood still, and the camera that had kept the marker had its right dot refused; and (found by review) the same pair of markers could reach the identity check by two links, so one partner passed for two. | No C++ counterpart: the method is new in this program (A218) | Measured against points finished by hand on three examples by the nine-run mean (`python3 oracle/py/heldout.py all --jitter`: 24 cases, as given and with four settings moved slightly either way; one run's totals move by chance, the Rat's count of points more than 5 px off between 203 and 481 for a threshold moved by 0.01); three independent reviews (16a, 16b, 16c), whose tests are in the tree; no reference run (PLAN.md section 13) | Improvement of A218 (the author, 22 November 2026: "Yes, build them"), default mode only; the existing kinds and the XMALab 3 mode give byte-identical files before and after. `contrast_relative` is a switch, off by default (the author, 22 November 2026, on the recommendation of the review: "Keep off if that's what you recommend"). (6) was built on the author's remark of the same day that the guineafowl's markers are conical metal points and "users ... just click in the dark blob", and reported to the author with its measurements | **What the method does now, beyond A218.** (1) *Identity.* A marker found again needs `reacquire_links` (2) rigid partners kept and none broken, but no more than it has, at least one (a marker with one partner needs that one; with none it cannot be confirmed). A partner counts once, is kept only when every link given for the pair holds (the learned link and a lost marker's rigid-prediction link for the same pair) and is broken when any breaks, for `reacquire_links` and for `rigid_majority` alike. (2) *A wide first step* (`gate_start`, 40 px; a value that is not finite and positive turns it off). In a camera where neither the 3D track nor the camera's own 2D track has a velocity, candidates are also searched within `gate_start` (the maxima beyond the ordinary window, strongest first, at most 64 examined). A candidate beyond the ordinary no-velocity gate is kept only when (a) the image of the marker's earlier frame shows no other dot nearer to it than the marker's own earlier point was (a dot within the marker's dot radius plus `same_dot` of that point is the marker's own), and (b) the image of the next frame holds a dot within `gate` of where constant velocity in that camera puts it (template cut at the candidate). Only those two images are read, never a stored point. For a marker with such a cell the choice ranks combinations in classes (two or more cameras without a far dot; two or more with one; one camera): the wide step never costs a point the ordinary search would have accepted in two or more cameras, and a point seen by one camera gives way to a far pair that triangulates. (3) *Velocity after a gap.* A camera whose own 2D track has a velocity uses it when the 3D track has none. (4) *`contrast_relative`* (`xma track --multi contrast_relative=1`; off by default; the window uses the defaults): the contrast test compares the contrast as a share of the brightness of the ring round the dot with the median of the same quantity, so that a marker is not refused when something denser passes over it; the absolute test is used where another marker of the run is predicted inside that ring, and the absolute contrast must reach half the usual fraction as well. (5) `xma` no longer holds its output locks while it runs (a message printed from a worker thread hung it; found while tracing a start). (6) *Points the run did not detect itself are refined.* For a point the run reads but did not write (the start frame and up to `max_gap` + `velocity_window` frames behind it, 7 with the defaults and never more than 16; a protected point it reaches) the run works from the marker's own detection at that point, run again from its result until it settles (at most 6 runs, the last moving no more than 1 px), when the blob is at most twice the marker's mean size, its centre is more than `same_dot` and at most 10 px from the stored point, the two lie on one dark shape with no brighter sample between them (`xma_vision::joint_tracking::on_one_shape`), no other marker's point the run reads there lies on that shape, and, where the run reads the marker in two cameras or more at that frame, the largest reprojection error with the refined positions is at most the larger of `max_reprojection_error` and the error from the stored points. Otherwise the stored point is used; a point within `same_dot` of its detected centre is used exactly as it is. Only the run's own record uses the refined position (templates, contrast baseline, 2D predictions and velocities, its own triangulation): the stored point is never changed, and a protected point is used as stored in the choice at its own frame. An image behind the start is read for this only and not kept. **Measured** (nine-run means, range in brackets), before and now: guineafowl found 39.6 % and 74.8 % (73.8 to 75.0), none more than 5 px off, 1 and 8 points where the author has none (looked at: the marker itself at the picture's edge, one or two frames past the author's first or last point); Rat 68.1 % and 68.2 % (67.8 to 68.8), 293 and 295 more than 5 px off (208 to 362 and 228 to 362); pig 99.2 % both, none off, 57 where the filled project has none. The ten guineafowl runs: 24.9, 35.2, 91.4, 90.0, 79.9, 99.2, 96.3, 97.3, 90.6 and 96.1 % (before: 0.0, 0.0, 36.2, 88.3, 79.9, 99.2, 41.3, 59.2, 30.6 and 56.1 %). **Measured for (6).** Why it was needed: with the guineafowl's start points moved inside their dark shapes (4 and 8 px along the shape, 3 px across it; ten runs for each move; a move that would leave the shape is not made, so of the 40 start points 22 and 6 are moved by 8 px, 34 and 33 by 4 px, 31 and 32 across; `python3 oracle/py/displaced_starts.py shape`) the mean share found fell from 80.1 % to between 14.3 and 55.8 %, and 9 to 31 of 36 starts were lost (a template cut off-centre is found off-centre, and the detection's move of more than `max_detection_shift` back to the centre was refused). With (6): 62.5 to 80.1 %, 30 to 36 starts kept, 0 or 1 point more than 5 px off per set; with the stored size removed as well, 66.7 to 80.0 % (before: 10.8 to 62.9 %). On the Rat and the pig, every start point moved in a random direction (`python3 oracle/py/displaced_starts.py random`; the Rat's nine one-frame starts with six seeds, the pig's two with twelve; one run's count of wrong points moves a great deal with the seed), before and with (6): moved 2 px, the Rat 62.6 % found both ways with 1,923 and 1,201 more than 5 px off, the pig 98.9 and 99.2 %, none off; moved 3 px, the Rat 50.2 and 60.3 % with 4,969 and 2,429 off, the pig 79.4 and 97.2 % with 360 and 48. The reviewer measured the same with moves of its own and looked at every refinement: of 1,130 kept there, 1,120 ended nearer the author's point and none within 3 px of another marker's. With the author's points as they are the nine-run means are level: Rat 68.6 % (68.2 to 69.0) with 290 more than 5 px off (227 to 360), guineafowl 74.8 % (73.8 to 75.0), none off, 10 where the author has none (the two new ones `TMTtop` in camera 2 at frames 25 and 26, the marker at the picture's edge), pig 99.2 % with 57. Seven of the 24 result files change (Rat from frames 1, 1 with JPEG images, 50 and 1 to 100; guineafowl forward from 300 and from 27 to 127, backward from 150): thirteen of the author's own points lie 1.5 to 4.3 px from their detected centre and are refined (looked at in the images by the builder and the reviewer: each on the marker's dark shape, none on a neighbour; the Rat's marker 2 in camera 1 at frame 50 lies in a dark band and is unclear). Two more are refused by the cross-camera check: the Rat's marker 12 in camera 1 at frame 97, hand-placed in a dark band, which the detection moved 9 px onto the band's edge (reprojection error 2.17 px as stored, 6.78 px moved; the case the check was built for, after "more dot contrast at the new place" was measured and did not separate it), and the guineafowl's `TMTmid` in camera 2 at frame 427 (0.96 and 1.86 px). The limit of that check was chosen between two: with `max_reprojection_cap` (3 px) the moved-start sets keep 4 to 12 points more coverage in four rows and get 16 points more than 5 px off, all from one refinement of `TMTtop` onto the bar-shaped marker beside it (error 1.78 px) that the run then follows for nine frames; with `max_reprojection_error` (1.5 px) that refinement is refused, at the cost of 13 of 231 refinements there (and 72 of about 1,200 on the reviewer's Rat and pig runs, every one of which would have been good). **With `contrast_relative` on:** guineafowl 82.5 %, Rat 69.3 % (68.8 to 69.7) with 226 off (194 to 273), pig 99.7 % with 70 where the filled project has none, and results that move less by chance (with (6) as well: Rat 69.3 % (68.9 to 69.6) with 229 off (194 to 272), the others the same). Why it is not the default: on the pig, marker 5 is put on marker 9's dot or between the two at frames 42 to 52 of camera 1, where the two merge and the default takes the right dot; on the Rat 45 points more than 5 px off appear that the default does not make, some on another marker's dot and some where there is no dot (the totals still fall, because fewer runs go astray); the Rat's floor from frame 201 is missed (79.9 % against 81 %); and one guineafowl start that the looser test had allowed is refused by rule (a): `TMTtop` backward from frame 427, where another, bar-shaped marker stands against it in camera 1 and lay nearer to where it lands. **Reviews.** 16a found five faults in the first build of (2) and one older one: a far dot the next frame confirmed made the right near dot a tie (one Rat marker fell from 520 points to 29); the wide step ran where a camera had a velocity; a not-a-number `gate_start` turned it fully on; twelve faint dots hid the marker from it; the partner counted twice. 16b found three in the answers: a lone dot in one camera beat a far pair that triangulated; a row of dots moving together put a hidden marker on its neighbour; the guard against that refused a real fast marker when another dot had been where it lands (accepted as a limit: a refused start costs one more point set by hand, a wrong start looks tracked). 16c found the guard still passed a row moving faster than the ordinary gate (answered by rule (a) above) and judged the kind fit to use with the defaults, and not with `contrast_relative` on. A last check (16d) of rule (a) held against the fast row and against clutter, and left one contrived start as a known limit (`review16d_earlier_frame.rs`, its test ignored): a start point set on a blob of two merged dots of which the other leaves fast while the marker stays and is hidden; the images cannot tell that from the marker leaving. 16e reviewed (6): it repeated the figures, found the stored points, statuses and sizes at and behind the start unchanged in all 24 results (737,880 cells) and the other kinds byte-identical, and judged it fit to stay the default after two faults were put right: with a huge `max_gap` or `velocity_window` the first step read and kept every image behind the start (2,234 MB on the Rat where it had been 39 MB; now at most 16 frames back, each image dropped after use: 49 MB), and each of the five guards could be switched off without a test failing (now a test for each: four unit tests calling the refinement with the real detection, and a whole run for the cross-camera check). It also showed the merge limit below, and that a new run refines the points an earlier run wrote (the window's "next frame" is a run of one step: on the Rat's frames 1 to 26, single-step runs differ from those without (6) in 6 cells, one continuous run in 1). 16f checked the answers: the first step with every such setting made huge takes 47 MB and 0.6 s, nothing else in it grows with the settings, each of the five guards switched off fails exactly its own test, the results are byte-identical to those 16e reviewed, and the judgement stands (fit to stay the default); its one remark, of low weight, is that no test counts the images held at once (bounded by the 16-frame cap; said in the module documentation). Two rules of the first specification were wrong and were corrected when they were measured: "the first link given for a pair counts" threw away the link that could fail and raised the Rat's wrong points from 296 to 447 (the rule in (1) leaves them at 295). **Known limits:** two markers that merge in one camera can still be swapped (the main source of the Rat's wrong points; since addressed, row A220); the wide first step when the marker itself is hidden or faint at that step and something beside it passes both tests (a user sets the point at two frames for a fast marker: the run then has a velocity and does not use it); a point seen by one camera only is accepted on the prediction; a real fast marker is refused when another dot lay nearer to where it lands than it did, which includes a marker that touches another one at the start (the guineafowl's `TMTtop` at frame 427) (counted on the author's points, were every step of 10 to 40 px a first step: another marker refuses none of the guineafowl's 504 and 4 of the Rat's 40; with the defaults no start of the 24 cases is lost); with the default the contrast test still drops real points while something denser passes over a marker (guineafowl camera 1, frames 171 to 215); the guineafowl's `TMTtop` cannot be started at frame 27, where no marker is visible at the author's point in camera 1 and one camera alone may not jump 24 px; for (6): a marker that touches, at the start, something that is not a marker of the run makes one dark shape with it and can be worked from the merged shape's centre when the move is consistent between the cameras or lies along the other camera's epipolar line (review 16e's scene, a marker of size 3 against a dot of size 5: points about 3 px off the marker while the two stay merged, where the run without (6) left the first frame empty and was right from the second; a size limit of 1.5 times was measured, does not refuse that scene, whose blob is 1.49 times the mean size, and costs coverage on the moved guineafowl starts, so it stays at twice; test `review16e_start_on_a_marker_touching_a_non_marker_dot`, ignored); a point that one camera only has at its frame is not cross-checked; a point with no stored size has no size limit; an off-centre start of `TMTtop` beside the bar-shaped marker is still refused or misled; the rigid distances are learned from points up to `rigid_history` frames back, which are not refined. Not measured: three cameras, frames deeper than 8 bits. Tests: `xma-vision` `joint_tracking` unit tests (`identity_needs_no_more_links_than_partners`, `a_pair_given_twice_is_one_partner_kept_only_when_both_hold`, `rank_classes_with_a_near_gate`, `relative_contrast_of_a_dot`), `review15b_joint_hostile.rs`, `review16a_identity.rs`, `review16b_choice.rs`; `xma-engine/tests/multi_tracking.rs` (floors on the guineafowl too), `multi_wide_start.rs`, `multi_wide_static_dots.rs`, `multi_velocity_gap.rs`, `multi_contrast_guards.rs`, `review16a_wide_start.rs`, `review16b_wide_start.rs`, `review16c_wide_start.rs`, `review16d_earlier_frame.rs`, `multi_refine_start.rs`, `multi_refine_guards.rs`, `review16e_refine.rs`, and the unit tests of `tracking::multi` (`refinement_cross_camera_decision`, `refinement_across_a_bright_gap_is_refused`, `refinement_onto_a_shape_shared_with_another_marker_is_refused`, `refinement_that_walks_along_a_band_is_refused`, `refinement_onto_a_blob_much_larger_than_the_marker_is_refused`); `xma-vision` `points_on_one_shape` and `review16e_on_one_shape.rs`; `xma-cli/tests/std_io_not_held.rs`. |
| A220 | Multi-camera tracking (A218, A219): two markers of one run whose dots meet in one camera. Before this row the Rat's nine-run mean was 290 points more than 5 px off, and `oracle/py/wrong_points.py` over the nine runs says what they were on: 153 a run on the dot of another marker of the run that had no point of its own there (118 of them with this marker right in the other camera), 38 on a dot the other marker held too (two markers on one dot), 4 exchanged, 41 beside a merged shape (the marker's own dot within 8 px of another's), 53 with no marker near. Where a point sat on another marker's dot the author has the two 8 to 12 px apart in half the cases and further in a third: the identities were lost at an overlap earlier and the error carried on. A worked case (forward from frame 50, markers 17 and 3 in camera 1): the two overlap at frames 295 to 297; marker 17 is 3 to 4 px off at frames 293 to 305, has no point at 306 to 316, and from frame 317 sits on marker 3's dot for about 60 frames while the author has them 10 to 13 px apart; camera 2 is right throughout. | No C++ counterpart: the method is new in this program (A218). XMALab's own answer to overlaps is the merge-aware kind (`MergeMonitor`), which is unchanged | Measured on the merged tree: the nine-run means (`python3 oracle/py/heldout.py all --jitter`), each rule switched off in turn, `wrong_points.py` and `overlap_cells.py` over the nine runs, the displaced starts (`displaced_starts.py`), and the pig's crossing against a truth made from its two rigid bodies (`pig_crossing_truth.py`, `pig_crossing_score.py`; `oracle/DATASETS.md`, "Measuring a tracking result"). Independent review 17 and its checks 17b and 17c, whose tests are in the tree. No reference run (PLAN.md section 13) | Improvement of A218 (the author, 23 November 2026, to the proposal to add the handling of markers that merge in one camera to the multi-camera kind: "Yes, build it"; and, the same day, "the pig dataset has markers crossing"). Default mode only. Every rule is a parameter (`MultiTrackParams`, `xma track --multi`), and with all of them off the kind writes byte-identical files to the version before (all 216 results of the nine runs compared); the other kinds and the XMALab 3 mode are not touched. Two of the rules are a small trade between wrong points and coverage (Notes, "what each rule is worth"): both are on by the author's decision (23 November 2026: "Keep both on."). Writing a point "held" through an overlap, as the merge-aware kind does, is not built: the cells stay empty (for the author to decide) | **What the run does now.** It keeps, per pair of its markers and camera, an *overlap state*: the pair enters it when the two touch there (their positions, a point or else the prediction of a marker being followed, within the two dot radii plus `overlap_margin`, 1 px) and stays in it until both hold points further apart than that, or `overlap_max_frames` (30) steps after they last touched. With it: (1) *Sharing* (`share_needs_overlap`): two markers hold one dot in a camera only while they touch there now; before, any marker predicted within 1.5 px of a dot could share it. (2) *Merged points* (`overlap_clean`): a point accepted while its marker touches another is not a clean picture of its dot: no template or contrast is taken from it (the last clean template is kept, and does not expire while the marker still holds points there), and that frame's 3D point gives no depth to a lost marker, no rigid prediction and no rigid statistics. (3) *Identity out of an overlap* (`overlap_decision`, 0.5): after the choice, for every pair in the state, the dots given to the two in that camera are compared with the other assignment (the two exchanged; or, once they no longer touch and only one holds a dot, that dot given to the other), each marker costed with what it holds in the other cameras plus one per rigid partner broken; the assignment stands when the other costs at least 0.5 more, both get nothing there when the two are within that margin, and the other is taken when it is that much cheaper and can be taken with exactly what the markers hold elsewhere. All pairs are decided from the same state and applied together, so the result does not depend on the markers' numbering; a marker asked two different things gets nothing. (4) *A marker being found again never shares a dot* (`reacquire_exclusive`). (5) *Claims* (`claim_radius` 6 px, `claim_ratio` 0.5): a marker being found again may not take a dot within 6 px of where another marker, followed in that camera, is predicted, unless its own prediction is at most half as far from the dot. (6) *Where a lost marker is looked for* (`ray_links`): in a camera that lost the marker while the other kept it, the search is centred on the place on the other camera's line of sight where the marker keeps its distances to its rigid partners (two or more, and one such place only), with the narrow gate, instead of the depth of its last 3D point with the wide one. **Result** (nine-run means and ranges, before and now; "extra" is points where the author has none). Rat: found 68.6 % (68.2 to 69.0) and 68.9 % (68.5 to 69.1); within 1 px 97.0 and 97.3 %; more than 5 px off 290 (227 to 360) and 158 (130 to 179). Guineafowl: 74.8 %, none off, 10 extra, unchanged in every run. Pig: 99.2 %, none off, extra 57 and 55. The aim set for the round, the Rat's wrong points halved (145), was not reached. By what the wrong points are on, a run, before and now: another marker's dot while that marker has no point there 153 and 59; two markers on one dot 38 and 0; exchanged 4 and 0; beside a merged shape 41 and 42; no marker near 53 and 56. **In the overlap cells themselves** (`overlap_cells.py`: the author has another marker within 8 px in that camera; 14,284 of the Rat's 161,804 cells, 4 of the pig's, none of the guineafowl's), a run, before and now: within 5 px 6,227 and 5,862, more than 5 px off 84 and 72, empty 7,973 and 8,351; in all other cells within 5 px 104,477 and 105,412, more than 5 px off 206 and 86. So the run leaves about 380 more overlap cells empty and is right in about 935 more cells elsewhere: what it no longer guesses at an overlap it no longer carries on wrongly afterwards. **What each rule is worth** (the Rat's nine-run means with one rule off and the others on; the guineafowl and the pig do not move in any of them). Without (2), merged points not trusted: 68.3 % found and 192 more than 5 px off. Without (6), the search where the links put the marker: 68.3 % and 193. Without (3), the decision: 69.1 % and 168. Without (5), claims: 69.2 % and 170. Without both (3) and (5): 69.3 % and 221 (164 to 363). Without (1), and without (4): no change from all on (68.9 % and 158; the other rules cover the same cases). So (2) and (6) gain on both counts; (3) and (5) cover for each other, either alone removing about 50 wrong points, and the second of them buys 10 to 12 fewer wrong points for 350 to 500 fewer right ones (0.2 to 0.3 points of coverage; run by run, fewer wrong points in 8 or 9 of the nine runs and fewer right ones in 7 or 8): this is the trade the author decided on 23 November 2026 (both on). A figure in the builder's first report (203 without claims) came from an earlier build and is not used. **The pig's crossing** (markers 5 and 9 in camera 1, frames 34 to 53; 40 cells the reference leaves empty; truth to about 1 px). No kind puts a point more than 5 px off inside it. Multi-camera, forward: 2 points within 5 px and 38 cells empty, before and now; backward: 23 and 21 within 5 px, the rest empty; right on both sides of the crossing in both directions, before and now. Going forward, marker 5 is lost in camera 1 from about frame 16, before the crossing, for a reason that is not the crossing: the ground round it brightens at frames 7 to 14 and its contrast doubles, then falls back below 0.6 of what the run had got used to (the builder's trace; with `contrast_relative=1` it is kept and 13 of its 20 crossing cells are within 5 px). The existing kinds, for comparison: "all points" has a point within 5 px at every frame of the crossing (8 of 40 within 2 px forward, 6 backward) and comes out of it with one marker wrong (forward, marker 9 is more than 5 px off for 16 points and then lost; backward, marker 5 is more than 5 px off for 32 points); merge-aware forward has all 40 within 5 px (30 within 2 px) and is right afterwards; merge-aware backward stops both markers in both cameras at the crossing for good (131 cells empty outside it). **Start points off the marker's centre** (A219, item 6) are not hurt (`displaced_starts.py`, before and now): the guineafowl's ten runs with each start point moved inside its dark shape are the same in every line; the Rat's nine one-frame starts with six seeds, moved 2 px, 62.6 and 64.8 % found with 1,201 and 1,147 points more than 5 px off, and moved 3 px 60.3 and 60.5 % with 2,429 and 2,194; the pig's two with twelve seeds, moved 2 px, 99.2 % and none off, and moved 3 px 97.2 % with 48 and 43. **With `contrast_relative=1`** (the switch of A219, off) the rules cost coverage: Rat 69.3 % (68.9 to 69.6) with 229 (194 to 272) more than 5 px off before, 68.2 % (66.7 to 68.7) with 174 (145 to 248) now; guineafowl 82.5 % and 21 extra, unchanged; pig 99.7 %, extra 70 and 68. **Two places where the author's Rat points may be off** were found in what remains (marker 4 in camera 1 at frames 435 to 438, about 22 of the 158 a run; marker 14 in camera 1 at frames 79 to 81 and 101 to 103, about 36): in the pictures the run is on the dark dot and the author's point beside it (`oracle/DATASETS.md`). Not confirmed by the author; the counts above include them. **Review 17** (independent of the implementation) found two faults, both fixed before this row was written, with its tests: a marker that touched another for longer than the template's life (301 frames with the defaults) lost its template and was never looked for in that camera again (a template now ages from the marker's latest point there, merged or not); and the decision in (3) was made pair by pair, each seeing the last one's result, so with three markers in contact the answer depended on their numbering (191 of 20,000 random scenes; now none). It confirmed that all rules off is byte-identical to the version before, that no existing test or tolerance changed, and that nothing reads the hand-finished points. Its check of the fixes (17b) confirmed both and found one more thing: where the decision in (3) empties one camera of a marker accepted in two, the other camera's point stays alone, without a 3D point and without the tests a point seen by one camera otherwise has to pass (`single_gate`, `single_ambiguity`). The check was built as a switch (`overlap_single_checked`) and measured: with it on, the Rat loses about 235 points a run that are within 2 px of the author's and no point more than 5 px off (68.7 % found against 68.9 %, 159 and 158 off; guineafowl and pig the same). It is therefore **off** by default (decided with the reviewer's agreement in its last check, 17c: the points are the accepted pair's own dots, and the same kind of point has arisen since A219 where the choice refuses a marker's other camera), and such points are unchecked. 17b also named three paths that no test covered; each now has a test that fails when the path is broken. 17c found no defect and judged the round fit to be the default for this kind, as a kind whose result the user looks over. **Known limits.** The rules see only markers of the run, and only where the run has a position for them: a marker lost in a camera with no prediction there is invisible, and another marker can still be put on its dot (most of the 59 a run that remain on another marker's dot); a marker that jumps after a one-frame gap, with the widened gate, onto the dot of a marker the run is not following is not caught; a marker left with one camera by the decision in (3) keeps that point unchecked (review 17b, above); the cells of an overlap are left empty, not filled (8,351 of the Rat's 14,284 a run); a new run starts with no overlap state, so a pair that parted just before the start frame is not held (the start points are taken as right); claims for markers being followed too, and taking no point at all while two markers touch (`overlap_take=0`), were measured by the builder and were worse in found and wrong points alike. Not measured: three cameras. The five numbers (margin 1 px, hold 30 frames, claim distance 6 px, claim ratio 0.5, decision margin 0.5: set when each rule was written and not moved since; checked afterwards by halving and doubling each, nine-run means on the Rat against 68.9 % and 158: margin 0.5 px 69.0 % and 165, 2 px 68.0 % and 233; hold 15 or 60 frames no change; claim distance 3 px 69.3 % and 170, 12 px 68.3 % and 157; claim ratio 0.25 68.5 % and 156, 1 69.0 % and 164; decision margin 0.25 69.0 % and 165, 1 68.8 % and 162; guineafowl and pig unmoved; so four are flat and the touching margin is the sensitive one, a wider margin losing a third of the gain; nothing was moved after the check; `target/scratch-6b80ad34/r8d/`). Not measured: any second trial with many overlaps. **What this row's figures are evidence of** (the author asked about fitting to the test data, 23 November 2026): the rules were designed from the Rat's wrong points and measured on the same recording (its 12 cases are 12 starts in one trial; the nine runs vary settings, not data), so 290 to 158 is a fit to that trial, not a forecast; on data they were not made on they do no harm (guineafowl and pig the same in every sweep) and, at the pig's one crossing, no good either; the claim distance and the hold are absolute (pixels, frames) and may not suit much larger dots or a faster camera (PLAN.md, section 13, third round). Tests: `xma-vision` `joint_tracking` unit tests (`a_marker_found_again_does_not_share_a_dot`, `a_dot_where_another_marker_is_expected_is_claimed`, `markers_share_a_dot_only_where_they_overlap`, `identity_out_of_an_overlap_is_not_guessed`, `one_dot_out_of_an_overlap_goes_to_the_marker_it_fits`, `two_decisions_that_disagree_give_nothing`, `no_point_while_touching_leaves_a_consistent_result`, `settling_a_changed_marker`, `an_exchange_is_taken_only_with_exactly_the_other_picks`), `review15b_joint_hostile.rs` (extended), `review17_joint.rs`; `xma-engine/tests/multi_overlap.rs` (with `an_overlap_is_held_overlap_max_frames_steps_after_the_last_touch`), `review17_overlap.rs`, and in `multi_tracking.rs` `multi_rat_overlap_does_not_hand_a_dot_over` and `multi_pig_crossing_both_directions`. |
| A221 | Multi-camera tracking (A218): what a run does to a point ahead of it that it does not find again. Until this row, for a marker the run was following, every such point that the run may overwrite was reset to undefined ("an older point the run does not confirm is not left looking tracked", a rule set after review 15b). Unlike XMALab's kinds, this kind carries on past the frame where it loses a marker, so it went on resetting that marker's points in that camera for as long as the marker stayed lost. The author, 24 November 2026, after tracking the example pig project (which holds tracked points at frames 1 to 93) forward and then backward in the window: "It overwrites previously tracked points, which makes sense in some ways, but it's overwriting good tracking for no reason i can see." Measured on the program as it was: the forward run erased 17 of marker 5's earlier points (16 of them in camera 1), 14 within 2 px of the hand-finished project; the backward run over that result then erased the points at frames 1 to 6, which the forward run had started from, and frame 69, all of them right. On the Rat a backward run over a forward run's result erased 3,359 of its points, 3,320 of them within 2 px of the author's and 2 more than 5 px off, and left 50.8 % of the author's points where the forward run alone had left 67.2 % (`oracle/py/retrack.py --multi keep_unconfirmed=0`). | No C++ counterpart: the method is new in this program (A218). XMALab's own kinds stop following a marker at the frame where it fails and leave the later frames as they were | Measured on the merged tree: `oracle/py/retrack.py` (new: tracking over a project that already holds points, forward and then backward over the result, or over the plain tracker's result; each pass scored against the hand-finished project and compared with the project before it; `oracle/DATASETS.md`, "Measuring a tracking result"), with the rule and its second exception each switched off; `python3 oracle/py/heldout.py all --jitter` with the rule on and off (all 216 result files of the nine runs byte-identical: those start projects hold nothing ahead of a run). Tests `xma-engine/tests/multi_keep_unconfirmed.rs`, the `_keep` tests beside each test that pinned the old rule (`review15b_multi_leak.rs`, `review15d_multi_rules.rs`, `multi_tracking.rs`; the old tests run unchanged with the rule off), `xma-app/tests/multi_keep_console.rs`, `xma-cli/tests/track_multi.rs`. Independent review 18 (24 November 2026; its tests are in the tree, `xma-engine/tests/review18_keep.rs` and `xma-app/tests/review18_console.rs`): it repeated every figure here, read every place a step reads the project and found no way for a kept point to reach what a run writes (also with three cameras, and with every stored 3D point wiped from two start projects), removed each rule from the code in turn and saw a test fail, and found one fault, fixed: exception (a) kept an older point that could not be triangulated with the run's own point at all (a value far outside the image, as a hand-edited file can carry). No reference run (PLAN.md section 13) | Improvement of A218 (the author, 24 November 2026, to the proposal that a run stop erasing what it cannot find again: "Yes, build both"). Default mode only: `MultiTrackParams::keep_unconfirmed` (`xma track --multi keep_unconfirmed=0`) gives the rule before, byte for byte; the other kinds and the XMALab 3 mode are not touched. The second exception below is a small trade with a switch of its own (`keep_dot_rule`): it is on, as proposed and approved; whether it stays on was put to the author on 24 November 2026 with these figures | **The rule.** For a marker the run has had, a point at the frame a step ends on, in a camera the run tracks, that the run may overwrite and gave no point of its own is left exactly as it is (point, status, size), unless the run's own points of that step contradict it: (a) the run wrote a point of the same marker in another camera, and the older point cannot be triangulated with it or the 3D point from the two has a reprojection error above `max_reprojection_cap` (3 px, the most the method accepts for its own points); or (b) the run wrote a point of another marker in the same camera within `same_dot` (1.5 px) of it, and does not hold the two as touching there; then it is reset as before. Only points written in that step count, not points set by hand. A stored point that is not finite is reset. The run never reads a kept point, so the points it writes are the same with the rule on or off, and every point the rule removes the old rule removed too. A marker the run never had, points set by hand, and hidden or uncalibrated cameras are untouched, as before. **Measured** (`retrack.py`, the second pass of each case; "right" is within 2 px of the hand-finished point, "wrong" more than 5 px off): example pig project forward, nothing removed, 3 kept (3 right), marker 5 empty in camera 1 at frames 37, 40, 41, 47 and 49 only; then backward, nothing removed, 6 kept, marker 5 empty at frame 49 only and 1,711 of the 1,715 hand-finished cells of its six markers filled (99.8 %; 98.4 % within 1 px; one more than 5 px off, a point set by hand in the project itself, marker 5 in camera 1 at frame 64). Pig from a clean start, backward over forward: 15 kept (11 right, 4 where the hand-finished project has none), none removed. Rat, backward over forward: 3,327 kept (3,291 right, 2 wrong), 32 removed (29 right, none wrong), 70.3 % of the author's points afterwards against 67.2 % after the forward run alone. Guineafowl, backward over forward: 3 kept, none removed, 95.6 % against 76.4 %. **What keeping costs** (the plain tracker's result on the Rat from frame 1, 3,574 points of which 167 wrong, then this kind over it): 3,235 replaced, 273 kept (250 right, 9 wrong), 28 removed (2 right, 24 wrong); 23 wrong points afterwards, where the old rule left 14 and erased 252 right ones. **The second exception is a trade.** Rule (b) removed all 32 on the Rat's backward pass, and 29 of them were right: two markers that really share a dot in that camera (the author has them 0 to 1.5 px apart), where the run gave the dot to one and had the other in no camera at that frame, so it did not know they touch. Over the plain tracker the same rule removed 23, of which 20 wrong: points the plain tracker had slid onto another marker's dot. With rule (b) off (`keep_dot_rule=0`): none removed on the backward pass, and 42 wrong points left over the plain tracker. An exemption for any overlap the run still holds, not only for markers touching now, was built and measured and not kept: it still removed 17 of the 32 (16 right) and left 40 wrong over the plain tracker. These figures rest on two cases of one recording (55 cells); on the pig and the guineafowl nothing is removed in any form. **Reporting.** A step reports the points kept and removed (`StepReport::kept_older`, `removed_older`); `xma track` and the window's Console line give the totals (U168). "Lost" in the Console is now decided from the run's own record, not from whether a cell holds a point. **Limits.** A wrong older point that agrees with the other camera (one slid along its line of sight) and is on no dot the run gave to another marker stays (the 9 above). A run of one step repeated ("next frame") starts each step from whatever the cell at its start frame holds, so a kept point becomes the next single step's start point, where one continuous run never reads it. Review 18 measured what that can cost on a scene made for it (the Rat from frame 150 to 200 with every cell ahead holding the next marker's hand-finished point, a real dot and the wrong one): one continuous run ends with 202 of those wrong points kept among 1,629, single steps with 345 among 1,516, the old rule with none among 1,422 and 1,093. The same holds for a run stopped and started again. Exception (a) needs the run's own point of the marker in another camera at that frame; with the marker found in no camera, only (b) can remove a point. |
| A222 | Multi-camera tracking (A218, A219): the test that a candidate dot has the contrast of a bead. It compared the dot's contrast in grey levels with the median of the marker's last ten points in that camera, and refused a dot under 0.6 of it. A bead takes away a share of the light round it, so its contrast in grey levels rises and falls with the brightness of the picture there; and the comparison is not updated while the marker is lost, so a marker refused once stays refused until the picture changes back. The author, 24 November 2026, on the pig in the window: "there's quite big gaps for marker 5, and there's different gaps running forward vs backward". Measured on the images: round marker 5 in camera 1 the picture brightens at frames 7 to 15 (90 to 159 grey levels) and again at 70 to 81, and the marker's contrast goes from 25 to 50 and back; its contrast as a share of the brightness stays between 0.16 and 0.33 throughout. Going forward the run lost the marker at frame 16 (the project's own points at 16, 18 to 22, 54 to 59 and 61 went with it, A221) and, but for frames 17 and 23, had it again only from 60, and lost it again at 82 to 84 after the second bright spell (the project's points there went too); going backward it lost it on the other side of each bright spell (frames 1 to 6, and 69). The rule that compares the contrast as a share of the brightness (`contrast_relative`, A219) was built on 22 November and left off by the author's decision of that day, on the advice of review 16. | No C++ counterpart: the method is new in this program (A218) | The images (`python3 oracle/py/marker_contrast.py 5 1`: the brightness round the marker and its contrast, frame by frame, at the hand-finished points); the nine-run means with the rule off and on (`python3 oracle/py/heldout.py all --jitter`, 24 November 2026, the same as on 23 November); `oracle/py/retrack.py` on the example pig project; `oracle/py/pig_crossing_truth.py` for the crossing; `oracle/py/wrong_points.py` on the Rat's first run of each. Nothing else about the rule or its two guards (A219) changed. By case the Rat moves both ways: from frame 150 forward 58.0 % where it was 63.3 %, from frames 1 to 201 79.5 % where it was 82.8 % (the test's floor of 81 % stays for the old rule; the default's floor is 78 %), from 225 backward 50.3 % where it was 48.4 %. The kinds of wrong point on the Rat are the same with either rule (`wrong_points.py`, the first run: 159 and 164). **The pig, as the author ran it.** On the example project, forward: marker 5 is empty in camera 1 at frames 37, 40, 41, 47 and 49, all inside the crossing of markers 5 and 9 (frames 34 to 53), where it was empty at 16, 18 to 22, 24 to 59, 61 and 82 to 84; after the backward pass, at frame 49 only. **The extra points.** The guineafowl's eleven more are the cells looked at on 22 November (the marker itself at the picture's edge, a frame or two outside the author's points: `TMTmid` at frames 16, 428 and 429, `TMTtop` at 25 and 26), reached by more of the ten runs. The pig's are marker 5 in camera 1 inside the crossing, which the hand-finished project leaves empty. **Inside the crossing the points are not centred.** The two markers are 4 to 5 px apart there and make one dark shape.
