Points on a merged shape are to be looked over by hand under either rule (A220, limit 3). **What it is evidence of.** The cause is physical and the rule is a change of units, not a tuned number (the fraction is the same 0.6); but each recording is one trial, and on the one with many markers close together the rule is no better. |
| A223 | Multi-camera tracking (A218, A220, A222): where a marker's dot touches another dark dot in one camera. Each candidate is refined by the marker's detection, which thresholds the picture; two dots 4 to 5 px apart come out as one shape, and the point is that shape's centre, between the two (A222's notes: about half of marker 5's points inside the pig's crossing lie 2 to 3 px off). The author, 25 November 2026: "Is the crux of the issue with crossing points. Anything clever we can do to keep the marker on the right point as two blobs merge/get close?" Looked at on the example pig project: the bead that marker 5 passes in camera 1 at frames 34 to 53 is not a marker of that project (it is marker 9 of the hand-finished ten-marker project), so the rules of A220, which know only the run's own markers, never applied there; and the two dots stay two dark cores throughout (each about 1.4 px wide as a Gaussian, 4 to 5 px apart). New: an option that fits the two dots apart (off by default when built, on since 26 November 2026). | No C++ counterpart: the method is new in this program (A218) | A trial in Python before building (scratch under `target/crossing/`, not kept in the tree; its figures in PLAN.md section 13, fifth round). Tests: `xma-vision/src/dot_fit.rs` (synthetic pictures: one dot; two dots 2.5 to 4.5 px apart with noise; a dot beside an edge; an elongated dot; a sloping ground; light dots; 16-bit values; a third dot; the shape that must cover both centres; hostile arguments), `xma-engine/tests/multi_split_touching.rs` (synthetic scenes on the pig's calibration), `xma-engine/tests/multi_split_pig.rs` (the pig's crossing against the truth from the rigid bodies, `oracle/py/pig_crossing_truth.py`; off is the program before, by a pinned hash), `xma-app/tests/multi_split_console.rs`, `xma-cli/tests/track_multi.rs`, and the review's `review19_dot_fit.rs`, `review19_split_run.rs`, `review19_split_pig.rs`. Measurements of 25 November 2026, each made when the change was built and repeated twice, once by the review: the nine-run means off and on (`python3 oracle/py/heldout.py all --jitter [--multi split_touching=1]`); `python3 oracle/py/retrack.py --multi split_touching=1 --gaps` and `oracle/py/pig_crossing_score.py` on its results; `oracle/py/split_cells.py` (where a run fitted dots apart and how those points score). `retrack.py`'s Rat cases were also run under the nine jitter settings. With the option off all 216 result files of the nine runs are byte-identical to the program before. Independent review 19, two looks (25 November 2026; PLAN.md section 13 gives both in full): the first found the first build firing on dots that do not touch and moving a marker between two beads, and judged it not fit to offer; the second, of the rule as it now is, found those gone, repeated every figure here, and found faults A and B of the Notes, each with a test that fails (`xma-engine/tests/review19b_rat.rs`, `review19b_guineafowl.rs`, kept and ignored). No reference run (PLAN.md section 13) | Addition to A218, **on by default since 26 November 2026** (the author that day, after trying the options in the window on the Rat recording: "Set the seperate touching dots, recognize lost marker, place lost marker, and remove old points to default on"; H12; the window's boxes: U171; a run with the defaults is byte for byte the run the option gave before, 24 of 24 result files). Built off by default (the author, 25 November 2026, to the proposal: "Build it"). `MultiTrackParams::split_touching`; `xma track --multi split_touching=1`; in the window the check box "Separate touching dots" (U169). Review 19 judged it fit to offer as an option and not yet fit to be the default (faults A and B open). Whether it becomes the default is the author's to say. Default mode only | **The method** (`xma_vision::dot_fit`, new in this program). The picture near a dot is described as a sloping ground minus one Gaussian dot per bead. The run learns each marker's dot width in each camera at its clean points (the median of the last ten). At each refined candidate of a marker with a known width where the detection found a shape: one dot of that width is fitted at the detected point; what it leaves is searched for a second dot, between 1.5 px and the reach less one width away (reach: 3 widths + 4 px) and at least 0.35 times as deep; two dots are fitted together, centres and depths free; they are kept when the fainter is at least 0.35 of the deeper, they are at least 1.5 px apart, neither moved more than 4 px and their misfit is at most 0.80 of the one-dot misfit on the same pixels; when the detected point lies nearer the middle of the two than either centre; when the detected shape covers both centres (each within the shape's radius of the detected point: the detected size for the contour methods, half of it for the blob-detector methods, whose size is a diameter); and when three dots do not explain the picture clearly better than two (the same 0.80 and 0.35). Only then do the two fitted centres replace the detected point as candidates (each within `max_detection_shift` of the correlation peak, its contrast tested on the picture with the other fitted dot taken out); the joint choice decides which is the marker. In every other case the candidate is the detected point exactly as without the option. The fit is tried before the check that a detection may not move more than `max_detection_shift` from its peak: at a joined shape the detection jumps 3 to 4.5 px from the marker's own dot to the shape's centre, and that check, applied first, dropped the candidate. It works from the picture alone, so the second dot need not be a marker. A point written from a fitted centre has status tracked and size -1, counts as a merged point (A220: with `overlap_clean`, no template, contrast or width is learned from it, and its frame gives no depth to a lost marker and no rigid statistics), and is counted in the run's report (U169). The numbers came from the trial and were not moved. **The first build** had the first four tests only and did harm (Rat 428 more than 5 px off a run against 174; guineafowl 71.9 % found against 82.5 %): a separate dot 6 to 8 px away counted as touching, two thirds of the Rat's fitted points and all the guineafowl's were of that kind, and each replaced a correct detection by a point nothing was learned from. Review 19 found it; the tests of "really joined" are the answer. **At the pig's crossing** (camera 1, frames 34 to 53, 20 cells; within 2 px of the truth from the rigid bodies / 2 to 5 px / empty; none more than 5 px off). Example project (the other bead is not a marker), forward: 19 / 0 / 1 where it is 7 / 8 / 5 with the option off; then backward: 19 / 0 / 1 where it is 9 / 10 / 1. From the clean start, where both beads are markers, after both passes: marker 5 19 / 0 / 1 where it is 12 / 3 / 5, marker 9 19 / 0 / 1 where it is 4 / 7 / 9. No point is on the other bead (up to three are with the option off). The empty frame is 43, where the detection lands on the other bead. The method was shaped on this crossing. **Over the three recordings** (nine-run means, range in brackets; option off, then on). Rat: 68.2 % found (66.7 to 68.7) with 174 more than 5 px off (145 to 248), then 70.4 % (69.8 to 71.5) with 189 (168 to 242). Guineafowl: 82.5 %, then 82.6 %, none more than 5 px off either way. Pig: 99.7 % with 68 extra, then 99.8 % with 101 extra (the crossing cells the hand-finished project leaves empty). About 1,050 points a run are fitted on the Rat (62 % within 2 px of the hand-finished point, 2 % more than 5 px off; 934 have another hand-finished marker within 6 px), 84 on the guineafowl. **Forward then backward on the Rat** (`retrack.py` from frame 1, under the nine jitter settings): 68.4 % of the hand-finished points after both passes (66.8 to 72.5) with 16 more than 5 px off, where it is 69.8 % (65.0 to 74.0) with 21 without the option; six of the nine end at 67 %, three at 70.0 to 72.5 %. The six are **fault A** (review 19, second look): marker 16 crosses marker 8 in camera 2 at frames 250 to 262; its points at frames 253 to 255 are fitted apart (2.2 to 2.5 px from the hand-finished ones), and it is then lost in camera 2 from frame 256 and in both cameras from 271 to the end, where the program without the option keeps it to frame 448; the backward pass starts without it. Why is not known yet. **Fault B** (the same review): in the guineafowl's camera 2 the marker TMTtop is a faint dot on a dark diagonal band; at frames 248, 249, 259 and 261 the program without the option has the hand-finished point exactly and the option fits a point 2.5 to 2.7 px along the band, 32 times a run. **Cost.** The Rat's 449 frames take 31 s where they take 6.7 s; the pig's 145 frames 4.5 s where they take 3.0 s. **Known limits.** Dots closer than about 2.3 widths (about 3.3 px for the pig's beads) are not told apart (the joined centre is taken as before). Tracking one frame at a time gets no benefit (on the pig's crossing 6 of 20 cells within 2 px, where one continuous run has 19 and stepping without the option 7): a run of one step starts from the fitted point of the step before and learns from it. A run that starts while the marker touches something has no width for it. Two dots only. Faults A and B. The test that the shape covers both centres is checked on data for detection method 0 only (no recording uses the blob-detector or centre-of-mass methods, and no test fails when the halving for the blob detector is removed). **What it is evidence of.** That two round beads passing 4 to 5 px apart can be told apart from the picture, on one crossing of one recording, the one the method was shaped on; and that on the Rat it finds more points at about as many wrong ones over 24 single passes, while losing one marker after one crossing in most forward runs from frame 1. Three recordings, one trial each. |
| A224 | Multi-camera tracking (A218, A219): how a marker that was lost is recognised when it is looked for again. Its distances to its neighbours had to match those at one earlier frame (the reference frame of its rigid prediction) within 2 %, besides the links the run has learned for pairs it holds as rigid. Counted on the three recordings on 25 November 2026 (PLAN.md section 13, "Why the multi-camera kind misses points"): on the Rat 65 % of the hand-finished points the method misses lie in 74 losses that never end; where the right dots were offered in every camera and refused, links from the reference frame did it; and the Rat's markers on different bones or on skin do not keep their distances to 2 % (marker 16 to its three nearest neighbours, from the hand-finished 3D points: all three within 2 % at 84 % of frames one frame later, 26 % ten frames later, 14 % thirty frames later). New: an option (off by default when built, on since 26 November 2026) that recognises a lost marker by the run's own statistics of each pair's distance. | No C++ counterpart: the method is new in this program (A218) | A replay of the idea on the program's own Rat runs before building (scratch under `target/crossing/fa/`, not kept in the tree; its figures in PLAN.md section 13, sixth round). Tests: `xma-engine/tests/multi_found_by_distances.rs` (synthetic scenes on the pig's calibration: `a_marker_that_moved_on_a_rigid_cluster_is_recognised_by_its_statistics`, `two_markers_lost_together_do_not_take_each_others_dots`, and A225's scene), `xma-vision/tests/review15b_joint_hostile.rs`, `xma-app/tests/multi_found_window.rs` (U170). Measurements of 25 and 26 November 2026: the nine-run means (`python3 oracle/py/heldout.py all --jitter --multi stat_links=1[,distance_placement=1][,split_touching=1]`), made by the builder, re-scored by review 21 from the builder's result files, and repeated by it with a binary of the merged code (216 of 216 files byte-identical); `python3 oracle/py/retrack.py --multi ... --gaps` (one run each); where the recognitions and the wrong points lie, from a trace kept as patches outside the tree. With the option off all 216 result files of the nine runs are byte-identical to the program before (builder and review). Independent review 21 (26 November 2026; PLAN.md section 13): no defect found by reading or by its own tests, every figure here repeated; its concerns are in the Notes. The full check passes on this state (`XMA_REQUIRE_ORACLE=1 cargo xtask check`, 26 November 2026: 442 test programs, none failed). No reference run (PLAN.md section 13) | Addition to A218, **on by default since 26 November 2026** (the author that day, after trying the options in the window on the Rat recording: "Set the seperate touching dots, recognize lost marker, place lost marker, and remove old points to default on"; H12; the window's boxes: U171; a run with the defaults is byte for byte the run the option gave before, 24 of 24 result files). Built off by default (the author, 25 November 2026, to the count: "Yes, next round"). `MultiTrackParams::stat_links`; `xma track --multi stat_links=1`; in the window the check box "Recognise a lost marker by its distances" (U170). It finds more points and puts more on a wrong dot: whether it becomes the default is the author's to say. Default mode only | **The rule.** The run keeps, for every pair of its markers, the mean and spread of their 3D distance over the frames it has tracked (the statistics its learned links are made from). For a marker being found again, every other marker of the run whose pair with it has been measured at `rigid_min_frames` (10) frames or more gives a link: the pair's mean distance, with a tolerance of the larger of `rigid_sigmas` (4) spreads and `rigid_min_tolerance` (1 %) of the mean, whether or not the pair counts as rigid (for a rigid pair this is the learned link it already has). The rigid prediction makes no reference-frame link for such a pair; a pair measured at fewer frames keeps its 2 % link. The check itself is unchanged: `reacquire_links` (2) partners kept and none broken. Followed markers are checked as before. No new number. **Over the three recordings** (nine-run means of the 24 cases, range in brackets; found, then points more than 5 px off per run of the Rat's twelve cases). The program as it is: 68.2 % (66.7 to 68.7), 174 (145 to 248). This option alone: 75.0 % (73.0 to 75.7), 325 (288 to 382). A225 alone: 78.3 % (77.8 to 78.5), 483 (418 to 538). Both: 80.2 % (79.4 to 80.8), 530 (449 to 655), 96.9 % of the points within 1 px where it is 97.2 %. Both with A223's option: 81.3 % (81.0 to 81.8), 335 (268 to 425), where A223's alone gives 70.4 % and 189. The guineafowl (82.5 %, none more than 5 px off, 21 extra) and the pig (99.7 %, none, 68 extra) are the same in every row without A223 and as with A223 alone with it: the guineafowl's bone has two markers, and neither rule acts there. **Forward then backward on the Rat** (`retrack.py` from frame 1, one run each): with both options 86.4 % of the hand-finished points after both passes with 96 more than 5 px off; with A223's as well 86.6 % with 62; as it is 70.3 % with 19. Over the plain tracker's result: 85.6 % with 95, 86.0 % with 63, against 68.8 % with 23. The pig and the guineafowl end as they did. **The recognitions are right; the wrong points come after** (the default setting's twelve Rat cases, both options). Found again from a placement (A225): 130 within 2 px of the hand-finished point, 5 at 2 to 5 px, 1 further. From a rigid prediction: 451, 61 and 6, where the program as it is has 300, 41 and none. The points more than 5 px off begin mostly in a marker that was found again within the 30 frames before and is then followed: 37 episodes holding 293 points, where the program as it is has 8 holding 27. Traced on 26 November 2026 (nothing built): they are a few places counted in several cases (marker 15 beside 16 in camera 1 at about frames 106 to 190; marker 9 beside 16 or 7 in camera 2 at about 217 to 261; marker 16 beside 2 in camera 1 at about 298 to 330), where the marker's dot touches another marker's dot in one camera (centres 5 to 8 px apart). The marker was lost there for that reason; found again there, its own dot stops giving a candidate within a few frames, its point slides off centre with the template cut anew at each step, and it settles on the neighbour's dot, which is free because the neighbour is lost (29 of the 37). 24 of the 37 episodes (226 points) are this; 11 (65 points) start from a recognition that was already 2 px or more off in that camera; 2 are other things. The program as it is has the same fault in its 8 episodes: the options expose it by finding markers again where touching dots lost them. An old template and a poor 3D prediction after the gap were looked for and are not the cause. A223's option, made for touching dots, takes the wrong points from 530 to 335. Three crude experiments on what happens after a recognition (no template renewal for 30 frames; every camera checked as when finding again; back to lost when a camera misses) were run once each and none is a remedy: the last cut the Rat's wrong points to 142 and its points found to 73.9 %, and took the guineafowl from 82.5 % to 66.7 %. **Review 21's concerns.** The links are loose for markers that are not on one bone: by its estimate from the hand-finished 3D points the tolerance is about ±16 % of the distance at the median for the Rat's 116 pairs that are not rigid (11 to 26 % for the middle half), so two such links are kept by many dots; it holds this the likely cause of the added wrong points, which the counts above do not bear out for the recognitions themselves. The links go to every marker of the run, so of two markers found again at one step the one served later counts the one served earlier. Four parts of the code can be removed without a test failing (the near-singular test and the step-halving of `point_from_distances`, the start's shift and the weighting in the placement); those tests are not written. **Fault A of A223** (marker 16 lost for the rest of a forward run after it crosses marker 8) does not occur with both options on: over the nine settings marker 16 is found again at frame 262 in all eight runs that reach the crossing. **Cost.** The Rat's 449 frames take 4.4 to 4.8 s of the program's own step time with both options where they take 3.6 to 3.7 s. **Known limits.** The guineafowl's losses are not covered. The fault traced above. With `rigid_min_frames` 0 no statistics are kept and the option does nothing. A run of one step has no statistics but those of the frames behind its start. **What it is evidence of.** That on one recording with nineteen markers on several bones and on skin, most of the markers the method lost for good can be found again on their own dots from the distances the run has itself measured, at the price of more points on a wrong dot where two markers' dots touch; nothing about recordings with few markers. Three recordings, one trial each; no number was moved to these totals. |
| A225 | Multi-camera tracking (A218, A219, A224): where a lost marker is looked for. It was looked for round its rigid prediction only: the position its nearest neighbours' movement gives it since the latest frame at which it and three followed neighbours had clean 3D points, and nowhere when those neighbours do not fit one rigid motion (to 2 %) or no such frame exists. Counted on 25 November 2026: on the Rat 56 % of the missed hand-finished points are at steps where the marker was not searched for at all (26,458 of 51,234 for want of a rigid fit), and where a rigid prediction exists it is within 6 px of the hand-finished point at 74 % of steps when its reference frame is 1 to 5 frames old and 22 % beyond 50. New: an option (off by default when built, on since 26 November 2026) that places such a marker by its distances. | No C++ counterpart: the method is new in this program (A218) | As A224 (the same replay, measurements, trace and review). Tests: `xma-vision/tests/point_from_distances.rs` (exact recovery from exact distances; the mirror point not taken; hostile arguments), the review's `xma-vision/tests/review21_point_from_distances.rs` (partners in one plane; the result is a minimum; subnormal and huge values, a start on a partner, a million links), `xma-engine/tests/multi_found_by_distances.rs` (`a_lost_marker_whose_neighbours_are_not_rigid_is_placed_by_its_distances`: never found again with the options off, found on its own dot with them on; `two_markers_lost_together_do_not_take_each_others_dots`), `xma-app/tests/multi_found_window.rs` (U170) | Addition to A218, **on by default since 26 November 2026** (the author that day, after trying the options in the window on the Rat recording: "Set the seperate touching dots, recognize lost marker, place lost marker, and remove old points to default on"; H12; the window's boxes: U171; a run with the defaults is byte for byte the run the option gave before, 24 of 24 result files). Built off by default (the author, 25 November 2026: "Yes, next round"). `MultiTrackParams::distance_placement`; `xma track --multi distance_placement=1`; in the window the check box "Place a lost marker by its distances" (U170). Whether it becomes the default is the author's to say, with A224. Default mode only | **The rule.** Where a lost marker has no rigid prediction and no camera still follows it on its own, it is placed at the point that best keeps its pairs' mean distances to the markers followed in 3D at this step whose pair with it the run has measured at `rigid_min_frames` (10) frames or more; four or more are needed (four distances fix a point, three leave two). The point is found by weighted least squares (`xma_vision::joint_tracking::point_from_distances`, new: Gauss-Newton with a cap on its steps, through `xma_vision::linalg`; each distance weighted by one over the square of the larger of the pair's spread and 1 % of its mean), started from the marker's latest clean 3D point within `depth_gap` moved by what its nearest partner has moved since. The marker is searched for round that point's projection as round a rigid prediction (gate `gate_rigid`, 6 px) and recognised by A224's links to the partners it was placed from, with or without A224's option (a placement has no reference frame). Where a rigid prediction exists it is used as before. No new number. **Measured**: the figures, the accuracy of the recognitions, the fault traced and the review's concerns are in A224. A look that is not a proposal: with the placement searched within 12 px instead of 6 the Rat gives 81.4 % (80.1 to 81.8) with 525 (440 to 595). **Known limits.** It needs four followed partners with statistics, so it does nothing on the guineafowl (two markers), whose losses need a search from one partner's distance; nothing is placed for a marker with no clean 3D point within `depth_gap` (300 frames). The fit of the placement is not tested: a placement that keeps no distance is still searched round, and the links are what refuse a wrong dot there (review 21). |
| A226 | Merge-aware tracking (XMALab 3's "Enable merge-aware tracking": the plain 2D tracker with a monitor that holds overlapping markers on their epipolar lines and assigns them when they separate; tracking kinds 5 and 6) is not in this program, in either mode. It was ported in phase 3 and matched XMALab on its three reference runs and on the Rat; the multi-camera kind (A218 to A225) has since overtaken it on every recording that has hand-finished points. Removed with it: its six compatibility switches and the differences they recorded (A84, A85, A96, A97, A215; A63 was a note on its code), `xma track --kind merge-selected` and `merge-all`, and the window's controls (U171). | `processing/MergeMonitor.cpp`; `ui/WizardDigitizationFrame.cpp` (`trackMerge`, `checkIfValidMerge`) | Measured on 26 November 2026 before removing, one run of each kind on the 24 cases against the hand-finished points (`python3 oracle/py/heldout.py all --kinds all,merge-all` with the program before, and `--multi split_touching=1,stat_links=1,distance_placement=1`; results under `target/crossing/supersede/`, not kept in the tree). Found, then points more than 5 px off: merge-aware Rat 32.8 %, 2,518; guineafowl 76.4 %, 303 (and 104 points where the author has none); pig 97.0 %, 0 (73). Multi-camera with the options of A223 to A225: 81.3 %, 347; 82.6 %, 0 (21); 99.8 %, 0 (101). Plain "all points": 28.7 %, 3,724; 62.1 %, 174; 97.3 %, 48. At the pig's crossing of markers 5 and 9 in camera 1 (frames 34 to 53, 40 cells; `oracle/py/pig_crossing_score.py`), where merge-aware tracking run forward was the best there was on 23 November: merge-aware forward 40 within 5 px (30 within 2 px), backward 2 and both markers lost for good; multi-camera forward 38 within 2 px and 2 empty, backward 31 within 2 px and 9 empty, none wrong. What merge-aware tracking did that the multi-camera kind does not: it left fewer cells empty on the guineafowl (a point in every cell in six of the ten starts, against 93 to 99 %), with the 303 wrong points among them. Tests: `xma-cli/tests/track.rs` (`merge_aware_kinds_are_refused`), `xma-cli/tests/tracking_parity.rs` (`merge_runs_are_not_compared`, `parity_command_reports_r06_as_not_compared`; the eleven other reference runs compare exactly as before), `xma-io` `settings_entry` (`retired_merge_settings_read_and_written_as_before`), `xma-app/tests/tracking_page_26nov.rs`. Removed: three source files of `xma-engine` (3,718 lines), ten test programs that tested only this kind (59 tests), and the merge-aware cases of the test files that mixed kinds. Independent review 23 (26 November 2026) read the change ignoring whitespace: in the tracking step, the run, the jobs and the history only merge branches, merge fields and comments went, and the six switches left `CppCompat` (122 items to 116) with no other value changed; the eleven other reference runs and the engine's tracking tests pass; `xma parity` decides "not compared" by the run's kind (5 or 6) alone, so no failure of another kind can hide behind it. It found that two of the deleted tests had also asserted something that is not merge-aware tracking (the document's change counter does not move for an edit that changes nothing) and wrote those assertions again (`xma-engine/tests/review23_retired.rs`, which also carries the five retired settings through save and reopen in both modes) | **Removed** (the author, 26 November 2026: "remove the old merge-aware tracking option and code, because it's clearly superceeded by this new one (unless I'm mistaken?)"; measured first, as in the evidence). By that instruction an exception to the rule that the XMALab 3 mode and its tests are frozen: the XMALab 3 mode no longer has this kind, and nothing else of it changed | The reference outputs stay (`oracle/tracking_runs.json` r06 to r08, the Rat's runs of kinds 5 and 6, the reports under `docs/parity/`): `xma parity` reports such a run as "not compared" with the reason and exits as for a clean comparison; `xma parity tier-c` and `tools/parity_reports.py` list them so. Project files are byte for byte as before: the five thresholds (`MergeEpipolarThreshold`, `MergeAuditThreshold`, `MergeMaxPendingFrames`, `MergeMaxHoldFrames`, `MergeMaxOverlapFrames`) are still read from and written to a project's settings entry (`xma_model::RetiredSettings`) and nothing reads them; the machine's settings file drops them when it is loaded. A user who wants merge-aware tracking uses XMALab 3: a project tracked there opens here. `docs/cpp-tracking.md` still describes the C++ code |

## Images and video

| # | Item | C++ location | Evidence | Decision | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | Every image is reduced to 8-bit greyscale on load; 16-bit data is scaled by 1/256. | `core/Image.cpp:60,73,193,206` | Confirmed | Fix, compat (the author, 14 November 2026: "process at full depth") | A method choice changed by the author's decision. Decided, not yet built: nothing in phases 1 and 2 runs an algorithm on pixels (the viewer already shows full depth), so this takes effect with detection and tracking in phase 3. Rules: (1) an 8-bit source is processed exactly as XMALab 3 processes it, in both modes, so the port can be checked against the C++ on 8-bit data such as the pig videos; (2) a deeper source (16-bit PNG or TIFF, `.cine` above 8 bits) is processed at its own depth by default, and grey-level parameters stored in 8-bit units (`ThresholdOffset`, `DefaultMarkerThreshold`) are scaled to that depth so existing projects and settings keep their meaning; (3) in the XMALab 3 mode a deeper source is reduced to 8 bits as the C++ does. The full-depth path has no C++ reference: it is to be checked against OpenCV's own 16-bit routines and reviewed independently. To settle then: V6 (calibration and grid images are stored as 8-bit inside the project file), V15 (packed 10-bit `.cine`), and how a 16-bit file holding fewer bits is scaled (the C++ divides by 256 whatever the true depth, which maps 12-bit data to grey levels 0 to 15). No real dataset deeper than 8 bits is available yet. Built in xma-vision (detection, tracker step, polynomial refinement; `xma_vision::depth`): a deeper image enters each algorithm as `f32` on the 8-bit scale, `value / 2^(depth - 8)`, unrounded, and the 8-bit algorithm runs unchanged on those values (same later quantisation points), so grey-level parameters keep their 8-bit meaning without rescaling; an 8-bit image promoted to 10, 12 or 16 bits gives the 8-bit detection and refinement results exactly; the tracker cuts its template with float `getRectSubPix` and correlates with OpenCV's float `matchTemplate` (on promoted pig frames 2 of 401 steps move by 1 px). The float routines are identical to OpenCV on 12- and 16-bit images (`oracle/py/gen_primitives_fulldepth.py`) except `cornerSubPix`, which samples as for 8-bit input on purpose. The XMALab 3 reduction is `xma_vision::depth::reduce_to_u8`, equal to `xma_video::Frame::to_gray8` for every value and depth. **Wired into the engine (phase 3).** `xma_engine::tracking::jobs::algorithm_image_for` is the one place a frame becomes the image an algorithm reads: an 8-bit frame is read as it is in every mode; a deeper frame is read at its own depth in every mode but XMALab 3's (`jobs::full_depth`: `CppCompat != XMALAB3`, the same test that picks the XMALab 3 readers), with samples above `2^depth - 1` saturated as the reduction saturates them, and reduced to 8 bits as the C++ does in the XMALab 3 mode. The tracker's template and step, the detection after tracking, placing a point, the polynomial refinement (`refine_points`) and the Detection Settings preview all take the path the image gives. A template cut from one kind of image and searched for in the other (a source whose frames change depth, such as an image sequence mixing 8-bit and 16-bit files, in the default mode) runs on the full-depth path with the 8-bit side taken over exactly (template as `f32`, image as samples of depth 8). Measured (`crates/xma-engine/tests/fulldepth_tracking.rs`): a 16-bit copy of the pig trial (frames times 256, 16-bit PNG): XMALab 3 mode, a whole "track all" run from frame 1 equals the 8-bit run exactly; default mode, detection at all 2844 stored points of `pig_clean`, 293 refinements, `refine_points` over 11 frames, placing a point and 80 previews equal the 8-bit results exactly, and the whole run differs from the 8-bit run in 9 of 2748 points (positions by at most 0.71 px, and their sizes; no status differs; it was 5 points and 0.5 px before the tracker's penalty was centred on the sub-pixel prediction, A64). A synthetic 12-bit trial with markers 0.4 of an 8-bit grey level deep (4 well-separated markers, 2 cameras, 39 steps): default mode, 312 of 312 points tracked, all within 1 px of the truth (worst 0.82 px); XMALab 3 mode, 38 points left, 20 of them within 1 px (the markers vanish in the reduction). `xma track` on a 16-bit trial: `crates/xma-cli/tests/track_deep.rs`. Readers: V15 (packed `.cine`) and V18 (declared depth of 16-bit stills). No real dataset deeper than 8 bits has been tracked yet; the full-depth path in the engine has had no independent review. README, "Deeper images are processed at their own depth". **Review of the full-depth path (14 November 2026):** The corner response method 3 computes at depth (the float image with the 8-bit derivative scale 1/(7 * 255)) is identical to OpenCV's `Sobel` and `boxFilter` with `calcMinEigenVal`'s formula (`cornereig255-fulldepth-*`). The XMALab 3 reduction is `xma_vision::depth::reduce_to_u8`, equal to `xma_video::Frame::to_gray8` for every value and depth. Values above the depth's largest, `2^depth - 1` (a hot pixel; data stored in the high bits of the 16-bit word), are read as that largest value, as the reduction saturates them to 255 (`xma_vision::image::ScaledU16`, the one place deep pixels are read; fixed after the independent review, `crates/xma-vision/tests/fulldepth_review_findings.rs`). Parameters keep their meaning except the blob detector's thresholds, which differ by half a grey level: for a threshold t the reduced path keeps values of at least floor(t) + 0.5 on the 8-bit scale, the deep path values above t. **Depth is not a uniform gain.** Measured on synthetic 12-bit images (`crates/xma-vision/tests/fulldepth_measured.rs`, 600 trials per cell, disc of radius 4; mean error in px, misses in brackets, deep against reduced): method 0, contrast 1, noise 0.5, sloped background 0.80 (49) against 0.82 (141); method 0, contrast 4, noise 0.5, flat 0.39 against 0.52; method 0, contrast 16, no noise, sloped 0.24 against 0.17; method 0, contrast 1, no noise, flat 0.26 against 0.13; method 5, contrast 16, no noise, sloped 0.21 against 0.15; method 3, contrast 4, no noise, flat 0.048 against 0.555; method 3, contrast 4, no noise, sloped 0.64 against 0.33; method 3, contrast 1, no noise, sloped (585) against 0.28 (0); refinement, contrast 1, no noise, flat 0.032 (0) against 0.151 (63); tracker step, contrast 1, noise 0.5, flat 1.00 (84) against 1.09 (275); at noise 2 the two paths are within the trials' scatter. Where depth helps: markers whose contrast is a few 8-bit grey levels or less, noise of half a grey level or more, the corner method on a flat background, the refinement without noise and the tracker on faint markers. Where it does not: strong markers without noise, where the contour methods (0, 5) are on average 0.005 to 0.016 px behind and in a single scene up to 0.07 px behind or ahead, because the reduced path's error depends on the background's grey level relative to the 8-bit steps (method 0, contrast 16: 0.16 to 0.28 px) and the deep path's does not (0.22 to 0.23 px; set by the 3 x 3 median and threshold, not by the blur, the normalisation or the conversion to 8 bits); and faint noise-free markers, which the reduction turns into an already thresholded two-level image. **The corner method (3) on a shaded background is worse at depth**: `cornerSubPix` is pulled by the background's gradient, which the reduction to 8 bits flattens. Misdeclared data: samples in the high bits of a 16-bit word declared at their true depth saturate to a nearly white image (contour methods return a wrong point, the others nothing); a 16-bit file holding fewer bits than declared reads as nearly black (12-bit data as 0 to 16), the contour and corner methods still give the right result at depth and the blob methods find nothing, as in XMALab (`crates/xma-vision/tests/fulldepth.rs`, `misdeclared_depth`). What to check: the largest value in a frame against `2^depth - 1`. |
| V2 | Colour images are converted with `COLOR_RGB2GRAY` although the data is in BGR order, so red and blue weights are swapped. | `core/Image.cpp:66,170,199` | Confirmed (call sites) | Fix, compat | Decided 14 November 2026: correct colour weights (BGR order) by default; the C++ weights are kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode. Implemented by the video package: the default grey is `(R*9798 + G*19235 + B*3735 + 2^14) >> 15` on the true channels (`xma-video/src/convert.rs`); `VideoCompat::XMALAB3` (`grey_weights: GreyWeights::Swapped`) keeps the C++'s swapped weights. Applies to stills, uncompressed colour AVI, MJPEG and the ffmpeg reader. No effect on grey video. |
| V3 | Version 0 `.cine` files: 4-byte frame offsets are read into an 8-byte `unsigned long` on Linux and macOS. | `core/CineVideo.cpp:613-625` | Confirmed on reference data (`video_cine_v0`): no frame of a version 0 file matches its content in the Linux C++ | Fix | The C++ behaviour reads wrong offsets, so it cannot have produced valid data on those systems. Windows behaviour (4-byte `long`) is the correct one. |
| V4 | `.cine` `RecBPP` is uninitialised when `SoftwareVersion` is 720 or below. | `core/CineVideo.cpp:601` | Confirmed | Fix | `RecBPP` is never initialised when `SoftwareVersion <= 720` and is used only for packed 10-bit data (the lookup-table path when it equals 12). Defined as "not 12" (linear `>> 2`; `CineHeader::rec_bpp` is `None`), which matches the Linux C++ on `video_cine_v0`. |
| V5 | H.264 and HEVC frames are decoded by whatever FFmpeg the OpenCV build links, so pixel values and frame counts follow that build. | `core/AviVideo.cpp:40-120` | Reported; measured against OpenCV 4.13 and 4.14 | Open | Measured 14 November 2026: the Rust ffmpeg reader (`-vf scale=flags=bicubic,format=bgr24`, then OpenCV 4.13's `RGB2GRAY` on the BGR bytes) is byte-identical to the C++ dump for all 290 pig frames, and to OpenCV 4.14 `VideoCapture` + `RGB2GRAY` on H.264 8-bit and 10-bit, full range, HEVC, untagged colour and bt709/pc-tagged colour. Still to check against the OpenCV and FFmpeg that the XMALab AppImage links. (Earlier, 13 November 2026: `ffmpeg -pix_fmt gray` gave bytes identical to OpenCV 4.14's greyscale for every frame of both pig videos.) |
| V6 | Calibration and grid images are re-encoded as 8-bit inside the project file. | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp` | Reported | Copy | Needed for XMALab 3 to open projects we save. File > New dataset (package engnew): an 8-bit image (grey or colour) is kept byte for byte (XMALab re-encodes it with OpenCV: the same pixels for TIFF, PNG and BMP, a second JPEG compression for a JPEG); a deeper image is reduced to 8 bits as XMALab reduces it (`Frame::to_gray8`) and encoded in the format its extension names by the `image` crate (`xma_video::image_file::encode_gray8`; a deep colour image is stored grey, XMALab stores it in colour). The bytes are not OpenCV's (a TIFF is not LZW-compressed). |
| V7 | A video's frame count is taken from the container header (`(int)(CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT + 0.45)`), which can exceed the frames that decode. Both pig videos declare 146 frames and decode 145. | `core/AviVideo.cpp`, `AviVideo::reloadFile`, `AviVideo::setActiveFrame` | Confirmed on the pig dataset; behaviour at unreadable frames measured on `video_avi_raw`, `video_cine_b`, `video_seq_odd` | Fix | Decided 14 November 2026: fix. The frame count is `(int)(nb_frames + 0.45)`, or `floor(duration * fps + 0.5)` when `nb_frames` is absent, as OpenCV 4.13's `get_total_frames` (`FrameIndex::cpp_frame_count`), so trials keep their length; `SourceInfo::readable_frames` is the number of packets in the ffprobe index, with discard-flagged packets dropped (the pig videos end in one). At a frame that cannot be read the C++ shows frame 0 (AVI and ffmpeg path: `setActiveFrame` fails, `reloadFile` reopens the capture and reads frame 0, the retry fails again), the last frame read (`.cine` beyond `ImageCount`) or an empty image (missing file in a sequence). Here the reader returns an error, the viewer shows no image and writes one line to the Console. |
| V8 | A video's container is identified by its content, not its extension. The pig videos are named `.avi` but are H.264 in an MP4 container. | `core/Trial.cpp:78-100` (extension chooses the reader class; FFmpeg then identifies the content) | Confirmed on the pig dataset | Copy | Choose the decoder from the file's signature (`xma_video::open::file_signature`). A `.avi` name must not be assumed to be an AVI file. |
| V9 | OpenCV converts decoded frames to BGR with libswscale. The two give different grey on colour or range-tagged video. | OpenCV 4.13 `modules/videoio/src/cap_ffmpeg_impl.hpp`, `CvCapture_FFMPEG::retrieveFrame` | Confirmed on a bt709/pc test clip against OpenCV 4.14 with libswscale 9.5 | Copy (the modern behaviour) | Identical for grey video with untagged or limited range, the X-ray case. Implemented in `xma-video/src/ffmpeg/grey.rs`. |
| V10 | On variable-frame-rate video, OpenCV's `CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES` seek converts the frame number to a time with the average rate and can land one frame late (4 of 73 seeks on the spike's VFR file); sequential reads are right. | OpenCV `modules/videoio/src/cap_ffmpeg_impl.hpp`, `CvCapture_FFMPEG::seek` | Measured in spike 4 | Fix | The Rust reader addresses frames through the packet index (`xma-video/src/ffmpeg/probe.rs`). Affects only what the C++ showed after a seek on VFR files. |

The mixed sequence's JPEG frames are grey, 4:2:0 colour and 4:4:4 colour, and its differences of 2 and 3 levels were not broken down by kind; `video_seq_odd`, which is grey only, differs by 1 level at most. The trial tables that `xma parity` compares do not depend on pixels, so they are unaffected. From phase 3, detection and tracking on a JPEG sequence start from these slightly different pixels, so a comparison of fresh tracking with the C++ on such a sequence carries this difference. |

| V18 | A 16-bit grey still is scaled by 1/256 whatever depth its data has: a 16-bit PNG or TIFF holding 12-bit samples becomes grey levels 0 to 15. OpenCV's `imread` reads neither PNG `sBIT` nor TIFF `MaxSampleValue`. | `core/Image.cpp:60,73`; OpenCV `grfmt_png.cpp`, `grfmt_tiff.cpp` | Confirmed in the source | Fix, compat (follows V1) | Default (`xma_video::image_file`): a 16-bit grey file that declares fewer significant bits is returned at that depth: PNG `sBIT` of 9 to 15 (the samples, which PNG stores scaled up to 16 bits, are shifted back down by `16 - sBIT`), TIFF `MaxSampleValue` from 256 to 65534 (the samples as stored, at the bit length of that value; ignored when a sample exceeds it). Without a declaration, or with one below 9 bits, the depth stays 16; nothing is inferred from the data. Detection and tracking then read the frame at that depth (V1), and the viewer shows it at that depth, so 12-bit data in a 16-bit file is shown at its full range rather than nearly black. XMALab 3 mode (`VideoCompat::still_depth_16`): every 16-bit still is 16 bits deep and reduced by 1/256, as the C++. Whether the files XROMM cameras and their software write carry either field is not known: no such file is available. A grey TIFF with `BitsPerSample` 10, 12 or 14 is not read by the decoder in use (`image` 0.25 maps only 1, 8 and 16-bit grey TIFF); that would need a decoder change (OpenCV reads them). Tests: `crates/xma-video/tests/declared_depth.rs`. |

A later frame of another size is a decode error. Without ffmpeg installed such a file no longer opens. |
| V35 | Still images were decoded with the decoder's size limits switched off, so a damaged header could ask for an allocation of any size (the program is ended at once on a system that does not overcommit memory). | No C++ counterpart | Review 11, second round (20 November 2026); `crates/xma-video/tests/review12_image_limits.rs`, `review12b_image_limits_tiff_bmp.rs` | Fix | A decoded image of more than 2 GiB is refused with a decode error before anything is allocated (PNG, JPEG, TIFF, BMP); the largest frame this program handles is 32 MiB. |
| V36 | An image wider or taller than the graphics device's largest texture (8192 or 16384 pixels on common devices): the graphics library ended the upload thread with a panic, after which that camera's view could wait for ever. | No C++ counterpart (what XMALab does with such an image is not known) | Review 11, second round (20 November 2026); `crates/xma-app/tests/review12c_oversized_still.rs`, `review12b_store_worker_panic.rs` | Fix | The image is not shown: its camera view shows a message giving the image's size and the device's limit, and the Console names the camera once. The upload thread is restarted if it ever ends on an error. Detection, tracking and exports do not use the graphics device and are not affected. |
| V37 | An AVI recording that was cut short. The frame count XMALab shows is the header's (`CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT`, which for AVI is `strh.dwLength`), and it asks for no frame at or above it. A recording stopped abruptly leaves that count at 0, or at the frames of the sections completed so far, although the later frames are in the file: XMALab shows 0 frames, or hides the rest. | `core/AviVideo.cpp`, `AviVideo::reloadFile`, `AviVideo::setActiveFrame` | Measured with `oracle/py/avi_cut_short.py` (a probe built against the OpenCV 4.13 and FFmpeg 8.1.2 of the XMALab AppImage; XMALab itself not started) on the AVI fixtures, the reference AVIs and two recordings made by killing ffmpeg 9.1 while it wrote; review 11 (20 November 2026); two independent reviews | Fix, compat (the author, 21 November 2026: "Count the real frames") | Default mode: every frame really in the file is readable, and the frame count is the header's or the frames found, whichever is larger: a count of 0 or below the frames present gives the frames found; a count above them is kept with the frames past those in the file unreadable, as V7 decided (trials keep their length). The frames are found through the OpenDML index when it holds entries, otherwise `idx1`; then the `movi` lists are scanned from the end of the indexed frame furthest into the file (only when that frame's chunk header agrees with the index) and the frame chunks of the stream found there are appended: the frames of a section the writer had not yet indexed when it stopped (a killed ffmpeg 9.1 file of 1.2 GB: header 275, indexes 274, 307 frames found). With neither index usable all of `movi` is scanned. A RIFF or `movi` size left at 0 is read to the end of the file (sizes beyond the file were already cut at its end; a killed ffmpeg leaves 0xFFFFFFFF). XMALab 3 mode (`VideoCompat::avi_count_from_header`): the header's count and no frame at or above it, as measured (0 frames for a header of 0, also with sizes never filled in); the search after the index runs here too, since XMALab reads such frames when they are below its count. Not reproduced: in a file cut in the middle XMALab also returns a partly stale frame at the cut; this reader fails there. Both modes: an OpenDML super index that was reserved but holds no entry is ignored (before, it gave 0 readable frames); the scan of `movi` steps into `LIST` and `RIFF` headers without recursion (before, a crafted file of 240 KB of nested lists overflowed the stack) and no longer cuts a list's children at the list's declared end, so with a damaged list size it finds more frames than before, and with one damaged frame size inside a `rec ` list fewer (it loses the frames after it, where the old scan recovered at the list's end); neither ever reports a chunk that is not a frame. Every intact file, and every file whose header count is at or above the frames present, reads exactly as before in both modes (fixtures, reference AVIs, ffmpeg-made files up to 3.5 GB with several `RIFF AVIX` lists, audio interleaved and two video streams, compared frame by frame before and after; 478 and 21,000 damaged variants). Not measured against XMALab: an OpenDML file whose `indx` is unusable and whose `idx1` covers the first RIFF list only (now all its frames are found). The OpenDML index is bounded by the file's size in both modes: of each `ix##` chunk only the header and the entries it declares are read, and a super index that names one chunk twice, or overlapping chunks, is unusable (its entries are dropped and the frames come from `idx1` or the scan, so none is counted twice; before, a 3 MB file naming one chunk many times needed more than 256 MiB, and a chunk named twice doubled its frames; found by the second review). Tests: `xma-video/tests/review14_avi_cut_short.rs`, `review14b_avi_counts.rs`, `review14b_avi_scan_depth.rs`, `review14c_avi_unindexed.rs`, `review14d_odml_amplification.rs`, `review14e_odml_layouts.rs`, the unit module `review14c_avi_scan`; `native_fixtures` (`avi_len0.avi`, `_fixed` keys). |

| F1 | MayaCam import reads distortion as one comma-separated line, but export writes one value per line, so only the first coefficient is read (the other seven spin boxes receive uninitialised values). | `ui/ExternalCalibrationFrame.cpp:246` | Confirmed (source); fixed in package engopt (15 November 2026) | Fix (both modes) | Decided 14 November 2026: fix when MayaCam import is ported. `read_mayacam2` reads the eight coefficients one per line, as XMALab's and this program's MayaCams 2.0 export write them, and also comma-separated on one line, as XMALab's reader expects (the stand-in file of c09); fewer than eight is an error (F40). Both modes: XMALab 3 leaves seven values uninitialised, which is not reproduced. Tests `external_calibration.rs` `exported_file_read_back_equal` (with and without a lens model), `f1_comma_separated_coefficients`; reference c09 identical. No C++ reference for the fixed behaviour: independent review pending. |
| F2 | The 2D export header is malformed when offset columns are on: the `cam<j>_offset` fields have no separators and are numbered from 0, and each data row has one extra `0,` field per camera. | `core/Trial.cpp:1719-1725,1757-1763` | Confirmed | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026: do not copy. The Rust program now writes one header field `cam<j+1>_offset` per camera, each followed by a comma, so the header has as many fields as each data row (one-file and per-marker exports; with one camera, that camera's 1-based number). Data rows are unchanged. The C++ header is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `xma export --xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` always uses. No reference dump has offset columns, so the reference data is unaffected. A consequence seen when the 2D import was ported (14 November 2026): the import takes the number of markers from the first line, so re-importing XMALab 3's own export with offset columns (header 40 fields, data rows 42, for 10 markers and 2 cameras) creates 9 markers, not 10. With the corrected header the round trip gives all 10 (`crates/xma-engine/tests/edit_import.rs`). |

The XML attributes written with 6 significant digits (recording speed, cutoff frequencies, mesh scale, grid centre, checkerboard square size, image scale and bias) are now written with `%.17g`, so a save loses nothing. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: XMALab 3 opens such a file without warnings: `crates/xma-io/tests/write_opens_in_xmalab.sh pig_clean` (14 November 2026) reports "XMALab loaded it; manifest warnings: []", and its dump agrees with the dump of XMALab's own save (74 files identical, 25 differ in numbers only, all in the tables listed as allowed). Test `big_project_round_trips` (the model comes back exactly). No effect on parity (no save is compared). Before: Needed for round trips. |

For projects below version 0.3 a `TrackingPenalty` read from the file is now converted from the old scale first, `value * 50 / 125` truncated, then capped by `setMaxPenalty`; a marker without the attribute keeps the default 50. The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Test `version_below_0_3_converts_only_the_penalty`. Before: To check against a real old project if one turns up. |
| F14 | Where the C++ loader carries on after a failure, the Rust reader stops with an error that names the entry and line: a failed CRC or extraction (the C++ loads whatever was extracted), malformed `project.xml` (the C++ loads up to the error and can loop forever), mismatched image resolutions (the C++ shows a dialog and returns a partial project as success), any data file that would make the C++ read out of bounds, and status, method or interpolation codes outside the known set. | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:286-290,1970-1974,2251,2476-2494` | Confirmed in the source (reader port) | Fix | The C++ behaviour here is a partial or undefined load, never valid data. Confirmed on one case: a blank last line in the frame specification makes the C++ crash, and the Rust reader reports the line. One case to revisit in phase 3: a data row that is NaN only in part makes the C++ read past its buffer and the Rust reader reject the project; XMALab never writes such rows, but the 2D import path has not been traced. |

The Rust reader now keeps `Meshmodel`, `DrawMeshmodel` and `MeshScale` whether or not the OBJ file can be read, and records separately whether it could (`RigidBody::mesh_readable`); the writer writes them, so a save no longer loses the mesh. In the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` always uses, the reader drops such a mesh and the writer leaves out a mesh recorded as unreadable, as the C++ does. Measured effect of the default on the reference data: only `v13_mesh_colour_threshold`, `rb_details.csv` body 0 (`has_mesh`, `mesh_path`, `draw_mesh`, `mesh_scale`: the dump holds the C++'s dropped mesh, 0, empty, 0, 1; the default keeps 1, the path, 1, 2.5). For a virtual point defined by coordinates the C++ holds a second point `(0, 0, last z read)`, which is never used or saved. | `core/Marker.cpp:50`, `core/RigidBody.cpp:2002` | Confirmed against the C++ on constructed files (independent review) | Copy | The second point is not held, as it has no effect. |

Intended: nothing is silently left out or overwritten by a save; names that cannot be stored are reported or replaced. |
| F21 | A project stores each calibration image's rotation as a matrix, while the program holds a rotation vector. Every load and save therefore moves the calibration rotations by up to 2.5 units in the last place, which the pose refinement amplifies to about 1e-8 in poses and 1e-5 in rigid-body errors. A project saved and reopened does not reproduce its own numbers exactly, in either program. | `core/CalibrationImage.cpp:612` | Confirmed: XMALab 3's own save shows the same differences against the reference dump as the Rust writer's | Copy (the author, 14 November 2026: "Keep") | This bounds what "the same numbers after saving" can mean: about 1e-5 relative in rigid-body errors, not exact. Measured with `xma recompute -o` and `xma parity` on the result (pig reference): rotation vectors move by 4.4e-16, 3D points by 2.5e-14, projection matrices by 1.8e-12, rigid-body poses by 4.5e-8, rigid-body errors by up to 1.1e-5 px. A saved marker is also no longer flagged for recomputation, so on reopening it is only reprojected (F9), which changes the placeholder projections at frames without a 3D point. |

| F24 | Known differences between a project written by the Rust code and one written by XMALab 3 from the same state: images are carried over byte for byte, not re-encoded (identical for the pig TIFFs; see V6); `log.html` is carried over, not regenerated (`xma_metadata.xml` was too until package p6portal; it is now written at every save as XMALab writes it, below); the compressed bytes differ (a different deflate implementation; unpacked contents are identical); every entry has one timestamp; line endings are `\n` and paths use `/` on every platform, as the Linux build writes. Everything else is byte-identical: 97 of 99 entries of the pig project, the other two being the rotation matrices of F21. | `crates/xma-io/tests/write_cpp.rs` | Confirmed against saves by XMALab 3 | Fix | XMALab 3 opens the files and its dump of them matches its dump of its own save in all 62 tables that do not depend on recomputation. `xma_metadata.xml` (package p6portal, 16 November 2026): written at every save by `xma_engine::portal_summary::xma_metadata_xml`, a port of `ProjectFileIO::writePortalFile` (`ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:1166-1421`): the study fields of the project's metadata, the calibration trial (each camera's grid and calibration images with their FileIds from the metadata, `Project::getFileID`; inliers and mean error, `Camera::getCalibrationError`), the calibration object, and each saved trial that is not the Default one (tracked ranges, rates, errors, the movies with their portal ids and FileIds, rigid bodies and markers), numbers as `QString::number`; `date-modified` is the save time with its UTC offset (`Z` for none). Identical to XMALab's file in the eight Portal runs (`docs/parity/phase6/portal/`). The undistortion list's element is written `<Camera  ...>` / `</Camera >` (`writeStartElement("Camera ")`) in both modes: that is well-formed XML read by any XML reader as the element `Camera` (an end tag may hold white space before `>`; attributes may be separated by several spaces), XMALab itself never reads the file, and how the Portal reads it cannot be known here, so the bytes are kept (test `portal_summary::tests::camera_element_with_a_space_is_well_formed`). |
| F25 | Export details copied as they are: a zero translation component is written `-0`; precision info names markers and bodies by their raw description, so an empty one gives a line starting with a comma; per-item file names keep an empty description (`Marker001__points3d.csv`); an event with an empty name is written to `.csv`, and events with the same name overwrite one another; with no markers or bodies the header and rows have no line end. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:2307-2309`, `core/Trial.cpp:1003-1010,1505-1511,1637,1727-1752`, `core/EventData.cpp:82` | Confirmed (export port; every export file byte-identical to the dumps) | Fix, compat (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026 ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity"). `CppCompat::export_details`. A zero is now written `0`, not `-0` (transformations); an empty description gets the export's default name (`marker%03d`, `RigidBody%03d`) in the precision report and in per-item file names; events with empty or repeated names get distinct file names (`Event%03d`, `<name>_2`, ...), names being compared ignoring case so that they are distinct on the case-insensitive file systems of Windows and macOS too (`Walk` and `walk` give `Walk.csv` and `walk_2.csv`); with no markers or bodies every line is ended. The event files a project save writes keep XMALab's names (F20). The C++ behaviour is kept in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode (`CppCompat::XMALAB3`, `--xmalab3-compat`), which `xma parity` uses. Measured effect: `pig_original` (both dumps) and `v05`: `reprojection_errors/` file names of markers with an empty description. Tests `transformations_fixed_details`, `marker_exports_fixed_details`, `events_fixed_names`, `precision_info_fixed`, `f25_event_names_are_distinct_ignoring_case`. |
| F26 | The filtered 3D export's dialog offers the trial cutoff rounded to one decimal place, so accepting the default filters at a slightly different frequency from the trial's. | `ui/MainWindow.cpp:1740` | Confirmed in the source (export port) | Fix | An interface matter for phase 2; the export function takes the frequency it is given. Decided 14 November 2026: fix in phase 2 (interface). Intended: the dialog offers the trial's cutoff as it is, so accepting the default filters at the trial's frequency. |
| F27 | The C++ stamps a saved project's creation date and its archive entries with local time. The Rust standard library has no time zones, so the engine stamps UTC. | `core/Project.cpp:151` | Confirmed in the source (save port) | Copy (14 November 2026) | Author's decision of 14 November 2026: use local time. The Rust program now stamps both values with the machine's local time, found with the `jiff` crate (`SaveTime::now`): the creation date as `QDateTime::currentDateTime().toString(Qt::ISODate)` writes it (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS`, no zone suffix, no milliseconds), the archive entries with the same instant (the C++ takes each entry's time from its temporary file's local modification time, `QuaZipNewInfo(name, file)`, `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:2355`). Where the zone cannot be found `jiff` uses UTC. Both modes now do what the C++ does, so this needs no compatibility switch. A fixed time can be given: `SaveTime::at`, `SaveTime::parse`, `xma recompute --save-time`. Affects no numbers. |

This program could save such a project but not read it back. | `core/Trial.cpp:135-170` | Read in the source | Fix | The reader accepts an empty list: the trial has no video for that camera, its frame count and rate come from the project file, and a warning says that XMALab cannot open the file. Test `a_trial_without_videos_is_saved_and_read_back`. |
| F33 | File > Import > Trial from xma-file does not compare the numbers of cameras: `Trial::Trial(name, folder)` builds the trial with this project's cameras, reading `<camera>_filenames_absolute.txt` by this project's camera names, and `Marker::load` reads each 2D row for this project's number of cameras. "Import from xma" (markers and bodies, `ProjectFileIO::loadMarker`) reads no camera data and is accepted from any number of cameras; "Copy From trial" copies within the project. | F49 | NaN and infinities in a project file. XMALab writes its data files with `std::ofstream << double` (17 digits), so a NaN or infinite value is written `nan`, `-nan`, `inf` or `-inf` (glibc; MSVC `nan(ind)`), and XML attributes with `QString::number` (`nan`, `inf`). It reads the data files with `for (double v; in >> v; littleHelper::comma(in))`, which stops at such a word (libstdc++ `num_get` takes only digits, sign, point and exponent; an overflowing number sets failbit too): a row that starts with one is skipped without a word (the frame keeps its initial value), a row with one later is short and the loader reads past its end (`tmp[2 * i + 1]`, undefined behaviour). The XML attributes and the `x,y,z` files are read with `QString::toDouble`, which accepts `nan` and `inf`. So a saved project with such a value does not open again as saved. | Writing: `core/Marker.cpp:634-698` (`save`), `core/Camera.cpp:416-419`, `:466-472`, `core/CalibrationImage.cpp:583-635`, `core/UndistortionObject.cpp:847-850`, `core/RigidBody.cpp:1455-1480`, `:2036-2060`, `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:1631`, `:1690`, `:1694`. Reading: `core/Marker.cpp:703-780` (`load`), `:808-850` (`load3DPoints`), `core/CalibrationImage.cpp:651-764`, `core/UndistortionObject.cpp:934`, `:982`, `core/HelperFunctions.cpp:144-151` (`comma`), `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:448-449`, `:601`, `:619-622` | Read in the source; review 7 (`review7_bounds.rs` `review7_nonfinite_point_survives_save`); package fix5 | Fix, compat | Default: no project holds a non-finite stored number, so none is written; a file that holds one (written by XMALab or the XMALab 3 mode) opens with one warning per value naming the entry and line ("T/data/Marker000points2d.csv, line 6, camera 2: a 2D point is not a finite number; the point is not set"), the value read as its field's "not set" value. The reader reads the words as values first (`cparse::stream_values_nonfinite`: `nan`, `-nan`, `nan(...)`, `inf`, `infinity`, any case, and overflowing numbers), so the row is whole, then `xma_io::finite::clear_non_finite` sets each to not set. Not set per field (`xma_io::finite` module doc): 2D point, the frame reset ((-2, -2), undefined, size -1); size, -1; 3D point, (-1000, -1000, -1000) undefined; marker reference point, none; camera matrix, identity and the camera and its images not calibrated; lens distortion, zeros and the lens model off; calibration image rotation or translation, not calibrated; calibration point, (-1, -1) not an inlier; detected point (all), removed; undistortion grid points, grid not computed (lists emptied); grid centre, none; bead coordinate, 0; square size, 0; view scale or bias, 1 or 0; trial recording speed or cutoff, 0; rigid-body reference point, the body's references reset (`resetReferences`, then the markers' reference points when all have one); virtual point reference or other body's point, the point removed; virtual point coordinate, none at that frame; body cutoff, 0 and override off; mesh scale, 1; a floating-point setting, its default (the settings entry already refused one). The writer, given a project holding one, writes the not-set value (`project_write::project_entries`). XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::nonfinite_values_kept`): kept, written and read as XMALab writes and reads them (a short row is an error naming the entry, line and column). Tests `fix5_nonfinite.rs` `f49_file_with_nan_opens_with_a_warning` (both modes), `finite.rs` `each_field_is_set_to_not_set`, `finite_project_is_untouched`. |
| F50 | A non-finite value computed into what a project stores (a 3D point, a pose's reference shape, a calibration) is kept by XMALab and written as F49 describes. | as F49 | Package fix5 | Fix, compat (the F49 field) | Default: the document stores the field's not-set value (F49's list) with a line in the warnings: after each committed edit step (`history.rs` `commit_step`), after a trial update (`Document::update_trial`), when a document is built (`Document::from_loaded`) and before saving (`save_entries`), so a save always opens again to the state the document holds (`Document::clear_non_finite`). Derived values the file does not store (reprojections, errors, poses) are left as computed. XMALab 3 mode: kept. Tests `fix5_nonfinite.rs` `f50_default_save_opens_to_the_same_state`, `f50_xmalab3_keeps_the_value`. |

Test `fix5_nonfinite.rs` `f51_edits_refuse_non_finite_numbers` (both modes). |

Review 8 (16 November 2026, package p6portal): every part of every name is checked, on every system alike, and these are refused: `..`; a leading `/` or `\`; any of `:` (a drive or an NTFS stream, in any part), `<`, `>`, `"`, `|`, `?`, `*` or a control character below U+0020; a part ending in `.` or a space (Windows drops them, so two names would be one file); the Windows device names `CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1` to `COM9`, `LPT1` to `LPT9` and `COM`/`LPT` with ¹ ² ³, with or without an extension (`xma_io::portal::entry_parts`). The study and trial names and every `Filename` are checked before anything is written; a later failure (a bad CRC, a full disk, Stop, a nested zip that cannot be read) removes exactly the files and folders this import wrote, never ones that were there before (`xma_io::portal::create_dirs`, `undo`). | F70 | Names an export builds from what the project holds (a trial's or camera's name, a marker's or event's description, a video's file name: All Trials for External's `<trial>/` folders, Undistorted Trial images' `<base>UND` folders and files, the denoising's files, File > Undistort sequence's files, the 2D point, 3D point, event, reprojection error and MayaCam files) are written as they are: a trial named `..` writes into the folder above the one chosen, `.` into the chosen folder itself, a `/` into a subfolder, and `:`, `*`, `?`, `"`, `<`, `>`, `|`, control characters, trailing dots and spaces and Windows device names (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`, ...) give names Windows cannot create. | `ui/MainWindow.cpp:2085-2108`, `core/Trial.cpp:1154-1314`, `processing/DenoiseTrial.cpp:58-79`, the exporters of `core/Trial.cpp` | Review 8, finding 12 (`review8_exports.rs` `all_trials_export_stays_inside_the_output_folder`); read in the source | Fix, compat | One function, `xma_io::export::safe_component`, for every such part. Refused in both modes (nothing written under it, the reason in the report): an empty name, `.`, `..`, and in the XMALab 3 mode a name with `/` or `\` (XMALab would write outside the file's folder; that is not reproduced). Replaced by default, each with `_`: `/`, `\`, `:`, `*`, `?`, `"`, `<`, `>`, `|`, control characters, trailing dots and spaces; a Windows device name gets `_` in front. `CppCompat::export_names_as_given`: the XMALab 3 mode keeps those characters (all can be created on Linux). A one-file export's name is the file the user chose and is not changed. A video's base name is taken after the last `/` or `\` on every system (`xma_io::export::complete_base_name`, `file_name_of`; this program held `\` paths whole, so on Windows the export went beside the video: review 8, finding 11, a fault of the port). Default: only cameras whose folders would be the same get `<base>UND_cam<n>` (`n` the camera, from 1); a single camera and cameras with distinct names keep XMALab's `<base>UND`. XMALab 3 mode: the shared folder, as XMALab, with a warning naming the cameras (the report's problems).
