| F73 | The project file does not store which axis of a checkerboard is inverted: `setCheckerboard` reads the setting `CheckerboadInvertedAxis` when the board is created and again when the project is opened, so a project calibrated with one setting and opened with another has its board mirrored against its stored poses (every reprojection wrong, the next calibration on mirrored correspondences). In this program the setting travels with the project (A10), which narrows but does not close the gap: the setting may be changed after the board was built. | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:1603-1615`, `:1980-1990`; `core/CalibrationObject.cpp:213-238` | Confirmed (source) | Fix, compat | Default: `CalibrationObject` gains the attribute `InvertedAxis` (the board's own axis, `xma_model::CalibrationObject::Checkerboard::inverted_axis`), which XMALab 3 ignores (`QXmlStreamAttributes` by name; its reader checked line by line); the reader uses it when present, else the setting. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::checkerboard_axis_from_setting`): not written, not read. Tests `write_synthetic.rs` `internal_checkerboard_element`, `f73_checkerboard_inverted_axis_round_trip`. |
| F74 | A checkerboard of any size: the dialog allows 99 x 99 corners, a project file (or a script) any `int` count, and `setCheckerboard` builds every point (`H x V` up to 2^62 allocations). | `core/CalibrationObject.cpp:226-235` | Confirmed (source) | Fix | Boards of more than 1000000 corners (`xma_model::project::MAX_CHECKERBOARD_POINTS`) are refused by the reader and by File > New dataset with a sentence. Tests `write_synthetic.rs` `f74_huge_checkerboard_refused`, `create.rs` `new_project_check_sentences`. |
| F75 | A checkerboard project written by XMALab carries no inverted axis (F73), so in the default mode it opened with the setting in force; with another setting the board is mirrored against its stored poses (review 8 measured a mean error of 430 px on k05 opened with the setting 0). | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:1980-1990` | Review 8; reference runs k02, k05, k06 | Fix, compat | Default, for a file without `InvertedAxis`: the axis the stored calibration fits is taken (`xma_io::checkerboard_axis`): for each of the three boards, the mean distance between the stored detected inliers and the board's points through each calibrated image's stored pose, camera matrix and lens model; the best is taken when below 5 px and below a fifth of the second; otherwise (no calibrated image, no clear winner) the setting. A warning names the axis taken when it differs from the setting. A wrong axis puts the points tens to hundreds of pixels off, the right one at the calibration's error, so a calibrated project is recognised reliably. XMALab 3 mode (`CppCompat::checkerboard_axis_from_setting`, F73's switch): the setting. Test `checkerboard_axis_files.rs` (k05: axis x, mean error 0.046 px; k06: axis y, 0.046 px; k02: none, 0.037 px, no warning; each opened with the setting 0; the XMALab 3 mode keeps 0). |
| F78 | Hostile but bounded Portal input: a metadata file of up to 64 MiB, a central directory of up to 256 MiB, and archives with many entries. Measured (package p6portal, review 8): 64 MiB of `<a/>` held 2.9 GB while tokenised; a directory of 64 MiB of one-byte names (1.4 million entries) held 280 MB. Three readings were quadratic: choosing the top folder (each folder tested against every name: 20 000 folders, 9 s), the list of files to extract (each name compared with every earlier one: 30 000 files, 16 s), and `readElementText` over nested elements (4 MB of nested `cameraNumber`, 15 s). | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:870-1142`, `core/Project.cpp:320-391` (XMALab has none of these limits; it holds the whole archive on disk and the metadata in a `QStringList`) | Measured; tests `portal_memory.rs`, `review8_portal.rs` | Fix (both modes) | Caps: at most 500 000 tokens in a metadata file (`xma_io::portal::MAX_METADATA_TOKENS`; a Portal file lists a file in about 40 tokens, so some 12 000 files, where real ones list a few to a few hundred) and at most 262 144 entries and 64 MiB of central directory in an archive read from a file (`xma_io::zip::MAX_FILE_ARCHIVE_ENTRIES`, `MAX_DIRECTORY_BYTES`; four times the 65 535 entries a zip without zip64 holds), each refused with an error before the data is held. Measured after: the 64 MiB metadata file 111 MB peak (the file itself is 64 MiB), the directory refused at 3.4 MB. The three readings are single passes; `readElementText` follows Qt's default (`ErrorOnUnexpectedElement`: the text up to the first child element, then an error that ends the reading, `xma_io::xml::read_element_text`); the project reader's other uses read leaf elements only, and every reference project reads as before. Tests `portal_memory.rs` `metadata_of_64_mib_of_empty_elements`, `central_directory_near_the_cap`, `at_the_caps`; `review8_portal.rs` `many_top_folders_are_refused_quickly`, `many_files_under_the_top_folder_open_quickly`, `nested_camera_numbers_are_read_quickly`. Second round of review 11 (20 November 2026): the same limit now applies to the metadata stored in a project when it is loaded (read once, where it was read twice, the first time without the limit; above the limit a warning) and when `xma_metadata.xml` is written at a save (read once per save, where it was read once per file looked up). |
| F79 | A Portal trial whose `TrialName` is empty (or white space, or `.`): the trial folder is `<folder>/` itself, the folder of the Portal file (or of the project file, or the study folder of the workspace), so the archive is extracted among the user's other files, and a `Filename` ending in `zip` there is unpacked and removed even when it is not a file of the archive: a metadata file listing the download's own name deletes the download, whatever "delete after import" was answered. | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:957-999` | Read in the source; review 8; package p6portal | Fix (both modes) | The trial is extracted to a folder named after the archive's top folder instead (`xma_engine::portal::trial_folder_name`; refusing would leave the user no way to import the file); only a zip this import itself wrote is unpacked and removed (`import_trial`); the download is deleted only by "delete after import" after a successful import (U151). Not kept in the XMALab 3 mode (data loss). Test `review8_portal.rs` `empty_trial_name_does_not_remove_the_download`. |
| F80 | A project whose three stored undistortion-grid lists (`GridPointsDistorted`, `GridPointsReferences`, `GridPointsInlier`) differ in length: an altered or damaged project, since XMALab writes the three together and has written the inlier file since its first commit. The load already ignores such a grid with a warning. A later refit ("recompute undistortion", or "Yes" to the pending changes, after "toggle outlier" or "remove outlier at border") reads out of range in XMALab and panicked here. | `core/UndistortionObject.cpp:203`, `:961-971`; `core/Camera.cpp:300-307` | Review 11; `crates/xma-engine/tests/review11_grid_lists.rs` | Fix (both modes) | The refit, "toggle outlier" and "remove outlier at border" are refused (`EditError::Rejected`, naming the camera and the three lengths) before anything changes; "remove outlier at border" is refused for every camera when one camera's lists differ. Computing the undistortion again from a new centre matches the grid afresh and still works. |
| F81 | A calibration image whose stored detected list is shorter than the calibration object while its inlier list is not (an altered project: every writer keeps the two the same length). A click in manual mode indexed past the detected list; XMALab reads out of range. | `ui/WizardCalibrationCubeFrame.cpp:905` (`reloadManualPoints`) | Review 11; `crates/xma-engine/tests/review11_manual_short_detected.rs` | Fix (both modes) | Such an image gets manual mode's fresh lists (`init(n)`), as one with a short inlier list does (`needs_manual_lists`). |
| F82 | A calibration sequence (a video of calibration frames) stored with a relative path: XMALab adjusts the separators and opens the path against the current working folder, and asks for the file when it is not there. The engine opened the stored text as it was, with no separator adjusted and no folder, while the camera views took it from the project's folder. | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:1881-1891`; `CalibrationSequence::loadTextures` | Review 11, second round (20 November 2026); read in the source by the implementer and by an independent reviewer; `crates/xma-engine/tests/review12b_calibration_sequence_path.rs` | Fix (both modes) | The separators are adjusted as XMALab adjusts them, and a relative path is taken from the project's folder, as every other stored path is and as the views already did (`xma_engine::resolve_stored_path`). No number changes. |
| F83 | Still images of a project (calibration images, undistortion grids) kept on disk and stored with the other system's separators: the camera views converted the separators and showed them, while the engine (detection, calibration) did not and reported them missing. The two also differed on an archive entry one folder deeper than `<camera>/<file>`. XMALab adjusts the separators and reads `basedir/<text>`. | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:1905-1909` | Review 11, second round (20 November 2026); `crates/xma-engine/tests/review12_still_paths.rs`, `crates/xma-app/tests/review12_stills_lookup.rs` (the engine did not find the file before) | Fix (both modes) | One function finds a still image for the views and the engine alike (`xma_engine::still_bytes`): the archive entry of that name with either separator, then `<camera>/<file>`, then the same one folder deeper, then the file on disk with its separators adjusted and a relative path taken from the project's folder. |
| F84 | A project file sets the size of per-frame arrays with no bound: a trial's `nbImages` (times the lines of `MarkerDescription.txt` and the cameras), one pose track per rigid-body line, one flag array per `<Event>`, one coordinate array per virtual point, `Frames` of a `<CalibrationSequence>`; and the rows of the data files beyond the declared frames extend the arrays (one flag per extra event row, one frame per extra 2D-points row, every coordinate row of a virtual point). A small damaged or hostile file makes the program allocate tens of gigabytes and it is killed with no message. | `core/Marker.cpp` (`Marker::init`, `Marker::load`, `addFrame`), `core/Trial.cpp`, `core/RigidBody.cpp` (`addDummyPoint`), `core/EventData.cpp` (`loadData`), `core/CalibrationSequence.cpp` | Read in the source; review 11 (20 November 2026); two independent reviews of the fix | Fix, both modes (the author, 21 November 2026: "Refuse above the limit": 10 million frames in one trial, 250 million for cameras × frames × markers) | Opening is refused with a sentence, the reader's ordinary error with the element's line and column, before anything of that size is allocated: a trial of more than 10,000,000 frames (`xma_model::project::MAX_FRAMES`), wherever the count comes from; a calibration sequence, or a camera's calibration frames in all, above the same number; any per-frame array of a trial that would reach more than 10,000,000 frames counting the rows of its data file; and, per trial, counted with the lengths the arrays really reach, cameras × frames × markers, frames × events or frames × virtual points above 250,000,000 (`MAX_TRIAL_CELLS`), or frames × rigid bodies above 50,000,000 (`MAX_TRIAL_BODY_CELLS`). The author's two numbers are the first and the marker budget; applying the 250,000,000 to events and virtual points, and the lower budget for rigid bodies (about 162 bytes per body and frame, so about 8 GB at 50,000,000 where 250,000,000 would be about 40 GB), are choices made after review. The elements counted are the elements read, from one shared walk of the trial (`trial_elements`), so nesting cannot hide any (first review: a `<Trial>` nested in a `<RigidBody>` hid virtual points). A file named by several elements counts once per element. Sentences: "trial 'T' has N frames, more than this program accepts (10000000)"; "trial 'T' is too large: cameras x frames x markers is a x b x c, more than this program accepts (250000000)"; with extra rows "..., counted with the rows of its data files, is N, ..."; "trial 'T': event 'E' would have N frames from the rows of 'T/data/E.txt', more than this program accepts (10000000)". Every project below the limits reads exactly as before in both modes, including one whose data files have a few rows more than the declared frames (48,400 reads of 200 reference projects and seeded mutations of them compared before and after; `review14d_reader_diff.rs`). No compatibility switch: XMALab 3's behaviour is running out of memory. What the limits do not do: below them one trial can still make the reader allocate about 25 GB of markers (peak up to 34 GB with many cameras), 8 GB of rigid bodies, 9.6 GB of virtual points and 0.25 GB of events (measured at a hundredth of each budget and scaled); there is no limit across trials (a total could refuse a legitimate project of very many long trials) and no byte budget; a trial of 0 frames can list any number of markers and cameras (bounded by the file's size); New trial and Change Trial Data take a video's own frame count without this check. Memory below the limits: the first count of each array is an upper bound only (the line ends of its data file plus one, one pass over the bytes) and is used to refuse, not to reserve; the exact lengths are counted, and reserved, only when the bound passes a limit, so blank lines and lines without values reserve nothing (the first build reserved from the bound: 370 MB for a 2D-points file of blank lines; found by the second review). An ordinary project loads as fast as before F84 (`perf13_io`, synthetic project, pinned to one core: 1.77 to 1.79 s against 1.78 to 1.80 s before; the first build took 1.84 to 1.90 s). Tests: `xma-io/tests/review14_size_limits.rs`, `review14b_size_limit_routes.rs`, `review14c_size_limit_routes.rs`, `review14d_reserve_blank_lines.rs`, `review14d_reader_diff.rs`, `review14e_reserve_exact.rs`. |
| F85 | A new export, **Blender scene** (File › Export › Blender scene; `xma export … blender-scene`). XMALab exports MayaCam files, which the author's Blender add-on (XROMM_BlenderTools) imports camera by camera. This export writes a folder that Blender turns into a whole scene: a fixed script `build_scene.py`, `scene.json`, the undistorted images, and optionally the bone meshes. Two kinds: **cameras only** (each calibrated camera at its pose for one calibration image, with that image undistorted on its plane) and **a trial** (the trial's undistorted images of a range of frames as an image sequence, the scene's frame range and rate set from the trial; optionally the rigid bodies as animated objects, their bone meshes attached, and the 3D points; filtered or not). When asked and when Blender is found (`XMALAB_BLENDER`, `blender` on the `PATH`, the usual install places) the export runs Blender in the background to save `<name>.blend` in the folder, with still images packed into it on request. Each camera is posed as the add-on poses it (rotation `Rᵀ·diag(1,−1,−1)`, location `−Rᵀt`) and, beyond the add-on, given the lens, pixel aspect and principal-point shift of the X-ray camera, so that the view through it is the image; its image plane fills the frame at any distance and is right for images that are not square. | No C++ counterpart (XMALab: `Project::exportMayaCam`, `exportMayaCamVersion2`) | Tests that run Blender 5.3 headless (`xma-engine/tests/blender_scene.rs`, 16; skipped without Blender, required with `XMA_REQUIRE_BLENDER=1`): 3D marker points projected through the Blender cameras against the program's own projection, largest difference 0.0006 px (the pig; a project with a mirrored camera; a made-up camera with fx ≠ fy, an off-centre principal point and an image that is not square); the plane's corners on the frame's corners, also after sliding; the right file at the right frame; rigid-body matrices against the transformations export (1.3e-7 relative), 3D points against the 3D-point export (5e-8), bone-mesh vertices against the program's own (2e-8 of the scene's size; projected, 0.0007 px); a body hidden at a frame without a pose; the saved `.blend` reopened with everything in it and the meshes inside it; a second run of the script in one file replacing the first. The exported calibration image is pixel-identical (0 grey levels) to what File › Undistort sequence writes, on the pig, the lens and the mirrored reference projects. `xma-cli/tests/blender_scene_cli.rs` (3), `xma-app/tests/blender_scene_ui.rs` (13). Independent review 27 (26 November 2026) rendered through the Blender cameras and found the beads within a median of 0.2 to 0.45 px of the program's projection, for the calibration image and for trial frames, with a mirrored camera and with a camera without a grid; found that names with quotes, `$( )`, `../` and newlines reach Blender only as data and nothing is written outside the folder; and found four faults, corrected the same day: a mesh path naming a pipe, a device or a huge file was read whole on the interface thread (now only a regular file of at most 256 MiB, on the job's thread); the 10-minute limit on a Blender run did not hold when its launcher left a child (now the whole process group is stopped and the wait is bounded); a folder of the user's named like the export's unfinished folder was deleted (the unfinished folder now has a name that cannot exist, and nothing the run did not make is removed); the command shown for running Blender by hand was not safe to paste into a shell (now quoted). Not run: Blender older than 5.3 (the script is written for 4.2 and later); Windows and macOS | **Added** (the author, 26 November 2026: "I'd like to add an export option for 'blender scene' 'just cameras' or 'trial [x]' … a one-click button that can create a blender scene with the cameras in there, and either calibration or trial images embedded. including meshes could also be an option?") | A `.blend` file is not written by this program (its format is Blender's own memory layout): Blender writes it. Blender cannot pack an image sequence, so a trial's images stay in the folder beside the `.blend`, with relative paths; stills and meshes are inside it. Units pass through unchanged. A frame without a pose hides the body; nothing is interpolated across it. An unfinished export leaves nothing; an existing folder that is not empty is refused, never overwritten. The window's own mesh loader has the weakness corrected here for the export (a mesh path naming a pipe or a device), on its own thread: not changed. Nothing of the project is changed by an export |

## Interface

These rows change no computed numbers. They record where the interface deliberately differs from XMALab 3 because XMALab's behaviour was a defect, and the interface defects still to be decided. Where a Rust option restores the C++ behaviour it is named; such options are for reference and tests, not settings.

| # | Item | C++ location | Evidence | Decision | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| U1 | Camera-view wheel zoom: the new ratio is `zoomRatio * 1 - delta / 1000`, which by operator precedence subtracts 0.12 per notch whatever the zoom, not a factor; from 500 % one notch in jumps to the 999 % limit, and zooming out is very slow. | `ui/GLCameraView.cpp:343`, `GLCameraView::wheelEvent` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Each notch multiplies the ratio by `1 - delta / 1000` (`WheelZoom::Factor`, `crates/xma-app/src/widgets/image_view/state.rs`). `WheelZoom::Xmalab3Linear` keeps the C++ step. Second round of review 11 (20 November 2026): one wheel event of 1000 angle units or more would make the factor zero or negative and jump to the 999 % limit; it now zooms in by 0.88^(1000/120), about 2.9 times. Events below 1000 units are unchanged, so the response is not smooth across that value (960 units zoom in about 25 times). `Xmalab3Linear` is unchanged. |
| U2 | The camera view's zoom box accepts 0, which makes the ratio `100 / 0` infinite: the image vanishes and the next wheel step makes the offsets NaN. | `ui/CameraViewWidget.cpp:210-214`, `GLCameraView::setZoom` (`ui/GLCameraView.cpp:377-381`) | Confirmed in the source | Fix | A zoom value of 0 is treated as 1 % (`ImageViewState::ratio_of_percent`). |
| U3 | In the Calibration workspace, the double-click position passed to `toggleInlier` leaves out the -0.5 that the press handler subtracts, so it is half a pixel off. | `ui/GLCameraView.cpp:203-205` (`mouseDoubleClickEvent`) against `:229-230` (`mousePressEvent`) | Confirmed in the source | Fix (when ported, phase 4) | Use the press handler's formula. Not ported yet (`camera_view/overlay_calibration.rs` is a stub). |
| U4 | The epipolar line is kept where `0 < y < width`: the variable named `height` holds the camera's width. | `core/Marker.cpp:1744`, `Marker::getEpipolarLine` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Kept where `0 < y < height` (`xma_vision::epipolar`). `EpipolarOptions::xmalab3` restores the C++ test. |
| U5 | A vertical epipolar line (`p2.x == p1.x`) gets slope 0 and is drawn horizontally in the wrong place; a steep line is sampled every `0.1 * m` pixels along y, so it is coarse or empty. | `core/Marker.cpp:1744`, `Marker::getEpipolarLine` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Lines steeper than 45° are sampled in steps of 0.1 along y. `EpipolarOptions::xmalab3` restores the C++ sampling. |
| U6 | The epipolar line is curved only when the camera has an undistortion-grid object: a camera with a lens model and no grid gets a straight line over a distorted image, and a grid that is loaded but not computed drops every sample, so no line is drawn. | `core/Marker.cpp:1744`, `Marker::getEpipolarLine`; `Camera::hasUndistortion` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Curved when a computed grid or a lens model is present. `EpipolarOptions::xmalab3` restores the C++ test. |
| U7 | Marker numbers are drawn for every marker at its stored position, so unset markers pile up at (-2, -2). | `core/Trial.cpp:2056`, `Trial::getDrawTextData` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Only markers with a status above 0 in that camera are labelled (`xma_engine::view::overlays::tracking_overlay`). |
| U8 | The detail view's crosshair for an unset point is drawn at (-2, -2). | `core/Trial.cpp:588`, `Trial::drawPoints` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Not drawn. |
| U9 | Shift + click can select an unset marker: the distance is measured to stored positions, including the placeholder (-2, -2). | `ui/WizardDigitizationFrame.cpp:135`, `WizardDigitizationFrame::selectDigitizationPoint` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Unset points are ignored (`overlays::marker_near`). |
| U10 | With "Use filtered transformations", the constellation is drawn from the filtered arrays even where they hold no pose (zeros); virtual points use the raw pose and their count the filtered one. | `core/RigidBody.cpp:2669`, `RigidBody::draw2D` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | The filtered pose is used only where it is valid, and the same pose for every point (`overlays::constellation`). |
| U11 | The scale and bias sliders are set to `(int)(value * 100)`, which truncates and feeds back into the spin box, so 0.29 becomes 0.28. | `ui/CameraViewDetailWidget.cpp:104-136`; the same pattern in `DigitizationInfoFrame` and `CalibrationInfoFrame` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Rounded (`panels/detail_view.rs`). The info frames are to be done the same way when they are ported. |
| U12 | A marker listed by two rigid bodies is shown under the first only: `reloadListFromObject` finds the marker's row by its text and moves only top-level rows, so it is not added to a later body (and a `MarkerTreeWidgetButton` is leaked). In `rv_vpchain` bodies 3 and 4 show no markers. | `ui/PointsDockWidget.cpp:181`, `PointsDockWidget::reloadListFromObject` | Confirmed on `rv_vpchain` | Fix | Shown under every body that lists it (`xma_engine::view::tree::tree_rows`); selecting it highlights each row and expands the first body. |
| U13 | Ctrl+A (`QTreeView::selectAll`) selects only shown rows, so markers in collapsed bodies are not selected and the "selected" tracking buttons skip them. | `ui/PointsDockWidget.cpp:302`, `PointsDockWidget::selectAllPoints` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Selects every marker and body. |
| U14 | "Save the transformation of selected rigid bodies" reads `bodies[0]` before checking that any body is selected (an out-of-range read). | `ui/MarkerTreeWidget.cpp:468`, `MarkerTreeWidget::action_SaveTransformations_triggered` | Confirmed in the source | Fix (when ported, phase 3) | Check the selection first. The entry is shown but disabled for now. |
| U15 | `reloadListFromObject` reads `getTrials()[activeTrial]` without checking that the active trial exists. | `ui/PointsDockWidget.cpp:181-210`, `PointsDockWidget::reloadListFromObject` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Guarded. |
| U16 | The Display Options check boxes are set from the settings only when the panel is built (and Hide all when it changes), so a setting changed elsewhere is not shown. | `ui/DisplayOptionsDockWidget.cpp:57-65` (constructor) | Confirmed in the source | Fix | The boxes are resynchronised from the settings every frame (`panels/display_options.rs`). |

| U18 | Console messages of level 0 are written in black, which is unreadable in the dark theme. | `ui/ConsoleDockWidget.cpp:143-160`, `ConsoleDockWidget::writeLog` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Level 0 uses the theme's text colour and the other levels lightened colours in dark mode (`LogLevel::color_on`, `crates/xma-app/src/log.rs`). |
| U19 | Ctrl+O is both File › Open dataset and the "Event off" application shortcut; Qt treats the pair as ambiguous and which fires depends on focus. | `ui/ui-files/MainWindow.ui:853`, `ui/Shortcuts.cpp:162,326` | Confirmed in the source | Fix | Open dataset wins while it is enabled; Event off gets the key otherwise (`actions::shortcut_table`, `collect_shortcuts`). |

XMALab 3's Edit > Undo restores one 2D point (not its size, not a marker created by the click), is cleared by any change of marker, camera, frame or trial, and is never available after Ctrl+click because the Points list rebuilds and clears it at once. Nothing else can be undone. | `ui/WizardDigitizationFrame.cpp:118-126`, `:192-248` (`docs/cpp-editing.md` 2.4, D1) | Read in the source (editing survey, 14 November 2026) | Fix (addition) | Every edit is an undo step and can be redone (`crates/xma-engine/src/history.rs`); a tracking run is one step. XMALab 3 has no modified state: closing, opening another project and quitting always show the same warning to make sure data has been saved, whether or not anything changed, and the title never shows unsaved changes. | `ui/MainWindow.cpp:335-349`, `:924-931`, `:1602-1665` (`docs/cpp-editing.md` 12.1, 12.2) | Read in the source | Fix (addition) | The document knows whether it differs from the saved file (`Document::is_modified`); the interface asks to save only then and marks the title. Built (`crates/xma-app/src/app/document.rs`); the prompts are U55 and U56, the title U57. Review 11 (20 November 2026): a dataset made by File > New (with or without external calibration), by New trial without calibration or by Open Dataset with different calibration is unsaved from the start (`xma_engine::create`). Before, it counted as saved until its first edit, so it closed with no question and was not autosaved. An untitled dataset's recovery copy is written to the settings folder; nothing offers it back at startup yet. |
| U54 | Saving. XMALab 3 deletes the old file before writing the new archive under the same name, still zips after an earlier step failed, shows errors from the worker thread, and updates the file name and title as if the save had succeeded; a failed save loses the previous file. It has no autosave or backup. | `ui/ProjectFileIO.cpp:115-273`, `:2306-2314`; `ui/MainWindow.cpp:983-994`, `:1222-1229` (`docs/cpp-editing.md` D8, 12.4) | Read in the source | Fix | The archive is written to a temporary file next to the target and moved into place only when complete; a failure leaves the old file and is reported. The temporary file is flushed before the move and removed on any failure. Autosave to a recovery file is added (U60). Built (`crates/xma-io/src/project_write.rs`, `crates/xma-app/src/saving.rs`); tested with a save into a read-only folder: the old file is intact and no temporary file remains. |
| U55 | Closing the dataset or opening another (File > Open dataset, Recent Files, a file given on the command line or by the system): XMALab 3 always asks "You are about to close your dataset. Please make sure your data have been saved. Are you sure you want to close current dataset[ and load an existing dataset]?" whenever a dataset is open, changed or not. | `ui/MainWindow.cpp:1647-1665`, `:1258-1263` | Read in the source | Fix (follows U53) | The new program asks "Save changes?" (Save, Don't Save, Cancel) only when the document has unsaved changes, and asks nothing otherwise; Save saves first and goes on only if the save succeeded (`XmaApp::unsaved_guard`). Review 11 (20 November 2026): the guard first ends a tracking run or a refinement in progress, which is one undo step still open and so not yet counted as a change; before, closing during a run dropped its points without a question. "Save" answered while saving is not possible (a job that disables the window) shows an error and leaves nothing to follow a later save. A recent file is carried by its path, not by its place in the list, which a Save As in between changes. |

A save in progress finishes before the window closes. Review 11 (20 November 2026): a quit asked for during a save is carried out after it as any quit is, with its questions; before, quitting during "Save subdataset as" exited with the dataset's own changes unsaved. |

The recovery file is `<name>.recovery.xma` next to the project (`untitled.recovery.xma` in the settings folder for a project never saved); it is removed after a successful save and when a project is closed without unsaved changes; when a project is opened and its recovery file is newer, the program offers "Open recovered version", "Open saved version" or Cancel. |
| U61 | The recovery file after "Don't Save": as first built, the recovery file is kept when a project is closed with unsaved changes and "Don't Save" is chosen, so the next open offers it again. | No C++ counterpart | Addition | Fix | Decided 14 November 2026: an explicit "Don't Save" discards the changes, so it also removes the recovery file; "Open saved version" removes it too. Built (`crates/xma-app/src/app.rs`, `dialog_result`), tested in `crates/xma-app/tests/trial_editing.rs`. Review 11 (20 November 2026): the file is removed when the project is actually closed, or the program quits, after "Don't Save", not when the answer is given; cancelling the next step (a file dialog, a creation dialog) keeps it. Opening the same file again after "Don't Save" removes it without offering it (`crates/xma-app/tests/review11_unsaved.rs`). |

The filter takes any value without overflow or an unbounded kernel: a radius below 0 is 0 and one above 1000 is 1000 (`xma_vision::visual_filter::MAX_KRAD`); non-finite values give an image (a value of 99 or less, all XMALab's dialog allows, is filtered as before). | U165 | Saved positions of floating panels and of the main window are used again without a check that they are on a screen: after a second monitor is unplugged a panel can open where nobody sees it, and three panels (Toolbox, Points, Progress) have no menu entry that brings them back. Qt clamps restored geometry to the screens; this program does not. | No C++ counterpart for the entry (`QDockAreaLayout`, `QWidget::restoreGeometry` do the clamping in Qt) | Review 11 (20 November 2026); behaviour with real windows measured on Linux only | Addition (the author, 21 November 2026: "View menu entry"; nothing is moved automatically) | View > Reset layout, the last entry of the View menu after a separator, not in XMALab 3 (`Action::ResetLayout`, no shortcut). It does at once what `--reset-layout` does at start, through the same `Shell::new_default`: the default panel arrangement and sizes, the 3D world view and the Console floating at their first-run sizes and placed by the window manager (their windows are closed and opened again), every other panel docked, the main window 1280 × 1024 at (50, 50), not maximised or full screen, and the default layout saved to the settings at once. Unchanged: which panels are shown, Lock UI Panes (the entry works while the panes are locked), the project (no edit, no undo step). Like the other View entries it is disabled while a job or a save blocks the window. The main window gets its geometry through viewport commands (leave full screen, leave maximised, size, position); from a window that reports maximised or full screen the size and position are sent once more, on the first frame in which it reports neither and its size has changed, and it gives up 2 s after the reset (a tiling window manager is not fought); the geometry is not recorded for saving while it waits. Measured on Linux with a throwaway program doing the same (not with the program itself): X11 from normal, maximised and full screen, and Wayland from maximised and full screen, all end at 1280 × 1024 (Wayland ignores the position; before the correction Wayland kept the size from before maximising and X11 from full screen ended one title bar short). Not measured: Windows, macOS (there the toolkit reads the maximised state only when the window is created, so a window zoomed later gets the four commands only), and the menu entry in a real window. Known: a window that leaves the maximised state at the same size it had at the reset keeps that size. Tests `xma-app/tests/review14_reset_layout.rs`, `review14b_reset_layout.rs`, `review14c_reset_layout.rs`, `review14d_reset_layout_late.rs`. |
| U166 | A camera hidden with the camera selector. This program read, filtered and uploaded every camera's frame on every frame change, and playback waited for all of them. | `core/Trial.cpp:363-385`, `ui/WorldViewDockGLWidget.cpp:328-470` | Read in the source; review 11 (20 November 2026); independent review | Fix (the author, 21 November 2026: "Skip them") | A hidden camera's image is not read, filtered or uploaded for display in any workspace, its texture is dropped, and playback does not wait for it (`Frames::wanted_with`); shown again, it gets the current frame at once. In the 3D world view its image plane is empty while it is hidden, in every workspace (the frustum lines stay): this differs from XMALab 3, which shows a stale frame there in Marker tracking and the current image in Calibration. The camera views are as XMALab's. Only display work is skipped: tracking, detection, point edits and exports read frames by another route (`Frames::read_now`, the engine) and get the hidden camera's frames (the tracker itself skips hidden cameras, as XMALab's does). With every camera visible, requests, filtering and uploads are as before. No number changes. Tests `xma-app/tests/review14_hidden_cameras.rs`. |

The two check boxes are independent. Usable when the trial has markers and at least two calibrated cameras that are shown (`AppState::camera_visible`, which the run is given; hiding one camera of two disables the buttons); otherwise disabled, with a sentence in the tooltip saying what is missing (also in the XMALab 3 mode, which the window never opens a project in). A ticked box can always be unticked outside a run: while the method cannot be used, the sentence is shown under it in place of the hint and the groups' buttons are disabled; an unticked box stays disabled. A run goes the way of the other kinds (`EditRequest::StartTracking`, one step per screen frame, one undo step "Track Points"; Stop, Escape, Play, a workspace or trial change and a second press stop it; cameras hidden when the run starts are neither read nor written, and the run keeps the cameras it started with, so a camera hidden while it is going is still tracked until it ends) through `xma_engine::tracking::TrackRun`, the type `xma track` uses too; the method's settings are its defaults and are not shown. The Console gets no line per refused point: at the end of a run one line (frames covered, points written, cells left empty) and one line per marker lost and not found again, with the frame it was last found at. "Left empty" is counted by the engine (`StepReport::left_empty`): at each frame the run reached, the cells of the markers the run has had by then (a marker it never had, such as a selected marker with no point at the start, is not counted), in the cameras it tracks, that hold no point, not counting cells marked untrackable (the run may never write there); the "lost" lines are for those markers and cameras too (corrected after review 15d, 22 November 2026: the count had included markers the run never had and cameras without a pose). The existing controls and kinds are unchanged. |

With neither, the line is as before. "Left empty" keeps its meaning (cells of the run's markers with no point after the step), so a kept point is not counted as empty. "Marker N was lost after frame F and not found again" is now decided from the run's own record (the points it started from, wrote, or set by hand that it reached), not from whether the cells hold a point: a marker the run lost at frame 20 is reported even when its earlier points remain to frame 93. No control changed; `docs/ui-map.md` and the form's `ui-status` table are as they were. |

| U177 | The Left and Right arrows (previous and next frame) and the keyboard focus. In XMALab 3 the shortcuts are application-wide: the arrows step frames wherever the focus is, also in a number box or a text field, in any panel, docked or floating. In this program a text field that has the keyboard keeps the plain keys, the arrows and the editing keys (Ctrl+A, X, Z, C, V), so that its text can be edited; that stays. Two things were wrong. (1) A fault of this program: the plot takes the keyboard when it is clicked (for Delete and Backspace), and was counted as a text field, so after a click in the plot no plain key did anything (arrows, Q, W, 1, 2, I, C, H, ...) until something else was clicked. Now the plot's focus leaves every shortcut to the program; its Delete and Backspace still act on the plot. (2) A floating panel is a window of its own, and the system sends the keys to the window that has the keyboard, so after a click in a floating panel (the 3D world view floats at first run) no key reached the program. Now such a window takes the Left and Right arrows by the main window's rules; the other shortcuts are still not taken there. (3) Only a click elsewhere ended the typing in a box (the toolkit's default: a button pressed and let go in place), so after a drag (a pan in a camera view, the frame slider, a rotation of the 3D view) the box still had the keyboard and the arrows moved its caret. Now a press of a mouse button anywhere outside the box ends the typing at once, in every window and dialog; what was typed is kept, as after a click. | `ui/Shortcuts.cpp:55-164` (`bindApplicationShortcuts`), `:193-383` (`eventFilter`; `:208-217` the keys left to a field), `ui/PlotWindow.cpp:937-947` | Tests `xma-app/tests/frame_arrows_focus.rs` (seven, on the pig project with the harness's own pointer and key events: the plot clicked, then Right, Left, W, Down and Delete, with the pointer on the plot and elsewhere; Shift+Right with the plot focused; a number box focused and the pointer on it, over a camera view, the plot, the Detailed View and the 3D world view floating and docked, the arrows and Q, W, 6 changing nothing, then Enter and the arrows stepping again; a click in the plot taking the keys back from a box; 150 typed in a camera's zoom box and then a pan started in the camera view with the right button: the box gives the keyboard up at the press, the zoom is 150 and the arrows step, which fails with the toolkit's default; a press and a small drag on the box itself keeping the typing; no arrow while a dialog is open), unit tests in `actions.rs` (two, the keys of a floating panel's window: only the two arrows are taken and a disabled action takes none; a focused field of that window keeps them). The first version of the change froze the program when the plot was clicked (the context was read while it was locked); the plot test showed it and it was corrected. The real window was not run; a floating panel as a real second window cannot be run in the harness, which has one window, so the few lines that take the keys there (`shell/floating.rs`) are read, not tested. Independent review 27 (27 November 2026) read the first version: no blocking defect, no key handled twice, nothing read while locked, and the ten existing test programs that press keys passed. Review 27b (the same day) read the version described here: nothing blocking; nothing of the removed pointer test left; a click gives the keyboard to nothing but a text field or the plot (the toolkit's source and this program's read for it); its remark that a drag did not end the typing led to (3), and the one about another window is in the notes. Review 27c (the same day) read (3): nothing blocking, the setting holds in every window and is reset nowhere, no value is written twice, a press on the box itself keeps the typing. It found a fault that was there before: in the Rigid body dialog's two boxes a value typed and then followed by the box's own arrow, without Enter, was lost (the arrow stepped from the stored value). With (3) a real click writes the typed value at the press and the arrow then adds to it; a press and release within one frame still lost it, corrected (`edit_forms::double_spin_box` steps from what was typed), test `bodies_editing.rs` (`an_arrow_after_typing_steps_from_what_was_typed`, both timings; the one-frame case gave 1 instead of 13.5 before the correction) | **Changed** (the author, 27 November 2026: "can we make it so that arrow keys (left and right) step through frames. They already do if the 2D view has focus, but the plot doesn't. and I'd like it to work if the mouse is hovering over the 2d views, 3d view, or plot"; and, of a first version in which the arrows stepped frames over a view even while a box had the keyboard: "it probably shouldn't do it when a text entry box has focus should it?"; and to the question whether a drag in a view should end the typing in a box as a click does: "Yes, do it") | Still different from XMALab 3: a text field that has the keyboard keeps the arrows and the plain keys, wherever the pointer is, until Enter or Escape is pressed or a mouse button is pressed anywhere else in the same window (a press in another window of the program leaves the keyboard with the box: found by review 27b, not changed); and in a floating panel's own window only the two arrows are shortcuts (U174's remark: the other keys do nothing until the main window is clicked). The first version marked whether the pointer was over a view and let the arrows through there whatever had the focus; with text fields excepted that test decided nothing (a click gives the keyboard only to a text field or to the plot), and it was removed. A control reached with Tab (a button, a slider) keeps the arrows as before. The press rule is the toolkit's own setting (`SurrenderFocusOn::Presses`, set once in `XmaApp::ui`), so it holds for the dialogs too. Most boxes write each keystroke as it is typed and are not affected; the Rigid body dialog's two boxes write when the typing ends, which is now at the press. The plot's own focus ends the same way: after a press anywhere else (a pan in a camera view, the frame slider), Delete and Backspace no longer act on the plot's range until the plot is clicked again; before, the focus lasted through a drag elsewhere and ended at a click. `widgets/view_keys.rs`, `actions::collect_shortcuts`, `collect_frame_arrows` |

| # | Item | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | Commit `5757d9c` (June 2025), "Fixed major bug in marker detection and tracking", may have changed numerical output relative to older published versions of XMALab. | Reported, not determined | The Rust program targets commit `2967a7e`. Whether that differs from XMALab 2.x results should be established before any published comparison. |
| H2 | 14 November 2026: the author decided ledger items A3, A4, F2 and F18 ("do not copy bugs, fix as we make the new program"; for F18 "keep path") and F27 ("use local time"). The Rust program fixes the four by default and keeps the C++ behaviour in the XMALab 3 compatibility mode; F27 now matches the C++. | Author's decision | Phase 1's standard, bit-for-bit identity with the C++ on the reference datasets, now holds in the compatibility mode (`xma parity`). In the default mode, 14 of the 39 datasets differ from their dumps: the nine `rv_vpchain*` and `rv_loadtime_vp_lens*` datasets (virtual points, A3, with A4 in the `_lens` ones), `v08_virtual_optimized_markerrefs` (A3), `rv_lens_sparse`, `rv_lens_sparse_noadv` and `v10_calibration_files` (A4), and `v13_mesh_colour_threshold` (F18, `rb_details.csv` only). Measured with the ignored test `crates/xma-cli/tests/fixed_mode_effect.rs`. |
| H3 | 14 November 2026: the author made "fix bugs, don't copy them" the general rule ("don't bring bugs over just to keep parity. I'd rather have improvement than the new app be identical"). Twenty more items became "Fix, compat" in code (A2, A11, A19, A20, A23 to A30, A32, A34, A35, A54, F4, F11, F13, F25), fifteen became "Fix" for when their code is ported (A1, A14, A15, A31, A33, A36, A47, A51, F1, F7, F12, F20, F26, V7) or "Fix, compat" in the video package (V2); the rest stay Copy as method choices or library internals. | Author's decision | `xma parity` stays bit-identical in the compatibility mode on all 47 datasets. Decided and to get their rows when built: a bound on the blob detector's threshold loop, limits on the sizes a project file declares, the merge-aware frame where a member leaves a pending group of three or more, the frame count of an AVI recording cut short, batching Import 2D Points and the point-edit loops. | Author's decision | Nothing in the list was built on that day. |

Rows: A210 (cap on ray combinations, with its Console line), A215 (merge-aware, a member dropped from a group of three or more), A216 (follow-up work once at the end), A217 (blob detector's threshold loop bounded), F37 (mean size after Change Trial Data), F84 (sizes a project file declares), V37 (AVI recording cut short), U37 (detection-method tooltips), U165 (View > Reset layout), U166 (hidden cameras). Three compatibility switches added (`pose_from_2d_uncapped`, `merge_drop_leaves_frame_out`, `point_edits_follow_up_per_point`; 122 in all) and one reader switch (`VideoCompat::avi_count_from_header`). Also, with no ledger row because they change no behaviour of the programs: `cargo xtask check` runs the test programs side by side (decision 19), and the long old-against-new comparison runs of the third review round are run on request (`cargo test --workspace --tests -- --ignored _long`; decision 20). Not built: decision 17 (removing `NumberingPolicy::Match`), which waits for a commit (built after the commit on the same day, A189). | The round's notes: PLAN.md, "Building the author's decisions"; tests named `review14_*` (builders), `review14b_*` and `review14d_*` (reviewers), `review14c_*` and `review14e_*` (corrections) | First reviews found faults in four of the ten changes (F37: a marker left with no counted size; F84: two routes round the limits; V37: unbounded recursion on a crafted file, and the frames of an unfinished last section; U165: the main window's size from a maximised or full-screen window) and in the route of A210's Console line (it reached standard output in `xma edit`); all corrected. The second reviews found two more, both corrected and read again: F84 reserved memory from its first, rough count of a data file's lines, and V37's OpenDML index could be made to read one part of a file many times. |

Kept: the XMALab 3 mode and its tests, frozen; project files exchangeable with XMALab 3; the familiar layout. This ledger goes on as the list of every difference from XMALab 3.
